## Inbox: window and questions

**The window** (`packages/inbox`, built by `apps/inbox` with Vite into one file).
- Build and serve: `build:hook` copies its `dist/index.html` to `apps/hook/dist/inbox.html`. The binary embeds it, and `runInboxCommand` reads it only on the path that serves (never for `inbox mcp` or another subcommand) and hands it to `startInboxServer` as `htmlContent`. Through the CLI a missing page fails the serve; a direct `startInboxServer` call with no `htmlContent` answers `/` with a note to run `build:hook`.
- One file, scripts on first use (#2593): the page keeps its stylesheet inline (fonts and pictures as data URLs), and its scripts are code-split and packed behind it (`build/pack-inbox.ts`, the plan and review apps' pack from `packages/shared/packed-app.ts`). The Inbox server answers `/` with the page and `/assets/<name>` with a script from the pack (`openPackedApp`; compressed on every listener, as the page is: see inbox-foundation.md), so a window loads its ~4.4 MB entry script up front and the guided and code review (`#guide-reader`), Shape, search, the posting dialog, Mermaid, Graphviz, KaTeX and each Shiki grammar on first use. The CSP is unchanged: `script-src 'self'` already covers them. Every script is ASCII (esbuild `charset: 'ascii'`, and the pack escapes the regular expression literals esbuild leaves as written), so V8 keeps each source at one byte per character; one non-ASCII character would make it two for the whole script, and the build fails on one. A lazy part whose chunk cannot arrive (the Inbox stopped, or restarted onto a new release under the page) sits in ui's `LazyBlockBoundary`: the guide, review and Shape panes say "Couldn't load this view. Reload" (`PanePart`); the posting dialog says the same beside Post, which stays usable; the search palette closes the window's search state; the rest of the window stays mounted (`packages/inbox/lazyChunkFailure.test.tsx`). React.lazy keeps a rejection for good, so the palette, the dialog and the Shape layer (`PanePart`'s `onFailed`) call their part's `retry()` and the next ⌘K, Post or Shape open asks for the chunk again (`packages/inbox/lazyRecovery.test.tsx`). The review reader, the Shape board, the search palette and the post dialog are `lazyParts.tsx` parts: `WarmWindowParts` fetches them once the window is idle (~0.5 MB of script), and a part already in renders in the same commit, never through Suspense (React holds a fallback at least 300 ms, so a bare `React.lazy` made a guided review's first open ~345 ms where the inline bundle took ~50 ms). Code and math: a thread read waits for its fences' grammars and, if it has math, KaTeX, before the thread draws (`threadDrawReady.ts`, called by `refreshThread`), so code never paints plain and math never paints as TeX first, as with the inline bundle; a thread with neither is not held up, and a stuck chunk holds a thread at most `DRAW_READY_CAP_MS`, once: while that chunk is still on its way, later reads draw at once and ui swaps the highlight in when it lands. A grammar whose chunk failed is asked for again on the next read (ui's `forgetHighlightRejection`), unless one HEAD for it (ui's `highlightRejection` and `failedChunkUrl`) got a 404: the tab is older than the Inbox's build, so the pair stays plain until Reload instead of fetching again on every open. A Markdown or text attachment's needs are kept for the last few files read, by name and text, as a thread's are by message. The same wait holds an attachment (`attachmentDrawReady`: a Markdown file's fences and math, a code file's language in Pierre's themes, in `AttachmentPane`) and a review's diff (`ReaderPart`: each file's language in the diff themes) (`packages/inbox/attachmentDrawReady.test.tsx`). Once the window is idle, `components/WarmCodeHighlight.tsx` attaches the JSON grammar in the palette's light and dark fence themes, which loads Pierre's highlighter and both themes: the wait is then only the thread's own grammars, and a light/dark switch does not paint the fences on screen plain. The phone's surface (`surface.html`) and the standalone Shape page (`shape.html`, `vite.shape.config.ts`) stay fully inline (vite-plugin-singlefile): the surface has no server to load from, and the Shape page's server serves the page alone. `tests/entry-assets.test.ts` guards all three.
- Styling: `packages/inbox/inbox.css` carries the design record's values on Plannotator's theme tokens with an `ib-` prefix. Plannotator's own components draw themselves.
- Shared thread parts: the thread's display parts are moving to `packages/ui/components/inbox-thread/`, so the commercial Workspaces app draws the same thread (see "Inbox thread parts" in `packages/ui/README.md`). A moved file's old path in `packages/inbox` stays as a re-export, so imports and tests there do not change; edit the file in `packages/ui`. A part takes what it shows as props and reaches no server, address, cookie or storage (`tests/inbox-thread-parts-host-neutral.test.ts`). The `ib-` rules stay in `inbox.css`; `packages/ui/scripts/build-inbox-thread-css.ts` generates `@plannotator/ui/inbox-thread.css` from it at `prepack`, scoped to `.pn-inbox`, so a rule added to `inbox.css` also ships to hosts (`tests/inbox-thread-css.test.ts` fails if it could style a host page, and a new at-rule fails the generator). To prove a move, run `bun run test:parity:inbox-thread`, then compare the plan review, code review and phone surface builds as well, because that harness only runs the Inbox and a file in `packages/ui/components` is scanned by every app's Tailwind. Compare packed-asset HTML by content: the order of the fonts in its table can change between builds.
- Server URLs: every `/api/inbox/...` URL the window builds (fetch, EventSource, attachment `src`) goes through `inboxUrl` in `packages/inbox/inboxUrl.ts`; `tests/inbox-url-helper.test.ts` fails on a raw one.
- Routing is the hash: `#thread=msg_…` (the link the MCP tools hand agents), `#project=prj_…`, `#settings` (General; on a phone the list of sections), `#settings/<section>` (`general`, `agents`, `prompts`, `devices`, `capabilities`, `activity`, `archived`, `storage`, `about`; an unknown one reads as `#settings`; `#settings/decision-data`, an old link, becomes `#settings/activity` in place; `#settings/experimental`, an old link, becomes `#settings/capabilities` in place and lands on its Experimental group), `#settings/devices/pair` (Pair a phone open), `&file=att_…` (with `&view=full|side`, the file's view; see "Side by side, remembered"), `&guide=msg_…` (from a Shape board, with the place to open at: `&code=path:line`, `&step=N` or `&files`, dropped once there), `&msg=msg_…` (land on that message: a search result; dropped from the hash once landed) (`packages/inbox/settingsRoute.ts` for the Settings addresses). An old link to the removed Decisions page (`#decisions=…`) opens the list.
- Opening a thread (`#thread=`): the pane shows once `GET /api/inbox/threads/:id` lands. A read that fails (a dropped connection, a 5xx) or has no answer in `THREAD_READ_TIMEOUT_MS` (8 s, an AbortSignal) is read again after `THREAD_READ_RETRY_MS` (250 ms, 1 s, 2 s, 4 s, then every 5 s while the thread stays open; a 404 is not; opening another thread or closing stops it; a read the event stream lands meanwhile ends the wait; `packages/inbox/App.tsx`). Reads are numbered, so an older one never overwrites a newer one that landed. Until it lands the thread's place beside the list says so (`components/ThreadWait.tsx`: nothing for the first 600 ms from the open in any state, then "Opening the thread…" (`aria-busy`), the failure with Try again (said once, not per retry), or for a 404 "not in the Inbox" with Close; focus inside it goes to the thread's subject when the thread lands; a phone and a stacked window keep the list, as before). Before this one failed read left the right side blank until some agent wrote anywhere in the Inbox (the event stream re-reads the open thread). Proof: `packages/inbox/threadReadRetry.test.tsx`.
- **Only the thread the person lands on is read, and going back is instant** (slow-link fixes, 2026-11-10; nothing on screen changes but speed). Each read of the open thread carries an AbortSignal (`openThreadReadsOut` in `App.tsx`); opening another thread or closing gives up the reads of the one left, so clicking through five threads on a slow link reads the last, not all five, and an answer for a thread left is never drawn (it was not before either: reads are numbered). The last `THREAD_CACHE_SIZE` (8) threads opened are kept as their last read (`packages/inbox/threadCache.ts`): opening one draws it at once and its read goes out as usual and replaces it (objects shared, `shareUnchanged`); the look, what the thread opened with and the "new" line still come from that fresh read. A copy is never shown stale: any record of that thread drops it (before any other handling of the record, own echoes too), a record the window cannot place drops every copy, as does the stream going quiet, reconnecting or catching up; the window's own pick saves drop the open thread's copy; a read begun before a drop is not kept (`begin`/`put` tokens); copies older than 5 min are not shown; a list read drops threads it no longer has; and only this Inbox's own listed, non-launched threads are kept. Proof: `packages/inbox/threadCache.test.ts`, e2e `tests/e2e/inbox-network-held.spec.ts` (five slow reads: at most two finish; going back draws before its read answers).
- **Send and row actions draw at once** (same day). The Send's last reply asks for the thread with its answer (`with_thread` on `POST /messages/:id/reply`, answering `thread_read`, exactly `GET /threads/:id`'s answer; Bun only, the Inbox has no Pi twin): the sent reply draws from it (`onSentRead` → `refreshThread(id, given)`) and only the list is read after. A linked machine's thread, or an older Inbox that sends no `thread_read`, reads as before. The reply itself still waits for its POST: the server composes its body (picks, earlier answers, actions, feedback, tags), and a copy drawn before would not be its real shape. Mark read and Archive draw their row as pressed (`packages/inbox/rowActions.ts`: read and not bold, or archived in place) and Star draws the star, on the list's row and the thread's header, before the write answers; the server's row replaces it when the list read after the write lands; a write that fails puts the row back and shows the action's own error (`showError`). Mark read's line under the header ("Read … N questions were closed unanswered") waits for the write and its reads (`readPending`), so it never shows half; a Move back pressed meanwhile goes after the Mark read (`readWrites`). Mark unread, Move back and Snooze still wait for their answer (what they do depends on the server).
- **Which records read the open thread again** (`packages/inbox/openThreadReads.ts`; perf study item 4a', meant to change nothing on screen). After records, the window still reads the list and the decisions (and the drafts when the records can change them: "Only what changed" below), but reads the open thread only when the records can change it: a message, question or annotation of that thread (a question record carries its message's `thread_id`, additive, from `eventPayload`), any project record, or a record it cannot place (a malformed frame, a kind it does not know, the read a pick save owes). It reads the thread on every record, as before, while the thread is on another machine, has no read that stands (not read yet, failed, waiting, refused), has no row in the list (an archived thread's header reads the thread read's own row, which live sessions, snoozes and drafts change with no record), or its project's repo is not known yet; and once its last read landed more than `INBOX_REPO_TTL_MS` (5 min, `packages/core/inbox-types.ts`) ago, the next record reads it, since the server refreshes a repo only when a read asks for it after that long (so a changed git `origin` reaches its links). A read skipped is replaced by what it did besides: the thread's files are read again (`filesSignal` on `ThreadPane`, below), and the thread is read after all when the list read that follows no longer has it (deleted elsewhere: no record says so). Proof: `packages/inbox/openThreadReads.test.ts`; `packages/inbox/openThreadReadsWiring.test.tsx` (DOM: the files read on a skip, and the read of a thread the list no longer has); and `packages/server/inbox-open-thread-reads.test.ts`, which runs every kind of write on a real Inbox and fails if one changes the thread's read while its records do not read it.
- **A change costs what it changed** (the owner's debug log, 2026-11-09; meant to change nothing on screen). Each key typed in a question's "Other…" field (or a free-text answer, or a note) saves, each save leaves a record, and each record read the list (twice), the decisions, the open thread, its files and the drafts, and drew the whole window and the open thread with every message again for each read: 7.5 renders per key on the 245-message thread, so typing fell seconds behind. Now:
  - The echo of the window's own save (`packages/inbox/ownSaves.ts`): a `question` record no newer than the answer a save of this window brought back (an older revision, or that very answer word for word; a Send marks a question sent without raising its revision, so a changed copy at the same revision is news) reads what shows the pick besides the pane, the list, the decisions and the drafts, never the open thread or its files (`openThreadRead` answers `none`; as before for a thread with no row in the list or on another machine). A run of saves still waits for the last one (`inbox-messages.md`), then reads that once, at once, with one list read, whether or not its records came yet; an echo that comes after that read started owes nothing at all (`covered`). Anything else (another window's or a phone's newer answer, the agent's) is read as before.
  - A read that brings nothing new draws nothing. The list (`packages/inbox/listSame.ts`): a read whose rows, sections and every other field the window draws are the same keeps the list held (rows compare by identity, which `listRead.ts` keeps, else by JSON; `list_version` never counts; the store's `cursor` counts only on Settings, whose Archived count reads again when it moves). The window still takes the newest cursor for its "older answer" check. `refreshHeldRows` (`held.ts`) keeps the list drawn when no row on screen changed, and each untouched section. The drafts, the decisions, the open thread's row and its run line keep what is drawn when a read brings the same (`keepSame`). The thread read already kept itself (`shareUnchanged`).
  - The open thread's files and reply draft are read again through signals (`packages/inbox/signal.ts`, `filesSignal` and `draftSignal` on `ThreadPane`), not counters in the window's state: a record that needs only the files, or `event: drafts`, used to draw the whole window first. The pane reads, and draws only what the read changed. `filesChanged` and `draftChanged` stay on `ThreadPane` for other hosts.
  - Measured (a sandboxed Inbox: 423 threads, the 245-message thread open, a question's "Other…" field typed in while an agent writes every 2.5 s): see the PR "Inbox: a change refreshes only what it touched". What is left per key is the pane drawn for the pick on screen and once more for the save's answer (the open-questions list, the foot and the Send read the pick); a memo per message would be the next step.
  - Proof: `packages/inbox/ownSaves.test.ts`, `listSame.test.ts`, the own-echo cases in `openThreadReads.test.ts`, `draftsReads.test.ts` and `held.test.ts`, and `packages/inbox/pickSaveReads.test.tsx` (DOM: a run of saves reads the list and the drafts once and never the thread or its files; an echo after the save's answer reads neither; a list read with nothing new commits nothing).
- **No `:has()` anchored on the page in the window** (meant to change nothing on screen). Chromium checks a `:has()` whose subject is `html`, `body`, `:root` or `.pn-inbox` again on every DOM change under it, and every part of a thread is under all four: on a long thread (360 highlighted fences, about 440,000 elements) the 23 such rules in the window's sheets cost about 42 ms of style work per DOM change (a fence highlighted, a message arriving, a menu opening); now 0.3 to 0.4 ms. The sheets keep the rules as written (other apps and `@plannotator/ui/inbox-thread.css` load them). The window's Vite build (`build/inbox-page-marks.ts`, in `apps/inbox/vite.config.ts`'s window build only) rewrites each to an attribute with the same specificity (`PAGE_MARKS`) and drops the theme.css rules on the plan and code review apps' attributes (`NEVER_IN_THE_INBOX`). The parts hold the attributes while they are in the page, in the same commit: `packages/inbox/pageMarks.ts` (`.pn-inbox` → `<html>`/`<body data-pn-inbox>`; the decision pill, the image composer, full screen's composer and the recipient list → their `.pn-inbox`; the Shape dock and pill → `<body>`), and ui's `pageMarks` option (PopoutDialog, the selection chooser). A new `html:has(…)`, `body:has(…)` or `.pn-inbox:has(…)` in a window sheet needs a `PAGE_MARKS` entry and a holder: `tests/inbox-page-marks.test.ts` fails on one left, and the build fails on an entry no sheet uses. A `:has()` on a smaller element (`.ib-message:has(…)`) is cheap and needs nothing.
- **A long thread's heavy work, off the critical path** (meant to change nothing on screen; numbers in the PR "Inbox: move heavy work off the main thread"). Opening a thread with a few hundred code fences highlighted every fence at once (a 4.4 s task on the seeded 120-message thread; ~400K spans):
  - Fences: the Inbox turns on ui's `deferOffscreenCodeHighlight` (`App.tsx`; see "Syntax Highlighting" in `guides-and-shared-ui.md`): a fence is highlighted before it first shows, the rest in idle time. Their tokens are made in a Web Worker (`codeHighlight.worker.ts` with `fenceMarkup.ts`, started by `codeHighlightWorker.ts` on the first fence that waits, built inline with Vite's `?worker&inline`, so it is a `blob:` worker the CSP's `worker-src 'self' blob:` allows in every build, packed or single file). The worker has no grammars of its own: each request carries the grammars the page's Pierre highlighter has attached that it has not been sent yet (all of them, so a grammar that embeds another has it) and the theme, as Pierre resolved them, the way Pierre's own diff worker gets them. It runs Shiki's core on its JavaScript engine, `shiki` pinned in `packages/inbox/package.json` to the version `@pierre/diffs` resolves, and the same markup builder. `codeHighlightWorker.test.ts` runs the real worker against the real page highlighter and wants the same markup byte for byte (and fails when the two Shiki versions part). In JavaScriptCore (Safari, Bun) Shiki's JavaScript engine colours a grammar's first fence in a realm unlike every later one, so the worker makes its first fence in each grammar twice and keeps the second. Anything that fails (no Worker, a refused worker, a grammar that does not clone) answers null and the page highlights that fence itself.
  - The new-messages pill keeps its count in a small store (`NewPill` in `Thread.tsx`, `useSyncExternalStore`): a scroll that changes the count draws the pill, not the thread, and the thread draws only when the pill shows or hides. Messages seen on sight are marked in a transition (`startTransition`), so the scroll frame is not the one that draws them. Before, every scroll frame on a long thread drew the whole thread (11 s of long tasks over a 6 s scroll of the 245-message thread).
  - The reply corner's MutationObserver (`useCornerRoom` in `floaters/useReplyFloaters.tsx`) skips changes inside a `<pre>`: a fence's highlight moves nothing it measures, and its measure walks the whole page (7 ms a call on a long code thread).
- **Only what changed, read once per burst** (#2593; meant to change nothing on screen). Every agent message used to make the window read the whole list again (about 0.35 MB with 300 open threads), plus the drafts and Tidy, and the browser kept those answers: the tab grew by about 200 MB under traffic. Now:
- **A thread left open holds no copy of earlier reads** (#2948; changes nothing on screen). Every record that re-reads the open thread brought a whole new copy of its messages and of its files' records, and copies of earlier reads stayed alive: a closure React keeps (an effect, a memoized callback, a listener) holds the scope of the render that made it, and through the thread view's per-render callbacks that render's whole thread. On a 245-message thread, 30 sends left 17 copies, and a window left open on a busy thread grew without end (240 sends, 80 of them into the open thread: +46 MB). Now a re-read keeps what did not change as the same objects (`shareUnchanged`, `packages/inbox/shareUnchanged.ts`, in App's `refreshThread` and the thread's attachments read), and `MessageBody` reads the host's callbacks (`onPick`, `comments`, `footerFor`) through refs, so its kept closures never reach the thread view's render: the same run grows +8.6 MB, what its 80 new messages hold. Rule for new code in a per-message component: a closure that React keeps never names a callback prop directly; read it through a ref.
- **A long thread draws its far messages' tooltips and menus plain** (#2948, `packages/inbox/messageArming.tsx`; changes nothing a person sees). Each open message carried about five tooltips and two menus (its ⋯ and the head line's right-click menu), plus an Open in… menu per file: Base UI roots of 50 to 200 hooks each, mounted whether or not anyone pointed at them (245 open messages: about 100 of the window's 160 MB of JS heap). A message is armed (its real tooltips and menus mounted) while it is within one view's height of the view (`ARM_MARGIN`), and when the pointer or focus reaches it; it is disarmed past three (`DISARM_MARGIN`), never while focus or an open menu is inside it. Disarmed, every control is still drawn, the same element with the same text and label (ui's opt-in `TooltipArmed` context; `CopyMenus.tsx` and `OpenIn.tsx` draw their ⋯ as a plain button that opens the real menu on a press). Focus that lands in a disarmed message stays on the same control once it is armed. Heavy thread: JS heap 161 → 57 MB, listeners 17.7K → 11.3K. `messageArming.test.tsx` guards that nothing visible changes.
  - The list (`packages/inbox/listRead.ts` behind `inboxApi.list()`): each read names the list it holds (`?since=<list_version>`, see `/api/inbox/threads` in `server-and-storage.md`) and gets only the changed rows; core's `applyInboxListDelta` puts them with that list into the model a full read gives. The first read, one after a restart and one the server cannot answer as a change are whole. Calls in the same tick share one read; a call while a read is out starts its own, as before (a slow answer never holds a newer one back), and each answer is put with the list it asked against.
  - Rows-only events (pins, deliveries, a draft's list read) share one trailing timer (`listAfterEvents` in `App.tsx`), dropped when a record's read is due anyway, since that read starts after them.
  - The drafts (`packages/inbox/draftsReads.ts`): after records, read only when one is of a thread holding unsent work before or after them (the reply drafts and the list read say), or cannot be placed, or when the list read's rows holding unsent work (this machine's only, not a linked machine's) are not the reply drafts shown (`replyDraftsDiffer`: a thread or project deleted, which no record says); on the Drafts page after every record, as before. A reply draft saved (`event: drafts`) reads them as before, and so does a compose draft saved, deleted or sent in any window (`event: drafts` with `compose: true`; drafts only, no list read), which no record says. So the sidebar's Drafts count is never left behind what a read after every record showed.
  - Tidy (`useTidy` in `components/Tidy.tsx`): a list or Board read marks the plan stale; it is read while the ⋮ menu or the popover is open, when the pointer reaches ⋮ or it takes focus, and when they open. Until that read lands (`pending`: a touch opens ⋮ at once, with no hover first) the menu draws the Tidy item as while the plan is first read (enabled, "Tidy" with no count or run progress), never the old count; no new words. The first read (a list read within its 250 ms puts it off, never drops it), a scope change and a run's polling are as before.
  - Proof: `packages/server/inbox-list-delta.test.ts` (a real Inbox; after every step of a mix of events, and several steps behind, the list put together equals a full read; a linked machine's rows; a read without `since` answered as before; restarts, forged versions, big changes), `packages/inbox/listRead.test.ts`, and `packages/inbox/listDeltaWindow.test.tsx` (DOM: no drafts or Tidy read after an unrelated message, both read when they can change, the list drawn equal to a fresh window's).
- A link to this Inbox opens in the same window, not a new tab: a capture-phase click listener in `App.tsx` beside the hash listeners, writing the route as a row does, so Back and a phone's stack treat it as one (`tests/e2e/inbox-links-in-place.spec.ts`). `inboxLinkHash` (`packages/inbox/links.ts`) decides: `#thread=` (with `&msg=` or `&file=`), `#project=` or `#settings` on the window's origin, the same port on a loopback spelling, or an advertised address (`settings.url`, the tailnet's); a bare `#thread=` or `#project=` too, but never a bare `#settings`, which in a document is a heading. The thread already open replaces its entry and lands on the message. Anything else stays the browser's: another host or port (a linked machine), the web, Cmd, Ctrl, Shift, Alt or middle click, `download`. A link in a dialog, or while a file comment is not saved yet (the pane's own rule, `openPaneUnsaved`: saved words compared, so an edit not changed does not count), opens a new tab, so nothing changes out of sight; a link inside an attached HTML page goes through the pane's `leave`, which keeps the comment as the file's draft. Known gap: an attached HTML page's own script can still move the window to another thread in this Inbox with no click. `followLink` opens in place only when the page's frame has focus and `navigator.userActivation.isActive` is true, but in Chromium and WebKit those checks do not stop a page script. The e2e case is `test.fixme`. A real fix (for example, the bridge sends only trusted clicks over a private channel) is a follow-up. One exception stays a new tab: a link inside an inline HTML block in a message (ui's `HtmlEmbedBlock` opens it, with no seam for the Inbox).
- Issue refs (#1840): bare `#123` / `@user` in messages and markdown attachments link to the project's git `origin` (GitHub or GitLab, per `forgeRefLinks`, new tab). The server resolves `git remote get-url origin` per project root off the request path (`packages/server/inbox-repos.ts`: for every project once the server is listening and when a project is first seen, at most 4 gits at once, refreshed in the background after 5 min, 3 s git timeout, parsed by `parseRemoteUrl`/`parseRemoteHost`) and `GET /api/inbox/threads/:id` carries it as `thread.project.repo: { display, host } | null` (null for a folder without git or without origin, or until the first resolve lands; a git failure or timeout is cached as null for those 5 min too). The window hands it to `Viewer` as `refRepo`, which links refs without drawing `repoInfo`'s repo badge. A digit-only 6 or 8 digit ref (`#186379`) is an issue ref, not a hex swatch, only in the Inbox (`configurePlannotatorUI({ digitHexIssueRefs: true })`, off for every other host), only when a ref link can be built (a repo on a known forge), and only without a leading zero (#1932). `owner/repo#123` links to that repo on the project's forge host, and, when the project has no repo, on the host most of the person's projects use (`packages/inbox/defaultRefHost.ts`). That host comes from the projects' `repo_host`, which every list read already carries, so it costs no request and no git. Each project with a repo counts once. A remote that names no host counts as github.com. A tie that includes github.com, no project with a repo, or a winner `forgeRefLinks` cannot form a link for gives github.com. ui hears it through `crossRepoRefsWithoutRepo: { host }`, and the Links view lists the ref the same way (`matchCrossRepoRef`, see "Hover cards for #refs" in `guides-and-shared-ui.md`), and the agents are told so in both `body` descriptions and `--help` (`INBOX_REF_LINK_RULE`); the opened Board card renders its You band, notes and history through the same `InlineMarkdown` with its own project's repo (`GET /api/inbox/cards/:id` `repo`).
- Hover cards for issue, PR and MR links (owner's call, 2026-10-26: GitHub and GitLab now; the latest comment only; 400 ms; no new key). A link in a message body to an issue, pull request or merge request (a `#123` linked as above, or a pasted `https://github.com/o/r/pull/N`, `…/issues/N`, `https://<gitlab host>/<namespace>/<project>/-/merge_requests/N`, `/-/issues/N`, `/-/work_items/N`) shows a card (its host taken exactly as written: `parseForgeRefUrl` does not fold the SSH aliases `normalizeForgeHost` folds for git remotes, and a host that only starts like `github.com` or `gitlab.com`, such as `github.com-evil.example`, gives no card) after the pointer or keyboard focus rests on it for `REF_CARD_DELAY_MS` (400 ms). Before that nothing is asked: opening a thread runs no CLI and makes no request (`tests/e2e/inbox-ref-cards.spec.ts`).
  - The card (`RefCardView` in `packages/ui/components/RefHoverCard.tsx`, the same in light, dark and on a phone): repo and "opened N ago"; the state icon, title and number (`!7` for a GitLab MR, `forgeRefLabel`); a state pill (Open, Draft, Merged, Closed, Not planned; merged and done in the palette's `--primary`); author, "updated" or "merged/closed N ago"; for a PR or MR `base ← head`, `+adds −dels · N files`, the checks (GitHub: the head commit's check runs and statuses; GitLab: the head pipeline's jobs, "Pipeline running · 2 of 4 jobs left") and the review decision (Approved, Changes requested, Review required; GitLab from `approved` and `detailedMergeStatus`, quiet once the MR is merged or closed); labels and assignee avatars. Then a scroll area: the body (markdown cut at 4,000 characters, "The rest is on GitHub"), "Closes" / "Closed by" (GitHub only), and the latest comment by a person (GitLab system notes skipped), cut at 600; it shows up to 216 px (about eight lines of a description) before it scrolls, 340 px once pinned (the sheet gives it the height). A footer: the comment count and **Open on GitHub / GitLab ↗**, always in view: when the window leaves too little room for the description (a short window), the header and the scroll area scroll together as one (`tight`, `data-ref-card-tight`) instead of squeezing the description to a line or cutting the footer off. Untrusted text renders through `RenderedMarkdown` (DOMPurify on HTML), images are never loaded (their alt text, an empty title), and a link in the body has no `href` once drawn (kept as `data-ref-card-href`, `role="link"`, focusable), raw-HTML and `<details>` links included: a MutationObserver strips every `a` and `area` `href` (and `xlink:href`) after any change to the body, because `HtmlBlock` writes raw HTML in a passive effect after the card's own pass, and native capture-phase `click`, `auxclick`, middle `mousedown` and Enter listeners on the body catch any link, `href` or not, before the renderer's listeners; a local path in the body is inert (`resolveLocalLink` answers unavailable). So a middle-click, a modified click or the context menu opens nothing; a click or Enter only offers "Open · Cancel" with its URL (the host whole and in the text colour, however long a lookalike makes it, the path cut in the middle when long, the whole URL for a screen reader; `confirmUrlParts`), whose Open is the one real link. Failures are a calm card that still offers Open on GitHub: the CLI not installed (`cli_missing`), signed out, not found or no access (`not_found`), rate limited, unavailable; a host this computer does not fetch from (`unsupported`) shows the same quiet card ("Details show for hosts your gh is signed in to…") with nothing fetched.
  - Behaviour (`useRefHoverCards(rootRef)`, event delegation on the region, so `InlineMarkdown` is unchanged): opens on hover or `:focus-visible` focus after the delay; Tab from the focused link moves into the card, Shift+Tab from its first control back; a press inside pins it (`data-ref-card-pinned`: it stays when the pointer leaves, scrolls, shows Close); Escape (used, so the thread's own Escape skips it), Close or a press outside close it, focus back on the link when it was in the card; a page scroll closes an unpinned card and moves a pinned one; one card on the page at a time. On a touch screen (`(hover: none)`, or a touch pointer) a tap shows it as a bottom sheet instead of following the link (44 px targets, its scrim closes on click so the tap never lands underneath). The Inbox's `MessageBody` calls it on each body (`packages/inbox/components/Thread.tsx`).
  - The seam: `configurePlannotatorUI({ refCardTransport })` / `setRefCardTransport` (`packages/ui/utils/refCard.ts`; the client keeps an answer 30 s per ref and shares one request between hovers). Default none: no card and no request, so every `@plannotator/ui` host that installs none is unchanged. Plan review, annotate and code review install one over their own `GET /api/ref` (see "Hover cards for #refs" in `guides-and-shared-ui.md`). The Inbox installs one over `GET /api/inbox/ref?url=` (`packages/inbox/App.tsx`). The link parser and the card's data types are browser-safe in `@plannotator/core/forge-ref-card` (`parseForgeRefUrl`, `forgeRefKey`, `ForgeRefCardData`, `ForgeRefCardResult`).
  - The fetch (`packages/server/ref-cards.ts`, `createRefCards`, reusable by any server): one read-only GraphQL `query` per ref through this computer's own CLI (the module refuses to load a non-query): GitHub `gh api graphql` (`issueOrPullRequest`, both kinds in one call); GitLab `glab api graphql` (`project.issue` or `project.mergeRequest` by the link's kind, since GitLab numbers them apart). Hosts: github.com and gitlab.com, plus the hosts this computer's own gh or glab is signed in to (`readSignedInHosts`: `gh auth status --json hosts`, a host counting only when an account there says `state: "success"`, else its text form, and `glab auth status`, read once per 5 min, `REF_AUTH_HOSTS_MS`; an answer with no host, as from a failed or timed-out `auth status`, only for 30 s, `REF_FAILURE_MS`), which covers GitHub Enterprise and self-managed GitLab; `--hostname <host>` is passed on every call, github.com and gitlab.com too, so `GH_HOST`, `GITLAB_HOST` or the working folder's git remote never pick the host; any other host answers `unsupported` without running anything, so a link or a cloned repo's origin naming `github.evil.example` can never make the CLI send `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` or `GITLAB_TOKEN` there. Owner and repo go as strings (`-f`; `-F` would turn a repo named `2048`, `true` or `null` into another type). GitHub accounts follow #2157's rule: the active account first; when GitHub refuses it (404/401/403/SAML), each other account signed in on that host is tried through its own token in `GH_TOKEN` (`GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` on GHES) for that child only, and the account that could see a repo is remembered for it (memory only, never logged). Cache per ref: fresh 60 s (`REF_FRESH_MS`), a failure 30 s (`REF_FAILURE_MS`); a stale answer is served at once while one refresh runs, up to 15 min, and a failed refresh keeps it and holds off the next for 30 s; hovers of one ref share one request; at most 2 CLIs at once; a rate-limit answer pauses that host for 60 s (`REF_RATE_LIMIT_MS`); each CLI is killed after 8 s. Memory is bounded for the life of the Inbox: at most 500 refs (`REF_CACHE_MAX`) and 200 remembered repo accounts (`REF_REPO_ACCOUNTS_MAX`), least recently used dropped first; answers past 15 min, failures past 30 s and finished holds are dropped at the next fetch; at most 500 lookups wait for a CLI (`REF_MAX_WAITING`, above a Board request's 200 links), and one more answers `unavailable` at once without being cached. Avatars are fetched once from GitHub's avatar host, the GitLab host itself or Gravatar (64 KB, png/jpeg/gif/webp) and inlined as data URIs, since the window's CSP takes no remote image (at most 500 kept, `AVATAR_CACHE_MAX`, and 8 MB of data URIs in all, `AVATAR_CACHE_MAX_BYTES`).
  - Exposure: the window's read guard (another site's request is `403 cross_origin`), the owner's page over the tailnet like every read route, and never the device door (not in its allowlist), so phones and the relay never get a card or make this computer run a CLI. Tests: `packages/server/ref-cards.test.ts` (parsers for both forges, one request per ref, the cache and its bounds, the account fallback, rate limits, the errors, the route's guards and the door), `packages/ui/components/RefHoverCard.dom.test.tsx` (raw-HTML and `<details>` body links: click, middle click, Cmd or Ctrl-click, Enter), `tests/e2e/inbox-ref-cards.spec.ts` (compiled binary with a scripted `gh` and `glab` on PATH, `tests/e2e/fake-forge.ts`; `PLANNOTATOR_REF_CARD_SHOTS` keeps screenshots).
- The sidebar:
  - The Tater mark (24 frames over 3.5 s, paused offscreen, the first frame under reduced motion). The sprite strip slides by `transform` inside a one-frame window (`.ib-tater` / `.ib-tater-strip` in `inbox.css`), so the compositor runs the loop and the page restyles about 8 times a second instead of 60; a `background-position` loop restyled the page 60 times a second while idle (#2593).
  - Inbox, and each project folder with the count of rows waiting on the person. A project with no thread in the window's list leaves the sidebar (see "The project menu").
  - Settings.
  - The Update button above Settings (below Copy prompt) when health reports `update` (`components/Updates.tsx`; Workspaces' rules: one full-width filled primary button, 32 px, no motion, never a percentage, never the error colour; tooltip "Plannotator X is available" / "is ready"; Available and Ready read "Update to X.Y.Z", one line, ending in an ellipsis rather than wrapping at the 200 px sidebar (`.ib-upd-l`), so it is never one of two bare "Update"s (approval `inbox-whats-new-header`); the accessible name is the visible text ("Update to X.Y.Z", WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name), and Failed's is "Update failed: Plannotator X didn't install"). Available opens the update dialog: "Update to Plannotator X", the notes' first paragraph, What's new, what happens (the install script with the remembered flags, the open sessions from `sessions/`, the Inbox restarting), the Claude Code plugin command only when `--skip-claude-plugin` was remembered (the install script updates the plugin otherwise), the "Install new versions automatically" checkbox (writes `autoUpdate` through `POST /api/inbox/settings` `{ auto_update }`), and Remind me tomorrow / Cancel / **Update now**; a binary the script does not manage gets the command instead of Update now. Updating and Restarting show the same button disabled with a small spinner. Ready restarts at once (`POST /api/inbox/restart`: the server stops, the binary on disk starts detached, and the page reloads on the new `serverSession`), first asking when the open thread's reply box holds unsent text (`unsent.ts`). Failed is a neutral outline button that opens the failure dialog (exit code, `update.log`, the install command, Try Again). What's new is a page at `#whats-new=X` (`GET /api/inbox/whats-new?version=X`: the stored notes through the read-only `Viewer`, and the one-line highlights of the releases skipped, "Also in the releases since <since>", folded past three). Its header is the approved Toolbar design (`.product/approved/inbox-whats-new-header/`, not in this repo): a sticky bar (`.ib-wn-bar`, over the notes' own stacking context) with the SidebarTrigger, `‹ Inbox` (`data-whats-new-back`: the browser's Back when the page was opened from that Inbox screen, `backTo` in `history.ts`, else a new entry to the last Inbox screen that was neither Settings nor What's new) and `What's new` on the left; on the right the GitHub release link (new tab) and, only while that release can be installed (`whatsNewCanUpdate`: the update's version is the page's and its state is available or ready), "Update to X.Y.Z" (primary; does what the sidebar's Update does; "to X.Y.Z" drops under 380 px, the name keeps it). Under it, aligned to the notes: "What's new in Plannotator", the version large, "Released <long date>", and one chip (`whatsNewStanding`): "You're on this version"; "Development build" for a running version that is not X.Y.Z (never "You have dev"); behind, a button "You have X · N releases in between" that opens and scrolls to the skipped releases and focuses them (no motion under reduced motion), or "You have X" alone when the notes name none in between; past the release, "You have X". On a phone the bar is one row of 44 px targets (no title, the GitHub mark alone). Proofs: `packages/inbox/whatsNewHeader.test.tsx`, the Back case in `whatsNewLink.test.tsx`, and the restart and downgrade cases in `packages/server/inbox-updates.test.ts`. **Images in the notes** (`packages/server/inbox-whats-new-images.ts`, Inbox only; the shared `update-check.ts`, vendored to Pi, fetches no image): the window never loads a remote image and its CSP stays `img-src 'self' data: blob:`. Instead the Inbox copies the images a release's saved "What's New" markdown shows (markdown images and `<img src>`, outside code, `inboxMessageRemoteImageRefsInOrder`) once, beside the notes, and the page loads the copies from `GET /api/inbox/whats-new/image`. Only `https:` on the default port, no credentials, no empty host label (`.githubusercontent.com` fails), and only the hosts release attachments use (`allowedReleaseImageUrl`): `github.com/user-attachments/assets/*`, `private-user-images.githubusercontent.com` (where user-attachments redirects), `user-images.githubusercontent.com`, and `raw.githubusercontent.com/backnotprop/plannotator/*`; no other `*.githubusercontent.com` host (gist., objects., camo., …). Redirects are followed by hand (`redirect: "manual"`), at most 5, each to an allowed host. Each image: at most 10 MB (declared or counted as it streams) and 50 megapixels, type sniffed from the bytes (`sniffImageContentType`), one 15 s deadline across redirects and body; at most 12 per release (the first 12 the notes show). The release check's rules: nothing is asked while `PLANNOTATOR_UPDATE_CHECK` is off, the `plannotator-update-check` user agent, and a claim per version in `update-state.json` (`releaseImagesFetch`, the `notesFetch` shape: claimed before the request and marked `inFlight` until it answers, again 6 h after a failure, 24 h after an answer, and 15 minutes after a claim its process abandoned mid-fetch; nothing at all once every image is stored). A failed image is left out and logged to `update.log`; it never fails the notes. Stored as `update-notes/<version>/<sha256>.<ext>` with the src → file map in `update-notes/<version>/images.json` (its own file, so the release check rewriting `<version>.json` never drops it); `uninstall --purge` removes `update-notes/` whole. When: in the background, whether or not What's new is ever opened: off the health tick for the newest known release and the "just updated" version once their notes are saved (never awaited), so besides the hourly `api.github.com` request the Inbox downloads up to 12 images per release from those image hosts, only while update checks are on; and from the What's new route, within its same 3 s wait, only for a version the Inbox may fetch notes for (`fetchableVersion`). The route answers `images` (the srcs stored), and the page hands ui's `ImageResolverContext` a resolver (`resolveImage` in `WhatsNewPage`): a stored src loads from `whatsNewImageUrl`, any other image (not saved, another host, an older Inbox without `images`) is drawn as its alt text in ui's quiet dashed chip, never a broken image and never a request. PLANNOTATOR_UPDATE_API_URL pointed at a loopback `http:` origin also lets images from that same origin through (a test's fake GitHub); a non-loopback mirror widens nothing. Proofs: `packages/server/inbox-whats-new-images.test.ts` (the allowlist, redirects, caps, the switch and claim, the route), the DOM test `packages/inbox/whatsNewImages.test.tsx`, and the tailnet owner and guest cases in `packages/server/inbox-tailscale.test.ts`. Settings → About: the version and when it last checked, Check now (`POST /api/inbox/update/check`, an explicit request even with checks off), "Tell me when a new version is out" (`update_check`) and "Keep Plannotator up to date" (`auto_update`, needs checks on). Settings → General opens with the same version row (`GeneralUpdateRow`, the owner, 2026-10-26: "the update button to also show up in the general part of settings"): "Plannotator X" and the sidebar's action (Update to X.Y.Z for available and ready, "Update failed…" opening the failure dialog, a disabled spinner while updating or restarting), Check now, "Up to date · checked <time>" when there is nothing to do, and the failing-check line with the install command (see "No one stuck on a version" in `inbox-foundation.md`). Like the sidebar button it is never drawn on a tailnet guest's page (`pageOffersUpdates` in `updateNotice.ts`). The owner over the tailnet gets all of this as on the computer, What's new and its images included (`pageOffersWhatsNew`), and Settings → About there also links to What's new in the running release (`whatsNewRunning` on `AboutBlock`; not for a development build). A guest's page offers What's new as every page does (public release notes; owner ruling 2026-11-11), and its About is the version row with What's new in it (`AboutBlock` with `updates={false}`); an Inbox too old to say `guest` counts as a guest. Proof: the DOM test `packages/inbox/whatsNewTailnet.test.tsx`. A restart asked from there that does not come back leaves the window saying so, in a long toast ("The Inbox hasn’t come back yet. If it stays away, start it on the computer."), since nobody is at the computer to see why.
  - While the sidebar is closed (docked closed, or the phone sheet closed) and the Update button would offer something (available, ready, failed; not while updating, restarting, snoozed or with no `update`, as on a tailnet guest's page), the sidebar toggle at the head of each page carries a 7 px dot at its top-right corner (the owner, 2026-10-24): primary for available and ready, `--muted-foreground` for failed (the button's rule, never the error colour), no motion, `aria-hidden`; the toggle's name becomes "Toggle Sidebar, update available" ("update ready", "update failed"). `sidebarUpdateNotice` (`packages/inbox/updateNotice.ts`) decides it, `SidebarShell` / `SidebarProvider`'s `toggleNotice` carries it, `SidebarTrigger` draws it. Its ring is the pane's ground (`--ib-pane-bg-s`, else `--ib-main-bg`), the toggle's fill when hovered, and `CanvasText` on `Canvas` under forced colors. For a tailnet guest: the server carries `update` in the list model, health and status frames whatever listener served them, but every update route refuses it, so `updateForPage` (same file) gives the page no update unless the settings read says it is this computer or the owner (`tailnetGuest` in `api.ts`, false only for `guest: false`, so an Inbox that predates the field is a guest; the rule "Reload Claude Code N" follows): no dot, no Update button in the sidebar, no About dot or Update now. Tests: the DOM test `packages/inbox/sidebarUpdateDot.test.tsx` and the first case of `tests/e2e/inbox-updates.spec.ts`.
  - **Copy connect prompt**, an outlined pill (`.ib-cpill`, not an `.ib-nav`) at the top of the sidebar's foot: above the Update button or "What's new in X", which sit above Settings (order: owner's call 2026-10-16; owner approval 2026-10-13, design record `inbox-sidebar-connect-prompt`; look amended 2026-10-15 by `inbox-connect-row`, option 1b; neither in this repo): the Claude Code, Codex, Pi and OpenCode marks (`AgentIcons`, 16 px, own colours, `aria-hidden`), then "Copy prompt"; its name is "Copy prompt to connect an agent" (screen-reader words after the visible ones). Content-wide, capped at the sidebar: at the 200 px minimum the label ends in an ellipsis, never the marks. Not drawn on a phone (owner, 2026-10-31: "On mobile, the whole copy agent prompts thing doesn't need to show in the sidebar"; `drawnSidebarItems` in `sidebarItems.ts`); the desktop sidebar and its hover peek keep it. Only a copy button: no preview, no flyout, no tooltip, no shortcut. A click copies the prompt from `packages/inbox/connectPrompt.ts` (the one place its text lives): connect yourself as `<binary> inbox --help` says, send a first message with a question offering real recent work, end its turn and get the answer by itself or with `read_thread` when the person says they answered. `<binary>` is this computer's absolute `plannotator`, `GET /api/inbox/settings` `mcp_command` without its trailing `inbox mcp` (`connectCommand`), or bare `plannotator` plus where it usually lives until the settings load. The label then reads a check in `--success` and "Copied" for 2 s (`CONNECT_COPIED_MS`; the marks stay; the check's colour does not fade), or "Couldn't copy" for 4 s; focus stays on it, a polite live region (`data-connect-prompt-live`) says "Connect prompt copied." or "Couldn't copy the connect prompt.", and the phone sheet stays open (it is not navigation). Shown on a desktop window, a page opened over the tailnet included: the prompt names this computer's paths, so it is for an agent on this computer. Tests: `connectPrompt.test.ts`, the DOM test `connectPromptRow.test.tsx`, and a step in `tests/e2e/inbox-sidebar.spec.ts` that reads the clipboard in Chromium.
  - **Feedback** (`components/FeedbackDialog.tsx`; owner's ask, 2026-10-27; a draft, no design record yet): an `.ib-nav` row right above the Settings row. It opens a dialog (the Inbox `Modal`; a bottom sheet on a phone) titled "Feedback", with the Inbox's segmented switch under the title (`.ib-seg` as `role="tablist"`, as Settings → Appearance colors; the arrows, Home and End move it; approval "inbox-feedback-faq", 2026-11-09): **Message**, the default, holds the form; **Common questions** is an accordion (one answer open at a time) of the known answers that carry a question (`feedbackFaq.ts` derives them from `packages/inbox/feedbackKnown.ts`; edit the words there), each answer with at most one action (`simple-list` sets the list's real View → Simple list, `listView.ts`; `settings-<section>` opens that Settings section and `settings-experimental` opens Capabilities on its Experimental group; the window closes the dialog first) and one link (release notes or docs), then "Not here? Tell us about it" back to Message and Close. Both panels stay in the page with `hidden`, and the `Modal`'s Tab loop skips hidden elements and `tabindex="-1"`. Title, switch, box, chips, the privacy line and the footer share one left edge; the chips and the email are one height (32 px, 44 px on a phone). The form: a message box (1–4,000 characters; a count from 3,600), an optional category (Bug, Idea, Other as toggle pills; a second press clears it), an optional email (placeholder "Email, if you'd like a reply (optional)"; no separate "optional" line), a line with no icon saying what is sent ("Sends your message, Plannotator {version}, the name of your OS and the names of your agents. Nothing from your threads or files. This is anonymous unless you add your email."; it leaves out that the send came from the Inbox, by the owner's call), then "Prompt your agent to send feedback", Cancel and Send; after Send, a short "Thanks, it's sent" with Done. **"These might help"** (the owner's ask: a plain search for similar content; known answers first, approval "inbox-feedback-faq", 2026-11-09): a quarter second after typing stops, up to five lines under the box (three on a phone), all worked out in the window, so nothing typed reaches any server. **First, at most one known answer** (`packages/inbox/feedbackKnown.ts`): a hand-written list of common complaints, features and Experimental switches, each a short answer and one action (a setting, a Settings section, or release notes or docs), matched on whole phrases, or on a subject and a symptom within 8 words that are not negated ("no longer jumps" is praise); a message that reports something broken matches only entries marked `whenBroken`. It shows as the first line: "**Fixed** in 0.28.52: the list holds still now · Use Simple list" (Fixed in the accent color, the words linked to the notes, the action an accent link the window runs after closing the dialog; "Simple list is on" when it is), or "Experimental: …", or the words alone. The window hides answers it cannot follow (`hiddenKnownAnswers`: starting agents on Windows, a switch the settings read does not report). Code review, Shape and System, held until 0.29 (`heldSwitch`), are never in the list. Test: `packages/inbox/feedbackKnown.test.ts` (must-match and must-not-match phrases). **Then the search** (`feedbackSearch.ts`) over `feedbackSearch.data.json` (a hand list of features with a one-line definition and other names, open issues, fixed closed issues with the release that fixed them, open and merged PRs, release note headings). The file is its own chunk, loaded at the first search. Only the first 4,000 characters are searched. Only meaningful words count: four letters or more and not filler (`FILLER`: "please", "really", "with", "inbox", "plannotator"…). A title is similar when it shares at least two of them with what is typed, at least one of them rare (a word in 1 title in 50 or more, as "list" or "agent", is common), and most of the shorter side's, give or take an ending of up to 3 letters or one slipped letter; a hand-written feature also shows for its one-word name. A fixed issue reads "**Fixed** in 0.28.30: its title". A feature shows its name as the link and its definition; anything else only its title; every line is one line cut at its end; the feature a known answer stands for (`feature`) is not listed again. With no match the form is as without it. On a computer the dialog is placed from its top, so the box stays put; in a short window it starts higher so five lines leave Send in view, and in a very short one it scrolls. On a phone with the keyboard up the title and the switch go, the box is shorter, one line shows and its room stays empty when there is none, so Send never moves, and Cancel sits beside Send. `scripts/feedback-search-index.ts` rewrites the file while preparing a release; CI and builds never run it. A closed issue goes in only when it was completed and a published release's notes link the PR that closed it (the latest such release) or the issue itself (the first). What was typed and not sent stays in memory for the window's life, so any close (Cancel, the backdrop, Escape, the prompt button) and a reopen show it again; a sent message clears it. Only Send sends (Enter in the email field does not; no shortcut, by the owner's rule); a failure keeps the words and shows the reason in a callout: the Worker's refusal, or, when the service is unreachable, not deployed, failing or silent (the Inbox gives up after 8 s, the window after 10 s), the one plain line "Couldn't send feedback right now; nothing was saved." (`FEEDBACK_UNAVAILABLE`, the CLI prints the same). Send posts `POST /api/inbox/feedback` (`packages/server/inbox-feedback.ts`; the window's CSP allows only its own server), which adds the version, the OS name, the agent names (`inboxFeedbackAgents`: names only, from the hosts of the agent messages here) and `surface: "inbox"` (the dialog says the first three) and forwards to the feedback Worker (`apps/feedback-service`); over the tailnet only the owner may send (a guest gets `403 owner_only`). Closing the dialog puts focus back on the opener, or on the page heading when the opener is gone (the phone drawer). "Prompt your agent to send feedback" copies `feedbackPrompt()` (`packages/inbox/feedbackPrompt.ts`, with what is typed so far quoted), closes the dialog and shows the window's toast "Prompt copied": the agent asks for the feedback, shows the person the exact text and runs `plannotator feedback --stdin [--category …] [--contact …]` with the text in a quoted heredoc (`<<'PLANNOTATOR_FEEDBACK_END'`, `FEEDBACK_HEREDOC_END`; in PowerShell a single-quoted here-string with UTF-8 `$OutputEncoding`), so no shell expands `$VAR` or backticks in the approved words (never double quotes; `apps/hook/server/feedback-command.ts`) only after they say yes. **Privacy:** nothing is sent without that click or command; a submission carries the message, the category and email the person typed, the version, the OS name, the surface and (from the Inbox) the names of the agents used, never thread content, paths, sessions or an identifier, and the Worker stores no IP. Tests: `packages/inbox/feedbackDialog.test.tsx` (DOM list), `packages/inbox/feedbackKnown.test.ts`, `packages/inbox/feedbackSearch.test.ts`, `scripts/feedback-search-index.test.ts`, `packages/server/inbox-feedback.test.ts`, `packages/server/inbox-feedback-agents.test.ts`, `packages/core/product-feedback.test.ts`, `apps/hook/server/feedback-command.test.ts`, `apps/feedback-service/test/`.
  - The frame is Workspaces' sidebar primitive, copied by the owner's word (2026-10-09) into `packages/inbox/shell/` (each file names its Workspaces source and commit): the toggle at the head of each page and ⌘B / Ctrl+B close and open it on Workspaces' one spring (`shell-motion.ts`, MotionValues from `motion`, reduced motion lands without travel); ⌘B does nothing in an input, a textarea or an editor; the state is the Inbox's own cookie `plannotator-inbox-sidebar-open` (open when absent). Closed, a 600 ms rest on the left edge peeks it (1024 px and wider). The edge collapses on a click and resizes on a drag (200 to 520 px, 232 by default, cookie `plannotator-inbox-sidebar-width`), closing past 100 px and reopening if the held pointer comes back. Below 768 px it is a sheet. A file beside a thread closes it on the same spring without changing the stored choice.
  - **Pinned and Projects open and close like folders** (the owner's pick, direction C "The section is the folder", 2026-10-29; design record not in this repo; `packages/inbox/sidebarSections.ts`, `SectionHeader` in `components/Sidebar.tsx`): each section's header is a quiet 30 px row (`.ib-side-sec`, a `<button>` with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`; the section is `aria-labelledby` it), with a star for Pinned ("Starred" on screen) and a folder for Projects that is open while the section is and closed while it is not (Heroicons `folderOpen` / `folder`). A click, Enter or Space anywhere on it opens or closes the section; no shortcut. A chevron takes the count's place on hover and keyboard focus, points right closed and down open, and turns in 160 ms (none under reduced motion). Closing never hides where you are: the project the list shows, or the open pinned thread, stays under its closed header with its selection. A closed Pinned shows how many pins wait on the person (a question count, Stopped or Holds; the name reads "Starred N waiting on you"); a closed Projects shows nothing. Pinning a thread (or Unpin's Undo) while Pinned is closed opens it. Unpin from a closed section moves focus to the next row on screen, then the Pinned header, then Inbox. When the focused row a closed section kept goes because it stops being current (Escape closes the thread, Back leaves the project), focus moves to that section's header. The choice is this browser's cookie `plannotator-inbox-sidebar-closed` (`pinned`, `projects` or `pinned,projects`; absent: both open), stored through `storage` like the sidebar's open state and width, never synced; the onboarding tour's sidebar ignores it (`rememberSections={false}`). On a phone the headers are 44 px, the chevron always shows beside the name and the section's count, and a tap keeps the sheet open (see "The sidebar sheet, once open" under the phone layout below). One spacing rule for all three sections (the owner, 2026-11-05: "better consistent spacing"): a header sits 2 px under a closed section with no row left, and 12 px under rows, a closed section's kept row included (a phone's sheet: 8 px under whatever is above it); rows sit directly under their header, with no divider. Tests: the DOM tests `packages/inbox/sidebarSections.test.tsx` and the pin-while-closed case in `pinnedThreads.test.tsx`, the last step of `tests/e2e/inbox-sidebar.spec.ts`, and `tests/e2e/inbox-sidebar-spacing.spec.ts` (every gap, closed and open, on a desktop and a phone).
  - **The Agents section** (approval "inbox-agents-section", APPROVED STATE 2026-11-09, `.product/approved/inbox-agents-section/`; `packages/inbox/agents/`): in Machines' old place, above Projects, whenever the list carries `agents` (`agentsList.ts`; an older Inbox's list does not) or a machine is linked. A section like Projects, straight on the sidebar's ground with no panel behind it (the owner, 2026-11-09: "I like it without background"; `.ib-agentsec`, `agents.css`); closed, its heading alone, with no Add buttons and nothing under it: no agent row, no machine name, no empty line, whatever thread or machine page is open. This differs from Pinned and Projects, which keep where you are, on the owner's word (2026-11-09: "the list should not be dynamic … It's a human-controlled list"): only a press on its heading opens or closes Agents, and nothing else ever does (no code calls `setSidebarSectionOpen('agents', …)`). Its header is the shared `SectionHeader` ("Agents", the Plannotator goggles in the heading grey, `GogglesMark`), and it opens and closes like Pinned and Projects (`agents` in the cookie; a cookie from before that names `machines` closes Agents). While it is open, two quiet buttons sit on the heading row (always shown, not only on hover), each with a tooltip: + **Add agent** opens New message with "New <agent> session" chosen in the project looked at (else the most recently active), the agent started last when that machine has it (`ComposeRequest.launch`, `defaultStartProject`), the cursor in the message, or a plain New message where launch is off; the laptop-plus **Add machine** opens Settings → Machines (not on a tailnet guest's page). Phone: 44 px targets. `useInboxAgents` is plain state, never `useSyncExternalStore`, which would draw in the middle of the list read that set the agents, before what the reader does next (a snoozed row's move): the row menu's focus hand-off depends on one draw (`rowMenu.test.tsx`).
    - Machine captions (the owner, 2026-11-09): a machine that has no agent shows nothing, and a machine's name shows only while agents show from more than one machine. Then each name is a small quiet grey caption (12 px, flush left, no icon, no count), still that machine's filter (`#machine=<id>`) with its Update word (`MachineSidebarWord`), and its agents sit a small step in; 10 px between machines. With one machine's agents (or no machine linked) there is no caption: one flat list (`data-flat`), the agents flush. So a machine with no agent, or the only machine with agents, has no filter, no asleep note and no Update word in the sidebar: its state and its update are in Settings → Machines (the heading's Add machine button goes there), and its filter only by its `#machine=<id>` link. Every machine empty: the one line "Agents show here while they run." Each agent is a 24 px animated mark and its name (36 px rows, 44 on a phone). No counts, no state marks, no guide lines; a hovered row fills as the sidebar's other rows do, the shown one with the selection fill.
    - An agent's name is the name the person gave it (Rename…, below), else its title, else its thread's name (its launch's, else its newest one on its machine, one waiting on the person first), else its project, else its host (`agents/model.ts`). An ended agent is not listed.
    - **Rename… on an agent**: the project row's menu, parts and words (`ProjectRow.tsx`): ⋯ (`.ib-pmore`) on hover, keyboard focus and while the menu is open, always on a screen that cannot hover and on a phone (where the menu is a sheet titled by the agent); a right-click on the row opens the same menu (a long press on the mark stays its face picker). Items: Rename…, and Reset name once a name is given. Rename… turns the row into the project rename field (`ProjectNameField`, aria-label "Agent name", the agent's own name as placeholder): Enter saves, Escape or leaving cancels, a name past 64 characters is refused, an empty name resets. The live region and toast use the project rename's words; focus goes back to ⋯. Stored in the agent's faces entry (`POST /api/inbox/agent-faces` with `name`, `server-and-storage.md`), so it reaches every window (`event: agent-faces`), survives a restart, follows a continued session (`continues`, a launch's session) and outlasts unnamed entries under the faces' 200-agent cap; a new session with no `continues` (`/clear`) is a new agent with no name. The name stays on this Inbox, so an agent on a linked machine offers no Rename… (no ⋯, no right-click menu) until names reach the machine that owns the agent. Precedence is decided at read time (`agents/agentNames.ts`): a given name, else the session's own title (its latest poll's `label`, sent on every poll and read again on `event: deliveries` with `labels`), else the row's fallbacks. So a title the tool changes later shows only while no name is given, and Reset name shows the title as it is then. Read through `givenName`/`withGivenLabels`: the agents' list hooks (`label`: sidebar row, hover card, agent filter and title, a thread's sessions), `useSessionLabels` (list rows' agents, a message's author, the phone's recipient sheet), `useComposeSessions` (To picker, decision and rows asks), `useLiveSessions` (Send to another session), and the thread's "You added …" and hand-off lines. Display only: an `@` row keeps its label, the token the agent reads (the given name leads its second line), and tags, hand-offs and reply words are sent as before. Tests: `agents/agentNames.test.ts`, `agents/agentRename.test.tsx` (DOM), `packages/server/inbox-agent-faces.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/inbox-agent-rename.spec.ts`.
    - **A click on an agent filters the list** to its threads (the owner, 2026-11-10: "it should act as a filter not open threads"), as a machine's caption does, and opens none of them, even when it has one. The address is `#agent=<InboxAgent.id>`; it replaces a project or machine filter (a project, a machine or Inbox clears it), the list's title is the agent's row name (back buttons read it too), the row carries the selection fill (`ib-on`, the machine row's look) and is a toggle (`aria-pressed`), and on a phone the sheet's "Showing only" line names it with the goggles, its Clear the same Clear. A second click on the same agent shows everything again and keeps the thread on screen. A thread opened from the filtered list keeps the filter, as a machine's does. A live agent with no thread in the window has nothing to filter: a click opens New message to it, as before. An empty result (every thread archived) is the list's ordinary empty sections.
    - Which threads are an agent's (`agentListFilter`, `agentRowOf`, `filterAgentRows` in `agents/model.ts`): every row on its machine that its sessions wrote, were addressed in by the person, or took part in (`rowOf`, the test its row's thread uses), and its launch's thread. Its sessions are the one it runs as and those its conversation had before (`continues`), but for one listed as an agent of its own, a branch, which keeps its threads; an older session drawn as this same row (both in one thread) counts as this agent. An agent the list no longer names (ended, or an old link) is matched by its id as a session on any machine, and the title says "An agent". Only threads the window has count (the list's rows and the snoozed; not archived threads). Data flow: App reads the agents only while the filter is on (`useAgentListFilter` in `agentsList.ts`, heard through `onInboxAgentsSet`, so it never makes the list read again) and draws again only when who the agents are changes, never for a poll; the filter object stays the same until what it matches or its name changes, so a list read never redraws the rows for it. Its hover card says its host, project and machine, its state, when it started (and "From the Inbox" for a launch), when it was last heard from, and its thread when the name is not the thread's: only what the window already has.
    - **A thread's sessions, by name** (issue #3108, approval "inbox-thread-session-names", option "Unfold", `.product/approved/inbox-thread-session-names/`; `components/ThreadSessions.tsx`, the names from `threadSessions` in `agents/model.ts`). A thread two or more sessions wrote in (or were addressed or tagged in, `threadParticipants`) starts its header's line with the sessions' marks and "N sessions"; the count and time keep the line's right end. A thread with one session has no such line: its header is as before. The marks are the faces the sessions already have (a face picked in the sidebar, else the agent's own mark; nothing is picked or saved here), one per look: sessions that look the same share one mark, so three Claude Code sessions with no face of their own are one Claude mark and "3 sessions", never three identical marks; at most four marks. A mouse resting on it (only a mouse: `pointerType`), a click or tap, or keyboard focus opens it in place into each session's name and state ("waiting on you" in the accent, "working", "idle", "ended"), first to join first, a mark starting each run of sessions that look alike. On a computer the opened line is a band laid over the header's line from its left (`position: absolute` in `.ib-tmeta[data-sessions]`), so nothing under it moves; it wraps down over the first message when the names need more room, and a name past 240 px ends in "…" with the whole name in its tooltip. On a phone the first tap opens it (no hover first: the toggle's hover fill is inside `@media (hover: hover)`), below the marks, one session per line. A mouse leaving closes what a mouse opened; Escape (used, so the thread stays open; focus goes back to the toggle), a click or tap outside, focus leaving it, or a second click closes what was held open. No new shortcut. Each session is found through the sidebar's groups (so a conversation that went on under a new id is one session, and an older session drawn as a later row is that row) and named by a name it has itself: its title (the list agent's `label`), else the window's label for it (`sessionLabels.ts`), else its agent's name ("Pi", "Claude Code"). Never by a thread's subject or a project, the sidebar's fallbacks (`agentRowName`), which inside a thread read as the thread's own title; the sidebar is unchanged. The state is the list's agent's, read in `ThreadSessions` (which counts as watching the agents, like the sidebar's section) so only that line draws again on a poll; `ThreadPane` names the sessions through `useInboxAgentsWho` (who they are, not what moves) with App's rows and machines (`agentContext`). A session the list does not name, on a machine whose agents it lists, is "ended"; with no agents list there is no state. A click on a listed session's name in the opened line filters the list to that agent, as a click on it in the sidebar does (`#agent=<id>`, the name a toggle with `aria-pressed` and the accent while its filter is on; a second click shows everything again), and keeps the thread on screen, where the sidebar's click closes it: the person is reading this thread; a session the list does not name has no filter and its name is words. In the same threads each message leads with its session's name, the header's, in place of its agent's (the agent is the mark beside it, the name's tooltip "Billing refactor · Claude Code" and what a screen reader hears after it, "Billing refactor, Claude Code"; `data-session-name`); a session whose name is its agent's name reads as before, and a thread with one session keeps "Claude Code · <label>" (issue #2450). A session the person added to the thread is one quiet line, "You added Retry worker · 4:20 PM" (`data-quiet`), with no icon and no mark; a hand-off line is unchanged. Another machine's thread is named from that machine's agents. Two unnamed sessions of one agent both read as the agent ("Claude Code", "Claude Code"); the closed line's "2 sessions" says there are two, and nothing numbers them. Tests: `packages/inbox/agents/threadSessions.test.ts` (names, never a thread or project, continued sessions, ended, another machine), `packages/inbox/threadSessionsHeader.test.tsx` (DOM: the pile, focus, tap, mouse, Escape, live states, the names' filter, the message lines and "You added", one session unchanged), `tests/e2e/inbox-thread-sessions.spec.ts` (the compiled binary, three same-kind and six mixed sessions, a phone).
    - **The marks** (approval "inbox-agent-icon-motion", `agents/motion.ts`, the rigs in `agents/rigs.ts`): always alive, and never a sign of the agent's state (the owner, 2026-11-09: "I'd rather just be animated. They always move around"; the row's hover card still says the state in words). Claude Code, Codex, Pi and OpenCode move part by part through the Web Animations API (a life loop and a gesture layer per part, composited transforms and opacity only); any other host's plain mark, and our faces in the click cycle, move as a whole. Each mark has its own life from a seed (the agent's id): its own tempo, a loop of a different length for every part, and gestures picked by weight after gaps that never repeat (now and then a quick second one or a longer rest, never the same gesture twice running), one gesture starting at a time across the page. Claude is mostly at rest (the owner, 2026-11-09: it "spins too much"): no loop and no endless turn, only now and then, after a gap drawn from its own seed, one small gesture (a slow part turn that eases in and out and settles, a soft breath of a few percent, a ray's shimmer or a ripple round its rays), sized and spaced by `CLAUDE_CALM` (gaps of 4 to 12 s, turns of 18 to 40 degrees; the owner chose this level, 2026-11-09); Codex's cloud bobs and its prompt blinks, glances and types; Pi's blocks ride a wave, shuffle and hop; OpenCode's frame sways and leans and its square peeks, glances and blinks. Amplitudes are set in screen pixels of the 24 px mark: under about 1 px is not motion. **Attention**: while the pointer is in the sidebar (`.ib-side`; an open section only) each mark leans toward it on its own critically damped spring (`.am-look`: at most `AGENT_LOOK.shift` 1.5 px and `AGENT_LOOK.turn` 4°) and a rig's eyes look too (`.am-e`: Codex's prompt, OpenCode's square, Pi's top block; 1.6 px); the lean adds to the mark's own life and lets go when the pointer leaves the sidebar or rests for `AGENT_LOOK.stillMs` (3 s). Touch is never followed. Our faces are PNGs, so their eyes cannot move apart from the face: the whole face turns. Perf rules: one IntersectionObserver for the page, and an off-screen mark pauses every animation, schedules no gesture and follows nothing; one passive `pointermove` listener for the page while any mark shows, handled once a frame, the frames stopping when every mark has settled; a frame reads no layout (the marks' centres are measured once and again only after a resize, a scroll or a change of the list); reduced motion (or no Web Animations) holds rest poses set once as styles, with nothing animating and nothing following, and a face swap is a 160 ms fade; a face not shown is stopped; unmount cancels everything, and the last one removes the listeners. In a simulated DOM (happy-dom, whose Web Animations finish a loop at once) the marks hold still so the window's other DOM tests never pay for them; the marks' own tests (`agentsSection.test.tsx`, `agentMotion.test.tsx`) turn motion on (`agentMotionOptions.simulatedDom`) and stand in for Web Animations.
    - **Faces** (`agents/faceChoices.ts`): the agent's own mark, ten Glass colours (Black, Red, Orange, Yellow, Green, Teal, Blue, Purple, Pink, Ice; vectors from one generator, `agents/glassArt.ts`, each set in OKLCH, Black and Yellow redrawing the tab icon's Glass and Yellow and keeping their stored ids `glass` and `yellow`; the tile's rounded corners, `GLASS_CORNER`, are in the picture itself, so the row, the picker and every size draw the same rounded tile; our faces move as a whole, turned, moved and scaled alike on both axes, never stretched), Transparent (the goggles with no tile, one build per theme, `assets/agent-marks/`) and Face (the tab icon's picture, `tabIconArt.ts`). The pictures load on the first click or the first look at the picker (`agents/faceArt.ts`, its own chunk). The face is kept per agent until changed, by the Inbox for every window, the phone's and the iPad's (`inbox/agent-faces.json`, `server-and-storage.md`, "Agent faces"), and shown on every icon of that agent, not only here ("Every icon of an agent", below). The mark is a button of its own over its place in the row (`.am-btn`, a 28 px target; `AgentMark.tsx`), never the row's click. **The agent icon dialog** (`agents/AgentIconDialog.tsx`): the fan's "Your own…" and "Sets" open it in the middle of the window, the Inbox's own dialog (`Modal` in `components/Updates.tsx`, `agents/iconDialog.css`; a full-height sheet on a phone). Its head shows the agent's name and the icon it has now, a switch between the two places and Close; picking an icon applies it and closes; Escape and a click outside close; focus goes back to the agent's face. **Your own…** (the inner arc's fourth place, a dashed + or the agent's picture; `agents/OwnIconsBody.tsx`, `agents/ownIcons.ts`): a large drop area (a click picks a file; a drop anywhere or a paste while the dialog is open adds one), then the uploaded pictures (× takes one away with no question), any of which any agent can show (`own:<hash>`, `server-and-storage.md`). A picture is drawn as uploaded, scaled to fit the mark's whole tile (`object-fit: contain`, no crop, no tile colour, no mask), only ever as an `<img>` of its address (never markup, so an SVG runs nothing); it plays its own animation in this section only, and shows its still everywhere else and here under reduced motion (an animation inside an image ignores the setting); a picture this Inbox does not keep, or one that fails to load, is the agent's own mark. Tests: `agents/ownAgentIcons.test.tsx`, `packages/server/inbox-agent-icons.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/inbox-own-agent-icon.spec.ts`. **Sets** (the inner arc's fifth place, a little grid of four or the agent's set icon; `agents/SetsBody.tsx`, `agents/iconSets.ts`): in the dialog, a search field ("Search by name or number"), the sets as tabs, and the chosen set's numbered grid (as many 52 px columns as the dialog holds; `#001`, `#0025` past a thousand). A number ("27", "#027") finds that icon, words find names and search words; the arrows move in the grid and Enter in the search picks the first found (the panel's own keys, no shortcut). Icons load only as they scroll into view (an IntersectionObserver on the grid sets each image's address or mask). Choosing one keeps `set:<set>/<icon>` for the agent (not a copy into the uploads, so no cap).
