Built in: Tiny Creatures, game-icons.net, Dungeon Crawl, Noto Emoji 3D (`packages/inbox/assets/icon-sets/`, licences in its NOTICE); game-icons.net's grid has the credits line its CC BY 3.0 asks for ("Icons by game-icons.net authors · CC BY 3.0 · Credits", Credits opening its CREDITS.md). Fetchable sets (`server-and-storage.md`) show "Get" and their count until downloaded, then a bar and "N of M" while their grid fills in, then are like any other set; one stopped short shows "Get" again with what it has. Drawing (`agents.css`): an icon fills the mark's tile like an uploaded picture (no row changes size); pixel sets (`pixelated`, native `size`) are drawn nearest-neighbour at the largest whole multiple of their native size in device pixels that fits, else exactly a half or a quarter (`round()` over `--ib-dpr`, set by resolution queries); one-colour sets (`mono`, game-icons.net) are a CSS mask filled with `--foreground`, with a hidden `<img>` of the same address that says when it fails; a set may ask for `zoom` (drawn larger, edges cut). Only ever the icon's address on this Inbox, never markup; one that fails is the agent's own mark. Tests: `agents/setAgentIcons.test.tsx`, `packages/server/inbox-icon-sets.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/inbox-agent-icon-sets.spec.ts`.
    - **Every icon of an agent** (`agents/agentFaces.ts`, the one owner of the choice in the window): the face shows, as a still picture (only this section's marks move), wherever an icon knows the agent: a list row's tiles (each host's tile is its first session's), the badge card and pinned row, a message's author, the thread's participants and its delivery and hand-off lines, the reply box's recipient pill, its list and the phone's pill and sheet, the `@` menu, New message's To (its picker and the phone's sheet), Reply instead, Send to another session, drafts and reply floaters, the Board's cards and New card, decision cards, Tidy, the initiative peek, search's "message X", the starred pills' tooltip. The Inbox's `HostMark` takes the agent (`agent`: a session id or an `InboxAgent.id`) and `AuthorMark` passes an author's session by itself (`packages/inbox/icons.tsx`; ui's marks are unchanged); a mark of a kind of agent (Settings, the tour, "New Claude Code session", side chat, artifacts, Reviews, search results, decision chips) stays the plain mark. An entry is the agent's id with every session it was or is, so a conversation that went on under a new id (`continues`) and a launch's session keep the face, after they ended too; the agents' list adds the sessions a live agent is now (`onInboxAgentsSet`, which does not make the list re-read). One map of id to face, rebuilt on a change; per-id listeners, so a choice redraws only that agent's icons. A reload paints from `plannotator-inbox-agent-faces-v2` (the last faces with this page's changes) and the pictures' script is asked for at once, so no plain mark shows first; the faces this browser kept before (`plannotator-inbox-agent-faces`) show at once and go to the Inbox once (`adopt`), the old key removed only when it answered. Every settings read reads the faces again; `event: agent-faces` carries every save. Tests: `packages/inbox/agents/agentFacesEverywhere.test.tsx` (DOM: a choice on every surface at once, the stream, a continued session and a launch, an ended agent, no flash, render counts over 300 rows, the old faces carried over), `packages/server/inbox-agent-faces.test.ts` (the file, links, the cap, adopt, the route).
    - **The click cycle**: a click on a mark shows the next face of the cycle: the agent, Black glass, Yellow glass, Transparent, Face, the agent (from a colour only the picker gives, Transparent).
    - **The picker** (`agents/FaceFan.tsx`, placed by `agents/fanLayout.ts`): every face on a half-circle fan round the mark, on a plate like a sundial's upper half laid on its side, opening toward the content (the plate is frosted glass, the owner, 2026-11-09: the theme's surface, translucent over a blur, lit along the top; the faces rest on it with no well behind them, a hover lifts one, and the face shown has a dot under it, no outline; the solid surface where there is no backdrop-filter or under reduced transparency); the kinds (the agent's mark, Transparent, Face) on the inner arc, the colours on the outer. A Base UI Popover anchored to the mark (portaled; the list never moves). It opens after the pointer rests on the mark for `FAN_DWELL_MS` (350 ms; a pointer passing over opens nothing; never on touch), on Enter or Space on the focused mark, or on a long press on a touch screen (`FAN_PRESS_MS`, 450 ms; then 40 px targets 44 px apart). Its choices travel out along the arcs one after another (about 220 ms) and fold back on close; under reduced motion they fade in place. It closes on a choice, Escape, a press outside, a scroll that moves the mark, a resize, or the pointer out of the mark and the fan for `FAN_GRACE_MS` (140 ms; the way from the mark to a choice counts as the fan). Near the window's edges an arc turns away from the edge, and short of room the colours split over two short arcs, so no choice is cut off. Inside: a radio group named "Icon for <agent>", the arrows move along the arcs, Home and End, Enter or Space chooses and focus returns to the mark. No global shortcut.
    - **Animate it** (brief "icon-animate", the owner's go-ahead 2026-11-10: "a new thread will prompt an agent you choose or a new one to animate it and upon completion upload it to the inbox"; `agents/AnimateIt.tsx`, the words in `agents/animatePrompt.ts`, `packages/server/inbox-icon-animate.ts`, `apps/hook/server/inbox-icon-command.ts`; Bun only, the Inbox has no Pi server). Under the agent's own picture in the icon dialog's "Your own…": the picture, one line ("Asks an agent, in a new thread, to animate this picture. What it sends back becomes this icon, and the original stays one click away.") and **Animate it**. A press keeps a request with the Inbox (`POST /api/inbox/icon-animate` `{ animate: { icon, agent, sessions } }`, answered with an id `ia_<24 hex>`), closes the dialog and opens New message filled in through `openComposer` (compose/openComposer.ts, see `inbox-compose.md`): thread name "Animate an agent icon", the request words with the id, the picture attached (its original bytes), and To open on its own list (the running agents and "New <agent> session", the launcher's own row with whatever the launcher offers there, e.g. model and effort). The person sends it; nothing is sent for them. A step that takes over 20 s, or fails, says "Couldn't reach the Inbox. Nothing was sent; try again." and the button is back.
      - **The words** (`animatePrompt`, the one place to tune them): an animated SVG with CSS keyframes only, sizes judged in screen pixels at 24 px, gestures with their own loop lengths, frozen frames checked, the same picture with animation off. Pixel art moves in whole source pixels. A smooth picture is never redrawn: it is embedded once and moved whole, plus at most three cut-outs of the same image (a `<clipPath>` per part, a `<mask>` hole in the base so nothing shows twice); a blink is a lid in a colour picked beside the eye. Tested 2026-11-10/11: a Sonnet agent that traced a smooth fox lost its look; with these words one kept it.
      - **The hand-back, the one door agents have to an icon**: `plannotator inbox icon set <file> --request <id> [--json]` (a command, not a tool: tool text costs every session tokens; its `--help` is its own usage). It posts the file to `POST /api/inbox/icon-animate` `{ request, picture }` on the loopback listener. The picture is kept by the upload's own `AgentIcons.add` (sniffed, at most 512 KB, Set picture…'s refusal words, drawn only as an `<img>`): no second check. It changes only the agent the request names, and only while that agent shows the picture asked about or this request's last result (so "make it slower" in the same thread works); another agent showing the same picture is untouched. If the person picked another icon meanwhile, the picture joins "Your own…" and nothing changes ("Kept with the person's pictures, but not shown: …"). A made-up id is `no_request`. Requests live in `inbox/icon-animate.json` (0600, the newest 30); their pictures are never the ones the 60-picture cap removes. A hand-back is a faces save, so every window and the phone hear `event: agent-faces`; a window that was offline or had the dialog closed reads the faces again when its stream returns. A picture that does not load (heard just before the link dropped) shows the agent's own mark and is asked again when the link is back, else after 2, 8 and 30 s, for the sidebar (`retryOwnFace` in `agents/motion.ts`) and every still (`agents/useImageRetry.ts`, uploaded pictures and set icons); before, it stayed the agent's own mark until the next face change.
      - **The way back**: when the agent's picture came back from a request, the row says "An agent animated this picture." with **Use the original** (the original's still in the button), which puts the old picture back on this agent and closes the dialog. The window learns which pictures came back from `GET /api/inbox/icon-animate` (`{ results: [{ icon, result }] }`, no ids), read again whenever the pictures change. The animated picture stays in "Your own…".
      - Tests: `apps/hook/server/inbox-icon-command.test.ts` (a real Inbox: only the agent asked about changes, again with a better version, not after the person moved on; a made-up request, a page dressed as an SVG and a file past the cap change nothing) and `tests/e2e/inbox-icon-animate.spec.ts` (the critical path in Chromium: Animate it, the filled-in message with To open, Send, the command's hand-back while the window is offline, the icon changing by itself, Use the original; `PLANNOTATOR_ANIMATE_POOR_LINK=1` runs it at 300 ms and 1 Mbit/s and prints the step's time).
    - Tests: `packages/inbox/agents/agentsSection.test.tsx` (DOM: grouping, quiet captions, the add buttons' routes, an agent's click filtering and a second click clearing, New message's chosen session, the click cycle kept, reduced motion still, the perf guards with stand-ins for Web Animations and IntersectionObserver), `packages/inbox/agents/agentMotion.test.tsx` (DOM: each mark's own life, the lean, Claude at rest with no endless turn at every calm and its turns chaining), `packages/inbox/agents/faceFan.test.tsx` (DOM: dwell opens and a pass-over does not, a choice kept and shown, Escape, pointer-leave and scroll close, keys, long press, reduced motion; the fan's fit in the window), `packages/inbox/agents/agentsModel.test.ts` (groups, names, threads, the hover card's facts, the old cookie, the agent filter's threads), `packages/inbox/phoneSidebarSheet.test.tsx` ("Showing only" an agent).
  - **The Tags section** (approval "inbox-thread-tags", step 1; `components/TagsSection.tsx`, `threadTags.css`; the tags themselves in `inbox-messages.md` "Thread tags"). Under Starred, above Agents, and absent until a live tag holds a thread the window lists or the person made a tag (`tagsSectionShown`). Its heading is the tag's `#` (InitiativePeek's `TagIcon`) and "Tags"; it folds like Starred and Projects (`sidebarSections.ts` `tags`) and is a hideable item in Customize sidebar and the sidebar's right-click menu (core `inbox-sidebar.ts` `tags`, `place: 'middle'`). `+` on the heading, always shown (not only on hover), opens a "New tag" field in the section (Enter makes it, Escape or leaving cancels). Every tag is a row: its plain name, and the number of its threads waiting on the person (Stopped, Holding, Waiting) only when there is one; with none the name reads quieter (`.ib-proj-quiet`); the tag the list shows has the selected project's fill. Closed, only that one stays. A right-click (or a finger held on it, the row menu's `longPress`) opens Show threads, Rename (the row becomes a field: Enter saves, Escape or leaving cancels) and Delete tag (no question; the toast "Deleted · “Release”" has Undo, which restores it with every thread it held).
  - **A tag's list** (`#tag=ini_…`, `Route.tag`): the threads in the tag in the Inbox's groups, picked in place of a project, machine or agent (never beside them), titled with the tag's `#` and name and "N threads · M projects" (quiet, `plural`). Inbox leaves it. Empty: "Nothing tagged <tag> yet" and "Right-click a thread and choose Add tag." A tag deleted while its list shows puts the Inbox in its place. On a phone's sheet, "Showing only" names the tag.
  - **Customize the sidebar** (approval "inbox-sidebar-customize", 2026-11-08, `.product/approved/inbox-sidebar-customize/`; core `inbox-sidebar.ts`, `packages/inbox/sidebarLayout.ts`, `sidebarItems.ts`, `components/SidebarMenu.tsx`, `components/SidebarCustomizer.tsx`). The person chooses which items show, their order, and whether each sits in the top stack or the bottom one. The items are one registry (core `INBOX_SIDEBAR_ITEMS`: id, words, place, whether it hides), so a new row is one entry there and an icon in `SIDEBAR_ITEM_ICON`.
    - Items: the logo (the Tater mark and "Plannotator Inbox"; it hides, it does not move), Search, New message, Inbox, Board, Shape, Reviews, Drafts, Copy prompt, Feedback, Usage, Settings, and the keyboard and light/dark buttons (they hide and ride on the Settings row). Inbox and Settings never hide (a lock, "Always shown"). **Starred**, **Tags**, **Agents** and **Projects** scroll between the two stacks and are items, place `middle` in the registry (the owner, 2026-11-09: "the customized sidebar and all that needs to be able to hide agents"; 2026-11-12: "A customized sidebar should allow me to hide anything, including stars"): each hides, none moves. Hidden, the whole section is gone (heading, add buttons, rows), on a phone too; nothing else changes (agents keep running, starred threads stay starred, every project's threads stay in the list). Agents and Tags are offered only while the sidebar draws them (`drawnSidebarItems`); Starred and Projects are always offered (Starred, shown, still appears only once a thread is starred, and not while "Keep starred above the list" draws the starred threads as pills; hiding Starred leaves the pills as they are). A layout saved before an item existed shows it, and no preset hides any of the four. The notices (Update, What's new, Can't check, Reload agents) are not items either: they head the bottom area, under Copy prompt when it leads it.
    - The default is the sidebar as it was. Search and New message share their row while they sit side by side; apart, New message is a row of its own. An item this Inbox does not draw (Board, Reviews or Shape with its capability off, Usage without `cswap`, Copy prompt and the keyboard button on a phone; `drawnSidebarItems`) is in neither the menu nor the panel and keeps its place in the layout for when it comes back.
    - **Right-click menu:** a right-click anywhere in the sidebar that a row's own menu does not claim (a project's, a starred thread's), or Shift+F10 / the menu key on a focused sidebar control (opened under it; no new shortcut). "Show in sidebar": a plain checkbox per item that hides, in the sidebar's order (Starred, Tags, Agents and Projects between the top stack's and the bottom's); a tick changes the sidebar at once and leaves the menu open. Then "Customize sidebar…" and, once the layout is not the default, "Reset to default". Nothing moves from the menu (the owner). A touch never opens it. Escape or a pick puts focus back on the control it was opened from, or on Inbox if that row just hid.
    - **The panel** ("Customize sidebar", a 300 px dialog beside the sidebar; on a phone a bottom sheet with 44 px rows): "Start from" Default / All at top / Minimal (buttons with `aria-pressed`; "Custom" when none), then Top and Bottom, each row a grip, the icon, the name and a switch (`role="switch"` named by the row's words). Drag a grip within or across the groups (a tinted gap shows where it lands; a touch never drags), or press it (click, Enter, Space, a tap) for Move up, Move down and Move to bottom / top (at the start of the bottom stack, the end of the top one, next to the lists). Move up / down skip rows this Inbox does not draw and stop at the end of their stack. Between Top and Bottom, the rows Starred, Tags, Agents and Projects, in the sidebar's order (the section's mark, the name, a switch; no grip). "Reset to default" (off at the default) and Done; × and Escape close it too. Focus lands on its title and goes back to what opened it. Opening it from Settings on a desktop opens the sidebar, so each change shows.
    - **Settings → General → Appearance → Sidebar** ("What shows, and where. Also from a right-click on the sidebar.", "Customize…"): the keyboard's way to the panel, and the phone's (a phone has no right-click).
    - **Saved for the person:** one layout everywhere, config.json `inboxSidebar` (`environment.md`) through `POST /api/inbox/settings` `{ sidebar }`, the whole layout each time. As the rows' look does (see "List options"): a change shows at once, then is saved, one save on its way at a time; every answer, settings read and `event: sidebar` carries the save count (`sidebar_rev`, config.json `inboxSidebarRev`), so a page always ends on the newest layout. The cookie `plannotator-inbox-sidebar-layout` (`top;bottom;hidden`, comma lists; absent at the default) paints the last layout before the settings read; it is never the truth. The sections' open state, the sidebar's width and its open state stay per browser. The onboarding tour's sidebar draws the default and has no menu.
    - Tests: `packages/core/inbox-sidebar.test.ts` (the model: reading a saved layout, a newer build's names, defaults for missing items, the strict write check, hiding and the locks, moves and capability-gated places, presets) and the DOM test `packages/inbox/sidebarCustomize.test.tsx` (the menu's checkboxes, the keyboard's way in, no menu on touch, the panel's switches, locks, grip menu, presets, Reset and Escape, a capability-gated item, another window's save, the tour, and Agents: listed only while drawn, hidden whole, kept across a reload, on a phone, an old layout showing it).
    - **The Sidebar tip** (the owner, 2026-11-09: "the same thing we did for … launch new agents … a little popover thing, arrow point into it"; `packages/inbox/sidebarTip.ts`, `components/SidebarTipCard.tsx`, `packages/shared/inbox/sidebar-tip.ts`): a one-time card that says the sidebar can be customized, the Launch tip's sibling (`inbox-launch.md` "The Launch tip") and the same card (`.ib-tour-coach.ib-ctip`, with `.ib-st`). Words (`SIDEBAR_TIP_WORDS`): eyebrow "New", title "Make the sidebar yours", line "Hide what you don't use and reorder the rest. Right-click the sidebar to start.", button "Got it".
      - **Who.** The owner's pages (never a tailnet guest) on an Inbox where no sidebar layout was ever saved (`inboxSidebarRev` counts the saves, so a layout set back to the default still counts: they know), a day or more after first use, until it was shown once. A desktop only: a phone's sidebar is a sheet the calm moment never finds open and a phone has no right-click, so a phone neither shows nor claims it.
      - **When.** At the reveals' calm moment (`useRevealMoment`, the star ask's): a thread read for 5 s and left, or the page idle for 20 s (`idleClock.ts`); 60 s into the page; the list loaded and not the first-run screen; the sidebar docked open on the Inbox page; nothing typed or open, no question waiting in the open thread, no toast. Never on a load with the tour or an update, nor while another one-time dialog is open or the star ask is armed, nor within the Inbox's 40-minute gap of any dialog or the Launch tip: the two tips keep the line's gap both ways (the owner, 2026-11-09; `packages/server/inbox.ts` `tipFree` reports and claims a tip only outside the gap, and `revealState` `tipShownAt` starts the gap from a tip's stamp, so a line dialog follows a tip, or a tip a dialog, 40 minutes later at the earliest, on the same tab or any load, and they never stack). It lets every other reveal go first: at its moment it waits `SIDEBAR_TIP_TIMES.yieldMs` (800 ms) and stands down, unclaimed, when one of them claimed meanwhile; a moment that stays held back is not spent either, so a later moment still has it. Once it is up the other reveals hold back for the gap (`noteOneTimeDialogShown`, `REVEAL_TIMES.otherDialogMs`), not for the rest of the load, and its claim makes the page read the line again (`lineBusy`), so the next dialog is armed by the one timeout when the gap ends.
      - **Seen once, server-side.** `GET /api/inbox/settings` `reveal.sidebar_tip` says it is due; the page claims it (`POST /api/inbox/reveal` `id: "sidebar_tip"`, with the page's key), which stamps config.json `inboxReveals.sidebar_tip: { shownAt, key }`. Every window shares it. Outside the line of one-time dialogs: no day, spacing or load stamp.
      - **Where.** Fixed, right of the sidebar, its arrow on the sidebar's edge level with the Inbox row (which never hides); it follows the sidebar's width, a short window keeps the whole card on screen and moves the arrow along it, and a window with no room beside the sidebar shows nothing (`sidebarTipPlace`). Under every menu and dialog.
      - **Leaves on** Got it, Escape (only the tip closes; with a menu or dialog open Escape is theirs), a right-click on the sidebar (its menu is what the tip is about; the menu still opens), the Customize panel opening, or the sidebar going away (closed, another page, a file, search or New message over it). No timer. Every way out is final.
      - **Accessibility.** Focus never moves to it and a pointer press on Got it keeps focus where it was; the window's polite region says it once (`SIDEBAR_TIP_SAID`). No ring on the sidebar, no new keyboard shortcuts.
      - Tests: `packages/inbox/sidebarTip.test.tsx` (DOM: the moment, not due, a refused claim, the sidebar closed, yielding to another reveal, held back, every way out, the place), `packages/shared/inbox/sidebar-tip.test.ts` (who and the claim), and the Inbox-level cases at the end of `packages/server/inbox-reveal.test.ts`.
  - **The order of Projects** (a person's feedback and the owner, 2026-11-05: "either alphabetically or most recent ... an elegant toggle"; `packages/inbox/projectSort.ts`, `ProjectSortToggle` in `components/Sidebar.tsx`): one quiet icon button left of the header's chevron, a sibling of the header in `.ib-secrow`, with no words and no extra row. The icon is the order on screen. A list starts in today's order (the server's, `created_at` ascending, so oldest project first; Hugeicons `sorting-01`), so nobody's list changes until they press. The first press goes A to Z (`arrow-down-a-z`), and from then on the button goes between most recent (`clock-01`) and A to Z, never back to today's order (the owner's "either alphabetically or most recent"). A to Z goes by the window's name for the project, ignoring case and accents and counting numbers (`app2` before `app10`). Most recent is the newest `last_at` among the project's threads in the window's list (`projectActivity`; a snoozed thread counts, an archived one does not), so it moves live; a project with no thread there (held on screen after its last was archived) goes last; a tie keeps today's order. While most recent is on, the rows are out of scroll anchoring (`overflow-anchor: none` on `.ib-projs[data-recent]`), or a row in view moving up would take the scrolled sidebar with it; the other orders keep the browser's anchoring. The icon shows on the header's hover and on keyboard focus, always where nothing hovers, and on a phone as a 44 px target at the row's end, after the heading's fold area; a tap keeps the sheet open. No toggle with fewer than two projects on screen or while the section is closed; going while it holds focus, it hands focus to the Projects header. The choice is this browser's cookie `plannotator-inbox-projects-sort` (`name` or `recent`; absent, as before the first press, or anything else: today's order), through `storage`, never synced; every sidebar on the page follows it. Only this list reads it: the project pickers, the combine menu and the Board's filter keep their orders. No shortcut. Tests: the DOM tests `packages/inbox/projectSort.test.tsx` and `tests/e2e/inbox-projects-sort.spec.ts` (a real reload, the hover, the phone's target).
- A project's name in the window (the rows of every shape, the sidebar's projects and its pins' tooltip and name, the head of one project's list, its back buttons and its "Everything in … is archived", the list's "N new in …") is its folder's name, with the parent added only when two projects on screen share it (owner's pick, 2026-10-29: "Shorten it in the window only … Agents still see the long name"; `shortProjectNames` in `packages/inbox/projectLabels.ts`). A checkout in a `worktrees` or `.worktrees` folder (Workspaces' `<repo>/worktrees/main`) reads as its repository, the folder above `worktrees` (owner's pick, 2026-10-29, a variant of B: `~/workspaces/projects/appnotes/worktrees/main` is "appnotes"); `worktrees` and the checkout's folder leave the name and the parents a clash adds (`labelPath`); above a dot-folder it goes up past it (Claude Code's `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/agent-…` reads as the repository), a `worktrees` folder right in a home folder (`/Users/<x>`, `/home/<x>`) stays an ordinary folder, and two checkouts of one repository are told apart by the checkout ("appnotes · main", "appnotes · fix"). Projects at one place (the same folder, or a plain folder and the checkouts in its `worktrees`, such as the owner's `appnotes` folder and `appnotes/worktrees/main`) are never told apart by parents, which they share: the folder reads its bare name and a checkout adds its own ("appnotes", "appnotes · main"); the same folder twice reads one name. Otherwise each project takes its own shortest parents that no other place of the name shares (normally one folder, "appnotes · projects"), and a home folder in them reads "~" ("appnotes · ~", "appnotes · ~/projects"), unless two would then read the same (the owner, 2026-11-02: the old rule climbed to "appnotes · Users/ramos/workspaces/projects"). `tests/e2e/inbox-project-labels.spec.ts` proves both against the binary. `App.tsx` works out ONE name per project (`windowProjectNames`) and hands it to all of them, over the projects the sidebar shows plus those with a row in the held list (a folded Quiet included): the list holds still while a thread is open and the sidebar does not, so naming each from its own set named a project two ways after an archive or a new message (the real-data check of #2349; `projectLabelHeld.test.tsx`). A pin from a project neither draws is named against that set plus itself. The rule is one copy in core (`@plannotator/core/inbox-project-labels`), which the server's `name` (`inboxProjectLabels`: agents, search, notifications, `plannotator inbox projects`, sentences in a thread) uses too, since 2026-11-02 (the owner: the window said "appnotes" while agents said "main"), so agents now see the short name as well (this supersedes "Agents still see the long name" above for agents; an existing project's agent-facing name can change, e.g. "api · ramos" to "api · ~", "agent-1" to "repo · agent-1", and a clone kept in a folder named `worktrees`, `~/src/worktrees/api`, reads "src", since a name is worked out from the path alone); `tests/project-label-parity.test.ts` holds them equal. Only the set differs: the server weighs every stored project, so an old worktree can make a server name read "plannotator · plannotator" where the window shows "plannotator". Known gap: the thread header, New message, the review and Reply read the thread's server name, so they can differ from the rows when a project off screen shares the name. A home folder in the parents reads "~" by its shape (`/Users/<x>`, `/home/<x>`, not `/Users/Shared`), so another person's home reads "~" too.
- **Every list group closes by a click on its header** (owner approval "inbox-list-groups-close", 2026-11-05; `packages/inbox/listGroups.ts`): each band is a `<button>` with `aria-expanded`; its chevron (`GroupBand` in `InboxList.tsx`, owner 2026-11-08) is decorative and hidden until the pointer is on the header (at once, no wait, as the sidebar's sections; owner, 2026-11-08) or keyboard focus lands on it, never on a touch, at the header's right end, fading in over 200 ms and out in 100 ms (opacity and a 3 px slide; the fade only under reduced motion); a closed band looks exactly as an open one (owner's correction, 2026-11-06: only its rows go), its count going bold (`.ib-n[data-new]`) when an unread row's latest agent message came after the group closed; closed Stopped, Holding up work and Snoozed keep the warning triangle (`FoldedBlocker`), a closed group never opens by itself, and focus in a closing group goes to its header. Which groups are closed is this browser's cookie (`plannotator-inbox-list-groups`, as `sidebarSections.ts`, never a setting; the tour ignores it): no entry is today's default, Quiet and the Snoozed band closed (Snoozed back to closed each time it reappears), every other group open. ↓ ↑ stop on every header; Home End and J K with no thread open go to threads only (`listKeyTarget` in `listKeys.ts`). Proof `listGroups.test.tsx`; DOM tests that click Quiet or Snoozed call `resetListGroupsForTests()` after each test.
- A wide row (a list 900 px and wider) is one line (approvals "inbox-row-byline", the one-line row, 2026-10-29, and "list-rows-mail-icons", 2026-10-30), in this order:
  - the agents' marks (see "Agents' marks" below) and, 6 px after them, the project label, in one column (`.ib-from`; 220 px, 260 px with the full words; in one project's list no label, the marks alone in a column as wide as that row's marks, the title 6 px after them, with or without the pin's column). The marks are never cut; a long project ends in "…"
  - the title cell (`.ib-subj`): the title (`.ib-title`, `titleOf`: the thread's name, else its subject; weight 600 when unread), then the grey preview (`.ib-pv`, `previewOf`: the thread's latest message, the row's `latest_preview`; see "The row's grey line" below), and the "Guided review" mark when the thread carries one. Short of room the preview gives way first, all the way, then the title, each cut with "…"
  - The row's grey line (approval "inbox-row-latest-preview", 2026-11-09, `.product/approved/inbox-row-latest-preview/`): the thread's latest message, like a mail app's. The server words it (`latest_preview` on each list row, `InboxStore.latestPreview` in `packages/shared/inbox/store.ts`, the words in `packages/core/inbox-row-preview.ts`): while an agent message is unread (after the look, `unseen`), the newest of them; else the newest message, the Inbox's own notes (👍, Mark read, Tidy) read past. An agent's question reads "Asked: <its first open prompt>", an open action item alone "Action item: …", files alone "2 files: a.zip, b.png", else the first words (markdown, fences, tables and question blocks dropped). The person's reply reads "You: <their words>", with no words "You answered: <the picks>" or "You skipped N questions". Plain text, cut at a word near `INBOX_ROW_PREVIEW_MAX` (160) characters; only the body's start is read (`firstWords`' `enough`), and the line is kept per message record with its questions' states, so a list rebuild reads no body again. Null for an unnamed thread whose latest message is its first (the title is that message), so an unnamed thread gets the line once it has a reply and its row is one line taller. A named Sent row keeps its own words instead (who has the reply, a re-ask that waits, an older Inbox's "Sent to"). A line that only repeats the title is not drawn. A row without the field (an older Inbox, a linked machine not updated) shows a named thread's subject, as before. Every row shape (beside a thread, compact, full width, phone) and Settings → Rows' pictures read it through `previewOf`; agents' `list_threads` rows never carry it. The linked machines' forward keeps it (rows are passed whole). Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/store-row-preview.test.ts`, `packages/inbox/listLabels.test.tsx`, `packages/inbox/rowsSettings.test.tsx`.
  - the count in words when it has words (`.ib-cw`; `CountWords` with `wide`, `countWords` in `InboxList.tsx`), in full in a list of 1150 px or more (`FULL_WORDS_MIN_LIST_WIDTH`, `.ib-fullw` on the list; a 1440 px window with the sidebar gives a 1192 px list): the owner's call of 2026-10-29 is full words in full-width mode. "Draft · Your reply, 2 picks", "Undelivered 14 min" (then "2 hours", "1 day"), "Answered, not sent", "Read", "Archived", drawn and read as they are (open action items are no words: see "Open counts" below). The column is 14% of the row, from 148 px to 160 px (1440 and wider); longer words end in "…" with the full words in the tooltip. A wide row in a narrower list (900 to 1150 px, and one project's list below 900 px, which keeps the wide row) keeps option A, as the three-line and phone rows do: the short forms ("Draft", "Undelivered 14m") in a 124 px column, the marks alone (an empty 52 px badge column) and a 220 px project column, so the title cell keeps its room; the full words are the tooltip and the row's accessible name. Every column's width depends only on the list's width, so every row's columns line up and every title cell has the same room, words or not
  - then, right-aligned, each in a fixed column of its own so they line up down the list in every row: the badge column (52 px, empty since the one question mark; with the full words 110 px: a blocked row's words, "Stopped" in the Stopped text colour and "Holds up 3" in the Holds up one, the owner's call of 2026-10-29 for full-width mode, kept or left to the tooltip by `WIDE_ROW_WAIT_WORDS`; `WaitWords`, see "Row badges" below), the pin (`.ib-cpin`; the owner, 2026-10-31: the pin always on the right, beside the counts, never right after a short preview), the open counts (`.ib-cq`, 84 px: the action pill, then `?N`, see "Open counts" below) and the time (76 px). The pin's column is there only in a list with a pinned row (`.ib-pincol`, Quiet's folded rows included), so a list without pins keeps its columns: an 11 px track plus the 10 px gap, the 13 px pin ending 8 px before the open counts' column, on one x down the list. In such a list, open counts wider than 84 px ("[□ 12] ?12", "[□ 99+] ?99+") widen the counts' column for the whole list to the widest (`fitCountsColumn` sets `--ib-cq-w` on the list), so no count runs under a pin; a list without pins keeps the 84 px column. The one-project list with Hide agent icons has its own pin-column templates (with the look options in `inbox.css`). Hovering or focusing a row hides only the time and the open counts, which the Mark read or Send now action covers; the words, the project with its marks and the badge column stay. So on a wide row a pointer reaches a blocked row's card through its words (full-width mode) only; the question mark under the action does not take a hover there (keyboard focus opens the card). Test: the last case in `tests/e2e/inbox-row-badges.spec.ts` seeds rows with words (a draft, Undelivered 14 minutes and a day) and with action items, and checks the full words at 1440 (a 1192 px list), the short forms at the 1000 and 900 px lists and in one project's list under 900 px, the action pill in the open counts' column, and at each width every column's right edge, the title cell as the widest column and the Undelivered pill inside its column

  A list narrower than 900 px (`WIDE_ROWS_MIN_LIST_WIDTH`, e.g. beside an open thread) draws the three-line row (`NarrowRow`; approval "list-rows-mail-icons", 2026-10-30, option B "mail-multi", which replaced the two lines of "inbox-row-byline", option 09f, and with them the thread pill in the row). Line 1 (`.ib-l1`, approval "inbox-list-row-v2", 2026-10-30, `.product/approved/inbox-list-row-v2/`) is the agents' tiles, then the project (13 px, weight 500; dark and 550 when unread) hugging them 6 px from the tile's edge, then the delivery ✓ and the time at the right: one 17 px line that never wraps. Line 2 (`.ib-l2`) is the title (the thread's name, else its subject) at the row's text size and weight when read (13.5 px, 400), bigger when unread (15 px, 600; approval "inbox-row-unread-bigger", 2026-10-30), never cut: a long one wraps. Line 3 (`.ib-l3`) is the grey preview (the named thread's subject) and the guided review mark, then at its right end the pin (always there, before the badge and the count, however short the preview; the owner, 2026-10-31), the badge (when the row has one) and the count (when there is one) at the far right; a row with none of these has no line 3 (`:empty`). The open counts are the action pill and `?N` (`LineCount`, `OpenCounts`; see "Open counts" below); any other count is in words, in its short form in a slot of at most 124 px (approval "inbox-row-byline", option A, 2026-10-29): "Draft", "Undelivered 14m", "Read", "Archived", "To <agent>" (cut with "…" past the slot). The full words are the tooltip (not on a phone) and the row's accessible name, so the preview keeps its room. Short of room the preview is cut first, all the way if it must. A long project never wraps: it ends in "…" (its tooltip shows it whole), and the tiles' run is bounded so it never pushes the project off the line. The time, the badge and the count are never cut. On hover or keyboard focus the Mark read or Send now action covers line 1's right end, above the title, and may cover the end of a long project there; the row keeps its height.

  **Agents' tiles** on a three-line or phone row (approval "inbox-list-row-v2", 2026-10-30; `RowAgentTiles` in `components/RowAgents.tsx`): before the project, the thread header's overlapping 20 px tiles (`.ib-tg-stack`'s size and 13 px glyph, 5 px under each other), one per agent (`rowAgentGroups`, as the wide row's marks: two sessions of one host, or two spellings of it, are one tile, so Claude Code twice and Codex draws a Claude tile and a Codex tile), first to join first, at most two (`ROW_AGENT_TILES`), then a "+N" tile counting the other agents. Every list row's tiles (these and a wide row's, not the header's) have no ring and no cut (the owner, 2026-10-31): the fills run together into one grey shape, and every logo paints over every fill, so each stays whole, 2 px clear of the next. On a hovered row the tile steps halfway from `--muted` to `--border`, because the hover pill is the tile's own grey at 45% and in dark the tile nearly vanished on it. The run is one `role="img"` named by every agent (`rowAgentWords`), the same words as its tooltip (not on a phone; a named session says its label, "Fix login redirect (Claude Code)", "3 Claude Code sessions (A, B, 1 unnamed)", issue #2450, see `inbox-compose.md`); the tiles are `aria-hidden`. In the DOM the project comes before the tiles, so a screen reader reads the project, then the agents, as on a wide row; CSS (`.ib-lead > .ib-atiles { order: -1 }`) draws the tiles first. Tests: `packages/inbox/rowAgents.test.tsx` (DOM: one tile per agent and "+N", a repeated host never hiding another agent, the name, the row read project first, the project never wraps), `tests/e2e/inbox-row-lead.spec.ts` (1440 beside a thread and a 390 px phone: a long project with five agents stays one line, the tiles before it).

  **Agents' tiles** on a wide row (approval "list-rows-mail-icons", 2026-10-30; `RowAgents` in `components/RowAgents.tsx`): before the project (the owner, 2026-10-31: "agent icons need to move to the left of the title in the long view", as the three-line row's tiles are), the same grey tiles as a three-line row (`.ib-atile`, above: 20 px, a 13 px logo, 5 px under each other, no cut, so every logo stays whole and the tiles read as one grey shape; the owner, 2026-10-31, who had seen bare 16 px marks with a mask and with no gap), one per agent host, first to join first (the owner, 2026-10-31: "they should be overlapped, always show multiple agents before multiple of the same"), from the row's `participants`, else the thread's agent (`rowAgent`: a person-started thread's addressee). Two spellings of one host are one tile (`agentName`). The row draws at most three tiles, two past three hosts, then "+N" as plain small text, which counts every session not drawn as a tile: three Claude Code sessions and a Pi are Claude, Pi, "+2"; two Claude Code sessions are Claude, "+1". No agent name is drawn as words and there is no "You" (one project's list shows the tiles alone). The tiles and "+N" are one `role="img"` whose name lists every agent, the hidden ones too ("Claude Code, 2 Codex sessions, OpenCode and Pi", `rowAgentWords`), the same words as its tooltip (not on a phone); the tiles and "+N" are `aria-hidden`. The hover card's footer keeps its agent's mark and name (`RowMark`). The project hugs the tiles: the tiles start the cell, then 6 px (the three-line row's gap, approval "inbox-list-row-v2", v2-r3), then the project (`.ib-from`, flex, `gap: 6px`). Nothing reserves width for the tiles: each row's project starts right after its own tiles, so the project labels do not line up down the list (the owner, 2026-10-31, who had rejected a reserved slot on the narrow row: "Why tf are you creating spaces big gaps like this between agent and project"). The tiles never shrink; the project ends in "…" and never wraps. In the DOM the project comes before the tiles, so a screen reader reads where, then who, as on a three-line row; CSS (`.ib-from > .ib-ags { order: -1 }`) draws the tiles first. With Hide agent icons the tiles leave and the project starts the cell. In one project's list the tiles' column is as wide as that row's tiles (`max-content`) and the title starts 6 px after them, the same gap (the grid's 10 px gap less a -4 px margin on the tiles' cell), with no blank before the title (the owner, 2026-10-31). Its titles therefore start after their own tiles, not on one left edge; only the columns right of the title line up. One project's list keeps the wide row at the narrowest desktop widths, down to a 496 px list (`ONE_PROJECT_WIDE_ROWS_MIN_LIST_WIDTH`; the default sidebar leaves 500 px at a 768 px window), so short of room the words column gives way: the title's track is `minmax(96px, 1fr)` and the words' `minmax(0, 124px)`, so the title keeps about nine characters, the words end in "…" (the tooltip has them whole), the tiles and "+N" stay whole on one line, and the counts and time stay in line, with or without the pin's column. Words nothing else on the row says (Draft, Answered, Read …) never go: such a row's words track is `fit-content(124px)`, so the title keeps what the words do not use; short of room the words keep 4.4em, "Answered" whole at any text size, and the title gives up the difference, down to 56 px. An Undelivered pill never shrinks to a letter: at 72 px of column or less it leaves (a container query on `.ib-cw`) and the row keeps a 96 px title. The clock before the time stays and a screen reader keeps the words in the row's name. The words' tooltip stays on the column, whose empty cell fills it, because a hovered row with Mark read or Send now hides the clock and the clock's own tooltip with it (`.ib-rw:hover:has(.ib-ract)`); nothing draws there, so a pointer finds it only by resting on the blank column. With room nothing changes, because the words' track fills to 124 px before the title's `1fr` grows. Under 496 px, which only a wide sidebar reaches, the list draws the three-line rows, where every word and the time stay whole, instead of letting the time run off the row. That measure is ignored on the commit that closes a thread, when it still holds the 380 px column's answer: trusting it swaps every row twice as the close hands a row its focus (`openStaysPut.test.tsx` hangs on it). Tests: `packages/inbox/rowAgents.test.tsx` (DOM, "a wide row's agents": the tiles, +N, the name, the hugging cell), the desktop case of `tests/e2e/inbox-row-badges.spec.ts` (on one- and two-tile rows the project starts 6 px after its own tiles), `tests/e2e/inbox.spec.ts` (the project after the tiles; in one project's list the title 6 px after each row's tiles, one tile or three with "+N"; the tiles overlap uncut with every logo whole and "+N" after them; at 780 and 768 px, with and without the pin's column, a 96 px title, the words in their column, "Draft" and "Answered" whole with the title keeping what they do not use, the pill gone at 72 px with the clock and the words' tooltip staying, the columns in line; a wide sidebar, nothing cut or off the row; the tile's contrast on a hovered row, light and dark), `tests/e2e/inbox-list-options.spec.ts` (Hide agent icons: the project starts the cell).

  **List options** (approval "inbox-list-options", 2026-10-30, `.product/approved/inbox-list-options/`; `packages/inbox/listLook.ts`): four checkboxes in the list's ⋮ menu under Tidy and Refresh (`TidyMenu`'s `look` and `onLook`; the Board's ⋮ has none): "Highlight thread subject", "Compact rows", "Hide agent icons", "Smaller thread names" (the owner, 2026-10-31); "Keep thread panel open" follows them and is not a look option (see "The thread panel view"). "Keep starred above the list" is the last item of the View group above them (see "Above the list, as pills" under "Pinned threads" in `inbox-messages.md`). Each is a plain checkbox with no icon (the owner, 2026-11-02: "those could all just be check boxes anyways. They don't look right as icons"; `MenuCheckbox` in `components/Tidy.tsx`, as are "Keep thread panel open" and "Hide offline machines"): a `Menu.CheckboxItem` (`role="menuitemcheckbox"`, `aria-checked`, `data-list-look`, `closeOnClick={false}`) whose box (`.ib-pmi-box`, ticked and filled with the accent while on) sits where Tidy's and Refresh's icons sit, so every label starts at one edge, so the menu stays open and the list changes at once. "Customize rows…" (`data-customize-rows`, no icon, its words on the labels' edge) ends the Rows group and opens Settings → Rows. Smaller thread names is on by default (the owner, 2026-11-05: "I feel like we should make it the default"; core `INBOX_ROWS_DEFAULT`, `LIST_LOOK_DEFAULT`); the other three are off. The look is the person's, wherever they use the Inbox (the owner, 2026-11-02: "Whatever they set goes wherever they're using the app"): config.json `inboxRows` (`environment.md`) through `POST /api/inbox/settings` `{ rows }`, the whole look as the names that are on. A change shows at once, then is saved; every open window, a phone's too, takes a save at once (`event: rows`, `adoptSavedRows`), and a stream that went silent reads the settings again. One save is on its way at a time; a change meanwhile waits, only the latest, and goes when it answers. Every answer, settings read and event carries the server's save count (`rows_rev`, config.json `inboxRowsRev`): one older than the newest this page heard is dropped (unless the count went back, config.json reset or hand-edited: a save's answer at or below the count heard when it was sent, or a read below it, and the page counts again from there; an event at or below the last event's count, the count gone back or a late copy relayed by a second leader tab, is never taken and asks for a settings read), one that arrives while this page has a save to make waits, and once every save answered the page shows the newest look, whichever window made it. A choice that turns an option on turns its rival off (Compact and Roomy, "2 lines" and no preview; the Dot turns the smaller names on, and turning them off turns the Dot off), in the menu and in Settings alike. The one-time list dialog's Done saves the whole look picked in it, as a Settings → Rows preset does (`writeListLookOptions`). The cookie `plannotator-inbox-rows` keeps the last look this browser showed (the names, or "off"), so a reload paints it before the settings read answers; it is never the truth. The four per-option cookies of the first version (`plannotator-inbox-list-highlight`, `-compact`, `-hide-agents`, `-small-names`, "1" while on) are read, never written: while the server has no `inboxRows` (GET answers `rows: null`), the first window saves its browser's look once if it is not the defaults, and from then on the server's look wins. **The new defaults and old looks (2026-11-05).** Before smaller names became a default, every option was off by default, and a window saved the look whenever it was touched (Balanced included), so a kept look of every option off was never a choice. Such a look reads as today's defaults wherever it is kept: config.json `inboxRows: []` with no `inboxRowsDefaults` (the server answers `["smallNames"]`, core `inboxRowsSaved`), the cookie's old "none", and no old per-option cookie on. Any other old look was chosen and stays exactly as it was, smaller names off included (a Compact look saved before keeps the larger names). Every save since writes `inboxRowsDefaults: 2` beside `inboxRows`, so every option off chosen today stays off, and the cookie says "off" for it, which an older build reads as every option off too. The column carries `.ib-lk-pill`, `.ib-lk-compact`, `.ib-lk-noagents`, `.ib-lk-small` only while an option is on, and every rule needs its class. No shortcut. The onboarding tour ignores the saved options (`InboxList`'s `rememberLook={false}`, as its sidebar ignores the saved sections), so it always shows the defaults.
  - Highlight thread subject (`.ib-lk-pill`, `RowTitle`, `TitleCell`'s `pill`): the title sits in the thread chip's pill (`.ib-key.ib-rowkey`, the open thread's pill: face, fill, radius) at the title's own size, one line cut with "…" instead of wrapping. A read row's title takes the pill's weight (500) and grey; an unread row keeps its 600 and bigger size, a Sent or Quiet row its quieter colour (`:where(.ib-lk-pill)` keeps the pill's text rule less specific than theirs) (its tooltip shows it whole while cut, not on a phone). On every shape: three-line, phone and wide rows.
  - Compact rows (`.ib-lk-compact`): a three-line or phone row has two lines. Line 1 is the agents' tiles, the project, the title and the time; line 2 the preview, the badge and the count. Rows are about 20 px shorter (62 px beside a thread, 70 px on a phone; the unrendered rows' `contain-intrinsic-size` estimates are their content boxes, 43 px and 47 px). The project (with its tiles) gives way first, down to min(its width, `--ib-lk-floor`: 136 px, 72 px on a phone 360 px wide or less so a long title keeps about ten characters), then the title is cut with "…" (its tooltip shows it whole, not on a phone); the time never is. Beside a thread, a long project next to a long title cuts the title where the three-line row showed it whole (accepted in the approval). A wide row is one line already and does not change.
  - Hide agent icons (`.ib-lk-noagents`): no agents' tiles or marks on any row, same row height; the run's words stay for a screen reader (visually hidden, `.ib-sr`'s technique). In one project's list, where the agents were the whole first cell, that cell goes and a wide row's title takes its column.
  - Smaller thread names (`.ib-lk-small`, CSS only): an unread row's name at the read size, still 600, the look before "inbox-row-unread-bigger" (13.5 px beside a thread, 14 px on a phone, no tightened tracking), in its pill too. Read and Sent rows and the wide one-line row are the same either way.
  - **Settings → Rows** (approval "inbox-customize-rows", 2026-11-02, `.product/approved/inbox-customize-rows/`; `components/RowsSettings.tsx`, `#settings/rows`, second after General): the whole look with no code. "Start from" Minimal (Thread name first, no preview, smaller names: the two-line row; approval "inbox-rows-dialog") / Balanced (the defaults: smaller names on, every other option off; also the way back) / Detailed (Roomy, 2 lines, letter marks Both as Avatar with Logo from the repo, no subject pill (pending owner answers q-70c1a472 and q-737e5fd4); the larger unread names), as buttons (`aria-pressed`, not radios: an arrow key moves without choosing, so a stray key never wipes a Custom look), and a "Custom" tag when the look matches none (the group's `aria-describedby`). Look: Density Compact / Comfortable / Roomy (`compact`, `roomy`: never both), First line Project / Thread name (`titleFirst`: on three-line and phone rows the name and the time on line 1, the agents and project under it; compact keeps one line, the name first; with Grey line under the name None too, the row is two lines: the guided review mark, pin, badge, snooze return and count move from line 3 to line 2's right end, after the agents and project, which give way with "…" first (unrendered row estimates: 66px beside a thread, 76px roomy; a phone row's content box 43px, 46px roomy), except with Time position "Under the preview", whose own line holds them; the full-width row keeps its columns), Thread names Plain / Highlighted (`highlight`), Unread Larger / Bold (the default) / Dot (`smallNames`, plus `unreadDot`: a 6 px accent dot before an unread row, the row's `::after`, so its `::before` stays the hover and selection pill and the focus ring), Grey line under the name None / 1 line / 2 lines (lines of the latest message; `hidePreview`, `previewTwo`: never both; a wide row stays one line), Time Clock / Relative (`relativeTime`: "now", "4m", "3h", "2d", then the date; a screen reader hears "4 minutes ago" (`relativeWords`) and the pointer's tooltip is the clock time; `relativeTime.ts`, every row on one shared minute timer that runs only while a row shows it). Show: Agent icons, Project ("With its machine", its words the switch's `aria-describedby`; `hideProject` gives a wide row one project's columns). Each choice is one save, its rival turned off with it. The time, the questions and action items, and the Stopped and Holds-up warnings always show; no option hides them (with Status mark on, the counts become one mark, their numbers in its tooltip and its name for a screen reader). The page's words are as few as the owner asked (2026-11-07): a lede saying a change applies everywhere, a description only where the pictures cannot show it (Project's machine, Mark style's side effect, where a picture comes from), and one note under the pictures ("Full width keeps one line. Text size is in General."). Two live pictures drawn by the list's own `ListRow` with three fixed sample threads: "With a thread open" (the 380 px column) beside the controls, and "Full width" under them, drawn at 900 px and zoomed down when the page is narrower. To show it at real size the Rows page alone widens into the right margin (up to 240 px, `.ib-spg[data-settings-section="rows"]`). A phone shows one picture, "On this phone". The phone list of sections shows the preset's name or "Custom". No shortcut.
  - **Letter marks** (approval "inbox-letter-marks", 2026-11-07, `.product/approved/inbox-letter-marks/`; `packages/inbox/letterMarks.tsx`, `projectPictures.ts`): only in Settings → Rows, all off by default, so with them off the Inbox looks exactly as before. Look → Mark style Inline / Avatar / Dot (`markInline`, `markDot`, neither is Avatar; nothing picked while marks are off, "Turns letter marks on", and a pick turns thread marks on). Show, after Agent icons and Project: Letter marks Off / Threads / Projects / Both (`marksThreads`, `marksProjects`), then the switches "Logo from the repo" (`repoLogo`) and "GitHub pictures" (`githubPictures`); a picture switch turned on with no project marks turns them on (Off becomes Projects, Threads becomes Both). Turning marks off drops the style and the switches (core `inboxRowsCanonical`), so they start again as Avatar with no pictures. Detailed turns marks on (Both, Avatar, Logo from the repo; GitHub pictures off); Minimal and Balanced keep them off. A thread's mark is the first and last letter of its title by grapheme (emoji and punctuation skipped; one glyph for Han, kana, Hangul, Arabic, Devanagari and other scripts whose letters do not stand alone at 20 px), coloured by one of eight hues from the thread id (a project's: its id), turned from the theme's `--primary` and mixed in oklab. Inline: a soft 20 px tile before the title and a 16 px one before the project, in rows, the sidebar and Board cards. Avatar: one solid mark per row in its own column (24 px wide, 30 px on three-line and phone rows), the project's on its corner; the sidebar's 20 px project marks; the bar's title leads with the pair. Dot: an 8 px dot in the thread's hue before the title, the letters in the thread (30 px over the subject), the sidebar and Board cards. A project's mark is its picture when it has one (`/api/inbox/projects/:id/picture`, server-and-storage.md: Set picture… in the project's ⋯ menu, this machine's projects, only while project marks show, then the repo's logo, then the GitHub owner's picture), else its letters; the picture covers the letters once loaded, so a miss shows letters. Every mark is `aria-hidden`: the name is always in text beside it. Agent icons off hides the agents' tiles in every style and also takes the agent's mark off the row's hover card (`RowMark`). The tour draws none (`RowMarksScope look={null}`). Proof: `packages/inbox/letterMarks.test.ts` (the letter rule, the hue, the look's rules and its round trip through core), `letterMarksRows.test.tsx` (DOM: off by default on every shape, each style's place, `aria-hidden`, Agent icons off and the hover card, the tour), `rowsSettings.test.tsx` (the controls and presets) and `packages/server/inbox-project-pictures.test.ts` (the picture order, the repo logo's places and its fences, GitHub once per owner with a scripted fetch, the week's refresh and its fallbacks).
  - **Notes-list look** (approval "inbox-customize-rows", addendum 2026-11-07, from the owner's notes-list picture; `RowsSettings.tsx`, `components/RowStatus.tsx`): four options, all off by default and in no preset (presets later), so with them off every row draws as before. The owner's word rule for this page (2026-11-07): only words a control's picture does not already say. Look → Time position Top right / Under the preview (`timeBelow`: on three-line and phone rows, compact ones too, which gain the line, the time leaves line 1 for a line of its own under the preview, at the left, `.ib-l4` / `.ib-m4`, and the row's end, pin, badge, words and count or status mark, moves to its right so the preview keeps its width; a wide row keeps its columns). Show, after Agent icons and Project, each a switch: Dividers (`dividers`, no description: a 1 px line in the theme's `--border` between two rows, inset to the text, drawn as the next row's background image so nothing moves and the hover pill never touches it; never a border or outline on a row), Status mark (`statusMark`, "Instead of counts": one 16 px mark in place of the action pill and `?N` on every shape. Waiting on you, a ring half filled in the warning hue, while a question or action item is open, whatever the section; Done, a ring with a tick in the success hue, in Quiet or archived; nothing in New and Sent. Its name and tooltip keep the count, "Waiting on you: 2 questions, 1 action item", and a Stopped or Holds up row keeps its filled question mark (or action box) before the status mark. On a done row the tick stands alone: no "Archived", "Read" or "👍 Looks good" on screen, though its name keeps the reason, "Done, read"; any other words stay. Done wins over an open question in Quiet (a Mark read put it there), and Sent, a Re-ask note's included, has no mark. With the time under the preview the row's hover action sits on the time's line, over the row's end, not over the name) and Bold names (`boldNames`, no description: every row's name at 600, read or not, Sent and Quiet keeping their colour). The Rows page's quiet sample thread shows Done. Separately, a name-first row whose second line draws nothing (Agent icons off and no project: Project off or one project's list) folds that line out of the flow (`data-lead-empty`, kept for a screen reader as `.ib-sr`), so the preview sits under the name; a row with the agents or the project shown never has it, and its markup is the same as before. Proof: `packages/inbox/rowsNotesLook.test.tsx` (DOM: off on every shape, the time's line and the row's end, a wide row untouched, the mark by section and its words, the tick alone, the filled marks kept, no rival, no preset, the dividers' rules never a border, each row's lines unchanged in the defaults, every preset and name first with agents or project shown, the folded line only with both off) and `rowsSettings.test.tsx` (the controls, one save each, the pictures, the phone's).
  - **The Rows page, rebuilt** (approval "inbox-rows-settings-page", 2026-11-10, `.product/approved/inbox-rows-settings-page/`; `components/RowsSettings.tsx`, control model `rowsControls.ts`). It replaces the flat Look / Show form above; the stored options are the same names, plus one.
    - **Your list**: the person's own six newest rows (App `rowsPagePreview`), drawn by `ListRow`; labelled examples when there are no threads. It stays in view: sticky beside the controls on a wide page; pinned at the top on a phone, two rows tall, and the two rows are the newest that show the last change (`rowShows`).
    - **Start from**: Balanced (the defaults), Dense (new: `compact`, `smallNames`, `relativeTime`, `dividers`, `statusMark`), Minimal and Detailed (their lists unchanged). Buttons, not radios; the picked one is filled like Settings' own nav, and a look that matches none has none pressed (no "Custom" tag; the phone's Settings list still says "Custom"). The one-time dialog keeps its three (`ROWS_INTRO_PRESETS`).
    - **Choices**: eight first (Spacing, Grey line under the name, First on the row, Time, When a thread needs you, Agent icons, Project name, Pull requests) and nine under "More choices" (New threads, Every thread name in bold, Thread name in a grey pill, Lines between rows, Where the time sits, Letter badges, Badge shape, Repo logo on project badges, GitHub pictures on project badges). Each control names the options it owns; a choice is a change given to `lookWithChange`, so the rival rules are still core's.
    - **Pull requests** (`hidePulls`, new, off by default): on, no row draws its PR mark (`ListRow` `noPulls`, every shape). The thread's own PR button stays. A look saved before it reads as shown.
    - **The change line, Undo, Reset**: each change says "Changed: Spacing → Compact" beside the list's title (a polite live region) with Undo, and tints the part of the rows that moved (`data-rows-flash`; a fill that fades, none under reduced motion). Undo, in the header too, steps back through this visit's changes, one save each; nothing about it is stored. Reset to default saves the defaults.
    - **Pictures on then off**: a picture switch turned on while no project badges showed turns them on (`lookWithChange`, as before). On this page, turning the pictures off again puts project badges back off, until the person picks the badges themselves. Page state only: a saved look reads as it always did.
    - **No migration, no write on open**: the page reads the look and saves only on a press. Proof: `packages/inbox/rowsControls.test.ts` (all 774,144 distinct stored looks read into the controls and write back the identical list; every option reachable on and off; a look without `hidePulls` shows PRs; the presets' exact lists) and `packages/inbox/rowsSettings.test.tsx` (DOM: zero saves on open for several saved looks; each choice one save; Undo, Reset, the live region, the pictures rule, the PR mark and its tint).
  - **Tell your agent** (approval "inbox-rows-settings-page"; `components/RowsAsk.tsx`, core `inbox-rows-words.ts`, `apps/hook/server/inbox-rows-command.ts`). The Inbox has no Pi server, so this is Bun only.
    - **The words live in one file.** `packages/core/inbox-rows-words.ts` holds every word an agent reads about rows: the instruction block (`INBOX_ROWS_ASK_BLOCK`), the setting names and values (`INBOX_ROWS_SETTINGS`, `INBOX_ROWS_LOOKS`), every line the commands print (`INBOX_ROWS_WORDS`) and their help. They are the owner's words: change them only with the owner. No tool text carries any of it.
    - **The ask box**: one quiet line under "Your list" ("Or tell your agent how you want your rows…"); the agent picker (only with more than one session) and Send show once it has focus; no example chips. Send is the New message Compose sends, to a live session on this computer, marked `rows_ask`, so what the agent receives carries the block after the person's words (the same path as `decision_ask`). The person stays on the page: "Sent to <agent>. Open thread", then the agent's newest line (the thread is read every 2.5 s for five minutes). With no agent running: "No agent is running" and Copy (the person's words only). Nothing starts an agent.
    - **The command**: `plannotator inbox rows` (every option, what it changes, its value now; `--json`), `rows set <name>=<value> ...` (one save; `look=` is applied first; any refused part saves nothing; prints before -> after in the page's words) and `rows undo`. A thin client of `GET`/`POST /api/inbox/settings`: it sends `rows`, `rows_by` and `rows_undo` and nothing else, so it reaches no other setting. `rows_by` is the session the shell names (`inboxHostSessionFromEnv`), when it names one.
    - **Who changed it, and Undo**: every rows save keeps the look before it and who made it (config.json `inboxRowsPrev`, `inboxRowsBy`), and `event: rows` carries both. A window on Settings → Rows that hears a save with a `by` says "<agent> changed: Spacing → Compact · Undo" (`listLook.ts` `onAgentRowsChange`); Undo, there and in the header, saves the look from before as the person's own save. No confirmation gate. The line shows for a change that arrives while the page is open: after a reload the list simply has the new look, and `plannotator inbox rows undo` (or Reset, or the choices) still puts it back.
    - Proof: `packages/inbox/rowsWords.test.ts` (each agent value saves exactly what the same click saves; `look=` is "Start from"), `apps/hook/server/inbox-rows-command.test.ts` (rows only, with who; refusals save nothing), `packages/inbox/rowsAsk.test.tsx` (DOM: the box at rest, Send, retry, no agent, the agent-changed line and its Undo), `packages/server/inbox.test.ts` (prev, by, the event, undo).
  - Proof: `packages/inbox/listLook.test.tsx` (DOM: the saved look, its adoption from old cookies and the server, one save at a time and the save count deciding out-of-order answers, rivals, the dialog's four options, the menu's checkboxes and "Customize rows…", name first, every option keeping the time and the count, each mode's rows on each shape, smaller names changing only the column's class, the tour ignoring saved options) and `tests/e2e/inbox-list-options.spec.ts` (the compiled binary: each option from the ⋮ menu by mouse and keyboard, the row's change, a reload keeps it; compact geometry beside a thread, wide, on a phone and on a 320 px phone with worst-case names; hidden agents still announced in the accessibility tree; an unread name's size and weight with smaller names on and off, beside a thread, on a phone and wide; Settings → Rows: a choice reaching another window at once, the menu's checkboxes on one edge, the Rows options' rows on 390 and 320 px phones; the Dot keeping the focus ring and the hover pill), `packages/inbox/rowsSettings.test.tsx` (presets as buttons an arrow key never applies, "Custom" and the Project words tied for a screen reader, the three-way choices, the pictures), `packages/inbox/relativeTime.test.tsx` (the words, one shared timer) and `packages/server/inbox.test.ts` (`inboxRows`' round trip and `event: rows`; an old look of every option off read as today's defaults, any other old look kept). `tests/e2e/inbox-row-unread-size.spec.ts` saves every option off first: it proves Unread "Larger".
  - **The one-time dialog, "Choose how your list looks"** (approval "inbox-rows-dialog", round 7b, 2026-11-07, `.product/approved/inbox-rows-dialog/`; `components/ListLookIntro.tsx`, `components/RowsIntroPicture.tsx`, `listIntro.ts`; it replaced the four switches of approval "inbox-list-options-intro", shipped in 0.28.42). The `Modal`, 720 px (`.ib-lo.ib-rl`). On top, a picture of an Inbox with example projects (bundled logos, `assets/rows-intro/`: Plannotator's own and eight well-known projects; caption "Example projects"): the sidebar open beside the list, no agent icons, drawn 620 px wide and scaled to the frame, following the window's mode (the owner, q-fd707b9e). Under it "Choose how your list looks", "Pick a look. Fine-tune it any time in Customize rows, from ⋮ above the list." ("Customize rows" opens Settings → Rows and saves nothing), then three looks as radios, Minimal, Balanced and Detailed (`ROWS_PRESETS`, each its name alone, centred, with "Current" under it that never moves the name), and "Keep as it is" and "Done". The look the person has now is picked when it opens and reads "Current" (`rowsPreset`; Balanced, the defaults, for most: the owner, "balance should be selected by default"), so Done at once saves the same look; a custom look picks none and Done waits for a pick. Once the demo holds, the picked look's card rises (below). Picking a look (a light primary tint, no border) raises a card (at most 372 × 320, the frame's middle) over the picture, which blurs (5 px), dims and steps back: the card's header "Inbox" and the person's own first seven rows (Quiet left out) drawn by `ListRow` in that look inside `RowMarksScope`, cut at the bottom with a fade; the caption turns to "Your list". A change of look crossfades the rows (out 110 ms, in 200 ms after 70 ms); the card rises 18 px from 0.965 in 360 ms. Done (disabled only while nothing is picked, a custom look) saves the picked look whole (`writeListLookOptions`, as Settings → Rows' preset buttons); Keep as it is and Escape save nothing. Focus starts on the first look. A phone: the bottom sheet, the same window smaller, the three looks in one row (sketch above the name, 84 px), Done and Keep as it is full width.
    - The demo (`rowsIntroScene`, `ROWS_INTRO_STEPS_MS`): letter marks pop into the rows and the sidebar at 0.3 s (55 ms apart), the logos land at 0.9 s, the row marks turn over to the projects at 1.5 s, back to the stacked look (the thread's letters, the project's logo on its corner) at 2.2 s as a notes-look card (two invented threads, a status mark each) rises 14 px in at the picture's bottom right, and from 2.6 s it holds there: no reset. The looks are never locked: any key, click or touch (window `keydown` / `pointerdown`, capture; a key synchronously) jumps to the held picture with no motion, and a tap on a look picks it in the same tap. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` it opens on the held picture and every change is instant.
    - The light picture (`lightScope`) is no longer this dialog's; ListViewIntro and VoiceIntro still draw one: `ib-lightscope theme-<the person's light palette> light` on their frame (Plannotator's palette if theirs has no light half), so the palette's light tokens apply there and the window's derived tokens are worked out again inside it: each of `inbox.css`'s token blocks names `.ib-lightscope` beside `.pn-inbox`, and each light block (`:root.light .pn-inbox`) names `.ib-lightscope.light`. A new token block, or a new `:root.light` rule for something such a picture draws, names the scope too.
    - Who and when: the Inbox decides who (config.json `inboxListIntro`, see `environment.md`: due once per version for everyone, fresh installs too, but never for someone who already picked a look (`listIntroLookPicked`: config.json `inboxRows` saved, with its save count; the owner, 2026-11-09: "Yes, skip it for them"; read live, so a pick before its moment drops it and it stops holding the dialogs behind it, as the tab icon's favicon does); decided at the first release start of the version: `packages/server/inbox-list-intro.ts`, `LIST_INTRO_VERSION` 2; the line's entry needs five threads, `minThreads`). The page decides when (`listIntroWanted` / `listIntroMayAsk`): due, a page on this computer, the list loaded with five threads or more (`LIST_INTRO_MIN_ROWS`; q-2c590791: a decent, growing list, no day count), not first run; the list (its rows, first run) and the page (this computer) are read once when the first list and settings are in (a list that grows later never brings it up mid-session), while `due` follows the line (the load's latch, below: a tab left loaded since before the line was free arms it once the Inbox names it); never on a load that showed the sound or agent-note dialog or the tour; it asks after both are decided, only once the Inbox page has been settled for `LIST_INTRO_SETTLED_MS` (2.5 s, about the wait a first load has anyway) without a break (`listIntroPageClear`: the Inbox page, with or without a thread open, so a load on a thread link, a notification or a reload shows it over the thread, on a phone and a desktop alike (the owner, 2026-10-31: "it should show if I have a thread open"); never while a file, document, guided review or Artifacts view is open on the thread, side by side or full screen (`route.file`, `guide`, `artifacts`: the claim waits, unspent, until it closes); never Settings, the Board, What's new, Drafts or the tour, and no search or new message over it), with no update dialog and nothing over the page or under way (`listIntroHeldBack`, checked every 700 ms): no other dialog (`modalOpen`), no modal layer ui draws on <body> (`[role="dialog"][aria-modal="true"]`), no image opened full size (`[data-image-lightbox]`), no pinned reference card (`[data-ref-card-pinned]`), no reply being typed, and no reply box left open with words or files in it, wherever focus is (the thread reports it: `unsent.ts` `replyBeingWritten`). Opening another thread, project or machine starts the wait again, so it never lands the moment a page changes. Then it claims it (`POST /api/inbox/list-intro`): only the first claim ever shows it, so two tabs never both do. A claim answered after the person opened Settings, the Board, a file, a guided review, the Artifacts view, a search, a new message or a reply opens once they are back on the Inbox page for that wait with nothing open or typed (it is spent, so never dropped). The notification lines wait for this load's answer and stand down while it is open. No keyboard shortcut besides Escape.
    - Tests: `packages/inbox/listIntro.test.ts` (the gate, a thread open included, a file, document, guided review or Artifacts view excluded; the preview covering every option that is off, never one already on), `packages/inbox/listIntroDialog.test.tsx` (DOM: the preview, smaller names staying on through it at the defaults, inert switches, a key ending it, reduced motion, Done, Escape, the Tab cycle, options already set, the picture as the only light scope), `packages/server/inbox-list-intro.test.ts` (the claim: due only for an earlier user on a release build, one claim of two at once, 409 and 405, a claim that cannot be saved shows nothing), `tests/e2e/inbox-list-intro.spec.ts` (a compiled release binary: once in two tabs, never with the tour or the sound dialog, new users never, Done on the real list across a reload, Escape and Keep, a key or click during the preview, reduced motion, the phone sheet, a first load on the list after the wait, a first load on a thread link, desktop and phone: over the thread after the wait with the picture's rows drawn, never while a reply is typed there or left with words in its box, never while a file is open beside it and after the wait once it is closed, never under an image opened full size or a modal layer, another thread opened just before the wait ends starting it again; the dialog in the window's mode with its text and switches at WCAG AA in dark and light (desktop and a dark phone), the picture light in both (and under Synthwave '84) and the only light scope on the page, never a second `.pn-inbox`; the picture's names on one line, a long one cut with "…"; Smaller thread names on from the open, and turned off by Done).
  - **The one-time Snooze dialog** (approval "inbox-snooze-announce", 2026-11-02, direction B "The real menu", `.product/approved/inbox-snooze-announce/`; `components/SnoozeIntro.tsx`, the page gate in `snoozeIntro.ts`, Try it in `snoozeIntroTry.ts`, the row rules in `snoozeIntroRows.ts`, who gets it in `packages/server/inbox-snooze-intro.ts`; neither is a core export, so the published UI package stays as it is). It tells people who have used the Inbox for a while, and never snoozed anything, that Snooze exists.
    - The picture is static, inert and has no animation. Off a phone (520 px dialog): two real narrow `ListRow`s, with the thread just left as the last row and in its menu-open look. Beside them is the real "‹ Snooze until" menu with its real times, a pointer on Tomorrow, and below it the real "Snoozed · <time> · Undo" toast. On a phone (the bottom sheet): the ⋯ sheet's times with Later today lit, drawn with compact rows. Then a quiet "New" line in the accent colour (its own element, before the title; the dialog's name is the title), the title "Snooze a thread until later", the body ("Pick later today, tomorrow, in 3 days, next week, or a time you choose. The thread waits in Snoozed and comes back on its own. Undo puts it back at once."; the owner's wording, q-8e89fe57 B), and a hint: "Right-click a thread, or click the clock at the top of an open thread." on a computer, "In a thread, tap ⋯ and pick **Snooze**." on a phone. The buttons are **Got it** and **Try it** (primary, first focus); on a phone they are stacked, 44 px tall.
    - Who: decided by the server (config.json `inboxSnoozeIntro`, see `environment.md`). It must be a release build, first use at least 2 days ago (`SNOOZE_INTRO_TENURE_MS`), never snoozed, and not shown yet. There is no rule about active days. It is the first entry of the reveals' queue (below): it is due only while it is `reveal.next`, so it also waits out the one-day spacing, and its claim stamps the spacing clock.
    - Where: on this computer and on the owner's phone (the owner, q-e29f8cd6); on a tagged machine, a login the owner allowed counts as the owner. A tailnet guest never sees it: the server reports it not due for a guest and refuses a guest's claim.
    - When (the window, `useSnoozeIntro`; the times are in `SNOOZE_INTRO_TIMES`): right after the person leaves a thread that still waits on them, after at least 5 s with it open, without answering and with no words left in its box. That thread is checked again before the claim and before the opening (a reply draft saves 500 ms after the last key, so the list can learn of it after the leave): gone, answered or holding a draft drops the moment, or, once claimed, spends it. All of these must also hold:
      - at least 2 rows have been read and left for 24 h or more (`snoozeIntroNeed`), and the list has at least 3 rows;
      - the page loaded at least 60 s ago;
      - no tour, update, sound, agent-notes, list or voice dialog is due or open, and the Board intro was not shown on this load (`noteOneTimeDialogShown`);
      - nothing is typed or open, no menu or toast is on screen, and the list has not changed for 2.5 s.
      The person's own snooze on this load cancels the dialog. If the moment passes without these, the dialog waits for the next one.
    - The claim and the dismiss: one compare-and-set claim (`POST /api/inbox/snooze-intro`) stamps `shownAt` before anything is drawn, so a second tab never shows it. Every way out is final: Got it, Escape, a click outside, or a reload.
