  - Nothing matches: "No threads match" and Clear filters in the list's body (`ListFilterEmpty`), focus back on the funnel.
  - Remembered per browser in the cookie `plannotator-inbox-list-filter` through `storage` (JSON; removed when nothing is picked; anything unreadable is no filter), never on the server. No shortcut.
  - Proof: `packages/inbox/listFilter.test.ts` (the rule as a table: groups, the day spans at midnight and the 7th and 30th day back, ranges, agents, a project's page, the groups, the menu's counts, the cookie, each Pull request pick against the server's row summary, a PR not read yet, Not seen alone and with other groups, a row held for it, an older cookie) and `packages/inbox/listFilterButton.test.tsx` (DOM: the funnel alone, the colour and count, picks narrowing the rows and bands, Clear filters, "No threads match" in the body, a project's page, a reload, the menu's order, a Pull request pick, no group while no thread has a PR, Not seen first, its count, on and off).
- The Soft look (`.product/approved/inbox-soft/`, approved 2026-10-16; not in this repo) modernizes today's window and does not relayout it (owner, 2026-10-16): densities, row and control heights, columns and places stay today's, and a border Soft removes stays as transparent width. The owner's rulings (recorded in the approval): the two blind rounds decided COLOURS only (2026-10-16): today's ground, selected row, row separators and lines, and section band in both modes; Soft's Stopped / Holds up badges (tinted pills), fewer accents (links, Reply, a tile's Open) and question card surface in both; the sidebar's current item and quiet text today's in light and Soft's in dark. Everything that is not a colour is Soft by default (2026-10-17: "I want the soft look to be its own thing"), at today's spacing and row pitch. Each part is one switch at the top of `inbox.css`: the colours `--ib-soft-ground` (Soft's tinted ground; off), `-selection`, `-rows`, `-band` (off), `-badges`, `-accents`, `-card` (on), `-nav`, `-quiet` (on in dark only); `-rows` was today's in the study and is on in both modes since the owner asked for the prototype's rows with no line between them (2026-10-17; only the rows: every other line keeps today's colour, its faint fill a switch of its own, `--ib-soft-lines`, off); and the non-colour parts, all on: `-panes` (rounded floating panes, 8 px gutters, their shadow, no rules between panes), `-labels` (small labels in sentence case at 12 px), `-shape` (radii), `-float` (floating surfaces' shadow, no outline), `-hover` (hover fills and fades), `-chip` (the search field's key hint and the thread chip as grey pills), `-hbtns`, `-foot`, `-accept`, `-surfaces`, `-buttons`, `-segmented` and `-groups`. The type's size is not a Soft switch (the `--ib-soft-type` 13 px switch it replaced is gone): it is the person's Text size setting (see "Text size" under the window's Settings). Layout corners are one scale tuned in one place (`--ib-lr-pane` 10, `-xl` 12, `-lg` 10, `-md` 9, `-sm` 8, `-xs` 7; owner, 2026-10-17: a subtle step smaller, layout only): panes 10 (was 14), dialogs, the search palette, compose and the question card 12 (16), and a phone's bottom sheets' top corners 12 (16; the dialog corner, owner's ruling q8, one token `--ib-sheet-corner`, which ui's `ImagePeek` and review's `MarkdownViewMenu` sheets match with `rounded-t-[12px]`), tiles, Settings' groups, the header's attachment band, the route chooser, popovers and menus 10 (12 to 14), the read line and callouts 9 (12), row pills, section bands, the folded line, the step box, sidebar, Settings-nav and search rows and the question card's options 8 (10), a tile's icon, menu items and picker options 7 (8 to 11); buttons, chips, badges and inputs keep their radii (`--ib-rN-s`). With every switch `initial` the window is today's, pixel for pixel; a space is Soft, `initial` today's, set per mode in the `:root.light` block), by the space-toggle: a token built as `var(--ib-soft-x) <Soft value>` is invalid while its switch is off, and `var(token, today's value)` falls back (for ui's own drawing, to ui's own values, named: `revert-layer` in a `var()` fallback lays the thread out differently in WebKit on Linux). The panes float on today's ground with the ground switch off: the palette's `--background`, the colour today's sidebar paints, with the panes on `--card`; in the palettes whose background is their card's colour (under 1.035:1: synthwave-84, terminal, and neutral, simple and caffeine in light) the ground is `--muted` mixed into `--background` (`--ib-ground-panes`), and `packages/inbox/softGround.test.ts` fails when a palette is added with no ground to float on. The sidebar's control fills and hovers follow the ground switch (card fills on Soft's tint, today's band and muted hovers on today's ground). The panes switch turns on the 8 px gutters; the window's measures read them from `--ib-gutter` at run time (`paneGutters` in `stack.ts`), so that switch alone moves the stacking widths. Every Soft colour, shadow and surface derives from the palette's theme tokens (no colour literals; Soft's dark tinted ground (the ground switch) and the shadows take `--background` toward black in its own hue through relative colours, and a separation floor keeps grounds and raised surfaces visible where a palette sets `--muted` to `--card`); a palette may replace the derived values with `--inbox-ground`, `--inbox-raise`, `--inbox-shadow-ink`, `--inbox-pane-shadow` and `--inbox-float-shadow`. Text on a raised Soft surface follows the quiet-text switch: stepped toward black on a light surface and white on a dark one (`--ib-card-ink`, `--ib-raise-*`, behind `@supports`) where it is on, else the palette's own text with its quiet text moved as far as the surface moved from the card (`--ib-raise-mfg-keep`), so a raised surface never costs today's contrast. Agent marks are on one size scale (the owner's design-system ruling q12, 2026-10-22): 16, 20, 24, 30 and 44 px, corners 16 → 5, 20 → 6, 24 → 6, 30 → 9 (8 with `--ib-soft-shape` off), 44 → 10, declared once in inbox.css as `--ib-mark-N` / `-r` (corner) / `-g` (the glyph in a tile) and used by every rule that sizes a mark (a bare 16 in list rows, menus and pickers, and the Copy prompt pill; the 30 tile in Settings, Settings → Agents, a message and Drafts; 24 in a message's head row and the @ picker; 20 in the first-run lists, the agent-note dialog and the tag stack, now square; 44 the first-run card, 30 on a phone). A width from one step takes that step's corner. `packages/inbox/agentMarkScale.test.ts` fails on a mark sized any other way, or a width and corner from different steps. No other size. Not agent marks of the scale: the in-text tag mark (`.ib-tg-mark`) drawn over a sent reply's `@`, which follows the `@` glyph (10 to 14 px, `tagHighlight.ts`; the test exempts it), and the harness picker's tab glyphs (`.ib-tglyph`, 14 px: icons in a tab label, like the Settings nav's, not agent marks; the reference's mark table does not list them). Of the non-colour switches (all on): buttons lose their outlines for quiet fills at a 10 px radius (`--ib-soft-buttons`; pills in the thread's foot, `--ib-soft-foot`), the Light / Dark / System control is a faint well (`--ib-soft-segmented`), dialogs, the search palette and compose float with a 12 px radius (`--ib-lr-xl`) and `--ib-float-shadow` (`--ib-soft-float`), small labels (dialogs' headings, Settings' eyebrow, setup headings, the search palette's groups) are 12 px sentence case (`--ib-soft-labels`), Settings' groups are raised cards (`--ib-soft-groups`), and Settings' current section follows the sidebar's current-item switch (`--ib-soft-nav`). On a phone, buttons keep 9 px and the annotations list and New message's sessions stay bottom sheets in both looks. Every declaration falls back to today's value (`var(<switch token>, <today's>)`), so the window with every switch at `initial` is today's pixel for pixel at 1440 px, light and dark, list, thread, file, Settings, search, compose and a phone (proved by screenshot diffs against main), except two named exceptions: the question card's decision row, dropped by a ruling of its own and since gone with Decisions, and the agent marks, which follow the q12 size scale (below) in both looks (the 22 and 36 px marks, the tag stack's 18 px circles and the agent-note dialog's 18 px glyph changed with Soft off too).
- Held order (`packages/inbox/held.ts`, `packages/inbox/settle.ts`; design record "inbox-open-stays-put", variant B, approved 2026-10-12, not in this repo): while any thread is open, the list's rows do not move. Opening a row records the look and unbolds that row where it is (it does not re-sort or leave its section, so focus and the selection stay on it); opening another row while one is open moves nothing; the person's own writes (a pick, a Send, Mark read, Archive) show in place (`refreshHeldRows`, owner ruling 2026-10-11; an archived row shows "Archived" in place through `markHeldRowArchived`, since the window's list leaves archived threads out). Sidebar counts stay live. The list settles (`settleOwn`: the person's own reads and actions take their places) when the thread closes: a thread archived while it was open leaves the list then, and one Mark read sent back to the archive leaves too, while an agent message that brings a thread back from the archive waits behind "N new" like any agent activity; it adopts everything when the folder changes, Settings or What's new open, or Show is pressed. New agent activity waits behind "N new in <project> · Show" (one line at every width, the owner, 2026-11-02: the count and Show never shrink and a long project name is cut with "…", the whole line then in a tooltip; `HeldNoticeLine` in `InboxList.tsx`, `heldNoticeLine.test.tsx`, `tests/e2e/inbox-held-notice-line.spec.ts`) and stays there after a close (owner's decision 4: closing settles only the person's own reads and actions); Show brings everything in, and each row it brought in (`showBroughtIn` in `held.ts`: new to the list, or carrying agent activity the row on screen lacked; never one that stayed as it was or changed by the person's own hand) opens every closed group it lands in (Show itself never scrolls the list since 2026-11-10; see "Show never scrolls; the list's pill" below) (the owner, 2026-11-08: "When I hit this two new, I want it to open up whatever groups they appear in"; `showLandsIn` in `held.ts` fills the notice's `lands`, InboxList opens them with the press through `useListGroups().open`, the same kept choice as a click on the header, so they stay open; groups no new row lands in stay as they are; the List view opens no group; a keyboard Show focuses the first row it brought in, without scrolling; `showOpensGroups.test.tsx`), and lights up briefly where it landed, once it is in view (owner's request, "inbox-polish" item 6): `data-lit` on the row once the rows have slid into place and the row is in view (`SHOW_LIT_AFTER_SLIDE_MS`; at once under reduced motion), the Show bar's tint on the row's pill held 1.2 s and faded over 1.8 s (owner, 2026-10-20; `@keyframes ib-lit` holds its first 40%, `SHOW_LIT_MS` 3000, then the mark comes off), under the selection and hover, which paint over it with `!important` while the animation keeps running, and under the focus ring; a phone's row takes the same tint. Only Show lights rows: never a close's settle or the first load. The thread the person has open is the exception (design record "inbox-new-in-open-thread", approved 2026-10-12; the `open` argument (`OpenThread`) of `heldNotice`, `refreshHeldRows` and `settleOwn`, which `App.tsx` passes as the open thread, else the one just closed until the list settles, with its row as it stood at the close (`asOf`), so an agent message that lands after the close, in the settle's beat, is news like any other): an agent message that arrives there is never counted or named in "N new", its row takes its state in place (bold on the arrival, not bold once seen on sight), and on close it settles where that state puts it, an arrival the person did not see bringing it back unread (New, or Waiting when it asks) instead of waiting behind Show. Where the thread covers the list (`COVERED_LIST_SETTLES` `'while-covered'`), Back moves that one row only when an arrival there is still unseen, or when the thread now waits on the person (Stopped, Holding, Waiting: an agent asked while it was open, seen or not) and is drawn in another group (`settlesOnBack`, then `settleThread`; after a keyboard Back, or a stacked window's mouse Back, focus goes with the redrawn row, `refocusRow`; a phone's tap moves none); everything else stays as left, so a thread only read is never moved, and one with open questions is never left folded in Quiet. With no thread open the same (approval "inbox-list-view", R7, 2026-11-05: a hover Mark read used to adopt the whole read, bringing in everything behind "N new" and sliding another row's Mark read under the pointer): every write (the row's hover Mark read and Mark unread, the row menu's Archive, Send now, a pick's save, compose's Send, the project menu's batches) reads the list without adopting it (`afterWrite` in `App.tsx` calls `refreshList(false)`), the written rows take the server's row in place even where an agent's message there waits behind "N new" (`afterWrite`'s `written`, `replaceHeldRow`; an archived row shows "Archived" in place, `markHeldRowArchived`), and agent activity anywhere else keeps waiting for Show. A hover Mark read leaves the row where it is, not bold, with Mark unread under the pointer; it moves to Quiet when the list next settles (the toast "Marked read · “subject”"); Mark unread from a row leaves it bold where it is ("Marked unread · “subject”", `markUnreadToast`'s `moved` false; from the open thread, which closes, "· back in New"). Mark read on a seen update the list still holds in New stays in place too (`seenStaysPut`; beside an open thread it still moves, `moveSeenToQuiet`). A row archived in place is faded (`data-archived` on `.ib-rw`, `.ib-rw[data-archived]` in `inbox.css`, the same under reduced motion), left out of its group's count (`liveCount`), and a list whose rows are all archived shows its empty state above them (Archive all at `#project=`). A written row the list already settled away (an Archive's Undo after Show or a refresh) goes back in at its place (`afterWrite` through `addOwnThreads`, a snoozed one into the band), never as "N new". The person's own snooze and unsnooze still move their one row (`moveSnoozedRow`, `moveRowAlone`), and a thread compose starts goes into Sent (`addOwnThreads`). `event: snoozes` (another tab or machine, or the snooze clock) with no thread open moves only the rows whose snooze changed (`snoozeChangedRows` in `held.ts`, each by `settleThread`, no whole sort; review of #2716, P1), and a return by the clock waits behind "N new". One exception to holding still: a click on a browser notification opens its thread and adopts the list as it now is, even with a thread open, because the person was away and the notified thread may not be on screen. The route-change layout effect in `App.tsx` decides when; one `[shown]` layout effect plays the motion and repairs focus.
  - The person's own snooze is the one write that moves a row while a thread is open (design record "inbox-snooze-moves-row", approved 2026-11-03, amending "inbox-open-stays-put"). It applies whenever the list is drawn, even hidden behind a full-screen thread, a file, or a Board card; phones and stacked windows are the exception (`listCovered()`). Once the server confirms the snooze (header ⋯ menu or row menu), `moveSnoozedRow` in `App.tsx` moves that one row into the Snoozed band with `settleThread` and the settle motion; every other row stays held. Snoozing the open thread takes its row out of the list while the thread stays open with its "Snoozed until … · Unsnooze" line. Undo (`putSnooze`) puts the row back between the same neighbours (`putRowBack`). So does an Unsnooze before the list settles (`snoozeMoved`, emptied on every settle or adopt). After a row-menu snooze, focus goes to the row now in its slot (`follow(id, true)` in `useContextMenuTrigger.ts`). Phones and stacked windows are unchanged: the list is covered and Back already shows the row gone.
  - **Show never scrolls; the list's pill** (the owner, 2026-11-10: "instead of scrolling me down all over the … place, if it's below, it'll highlight the ones at the top of the list, and if there's ones below, I can click it"; variant "A · Pill" of the design "new below", not in this repo; `packages/inbox/showLight.ts`, `packages/inbox/components/ListNewPill.tsx`). This amends the paragraph above: Show (and a refresh) no longer scrolls the first row into view, and the light is per row.
    - The light (`useShowLight`): each row Show brought in is lit when it is in view in the list (60% of it showing), for its whole 3 s from that moment, however long after Show. Rows out of view wait unlit (`unseen`, each with the edge it is past). Where the rows are is measured after the slide and then only when something can have moved them (the list's `scroll`, a ResizeObserver, a MutationObserver on the list), never on a timer; a row whose group is closed is dropped. A compose send's own row is lit at once as before (no list to measure in). A new Show replaces the run: rows of the last one still unseen lose their light.
    - The pill is the thread's own (`.ib-npill`, `data-list-new-pill`) at the list's edge, "↓ N new" or "↑ N new", for two cases. **Held**: news came while the "N new · Show" line is out of view; each row's edge is where it will land (the top of its group, its first row, scrolled away above: top; in view or further down: bottom; a closed group is its header; always top in the List view); a press is Show and goes to the rows it brings in past that edge (`onShowNew(edge)`, the nearest to the middle of the list). News that arrives while the line is in view never shows one, and scrolling the line into view takes it away. News that lands in Quiet or Sent shows none. **One direction at a time** (the owner, 2026-11-10: never "↑ 3 new" when two of the three are below): in both cases the pill counts the side with more rows (the owner: "make the pill say which way most of the new ones are"; below on a tie), below at the bottom edge or above at the top edge; after a press it shows what is left. **Shown**: after Show, the `unseen` rows; a press (`go`) scrolls to the nearest, to the middle, moves focus to it, and holds the count until the scroll ends (`scrollend`, at most 1.2 s) so the pill does not flash on the way. Scrolling to the rows by hand lights them and takes them off the count. The pill's press is the only thing that scrolls the list.
    - How long it stays is one constant, `LIST_NEW_PILL_STAYS` in `ListNewPill.tsx` (an open question with the owner). True (now): until it is pressed or the reader scrolls there. False: `LIST_NEW_PILL_MS` (5 s) after the last arrival, each arrival adding to the count and starting the 5 s again, then a fade (`data-fading`, 1800 ms ease-in-out, the end of Show's light), after which news counts from zero; the pointer or focus on it holds it; reduced motion has no fade.
    - State: what waits is the held list's (`heldNews` in `held.ts`, the rows `heldNotice` counts, on the notice as `news`) and the light's (`unseen`); the pill keeps only what the reader has had a sign of. It waits while the list is covered (`covered`: a phone's open thread, a stacked window, full screen) or the tab is hidden. A project's page and the list's filter: the pill follows the line, which counts only news in threads that list shows.
    - Accessibility: a real button before the rows in the Tab order. Its name is whole and says what the press does: held, "3 new messages below, show and go there"; after Show, "2 new messages below, go there"; a polite live region beside it (`data-list-new-said`) says "N new message(s) below / above" once as the pill comes. Off, it is out of the Tab order and `aria-hidden`. After a keyboard Show on the line, focus goes to the first row brought in without scrolling to it. A phone: a 44 px target (the pill stays 32 px), above the bottom safe area and under the group header stuck at the top; above "Reconnecting…" when that line shows. No sound, no shortcut.
    - Proof: `packages/inbox/showLight.test.tsx` and `packages/inbox/listNewPill.test.tsx` (DOM, a fake clock and stand-in layout: lit only in view and for the whole time, Show scrolling nothing, both cases of the pill, staying, the fading answer, Quiet and Sent, covered and hidden, the name and the one announcement), `held.test.ts` ("heldNews"), and `tests/e2e/inbox-list-new-pill.spec.ts` on the compiled binary (the pill at the bottom edge and staying, its press, Show leaving `scrollTop` alone with the pill for the row below, a 390 px phone).
  - Click Inbox to refresh (the owner, 2026-10-20: "Just click inbox, it refreshes. That way I don't have to refresh the page"; `refreshInbox` in `App.tsx`): a click (or Enter / Space) on the sidebar's Inbox row while the whole list shows, on the project folder the list shows, or on the list header's title (a button inside the `h1`, `data-list-refresh`) reads the list again and adopts it as Show does (held rows settle, rows it brings in light up, "N new" goes), and reads the drafts, settings and an open thread again: what a page reload would bring and nothing more. It records no look, so the rows' New and the tab's count (#2011) stay as the server says; the stale things it clears are the held rows (a Stopped badge or bold row whose thread moved on behind "N new") and a load error line. With a thread open the sidebar row keeps today's behaviour (the thread closes, a new history entry) and the list adopts the read instead of settling a beat later (`refreshPending` skips the settle); the title refreshes beside the open thread and keeps that thread's row in the group it is drawn in, between the same neighbours (`adoptKeepingOpen` in `held.ts`; a thread archived while open, which the window's read leaves out, keeps its row as drawn, Archived, until it closes), so the selection never leaves view. A click while a refresh is reading is ignored, and a read that brings nothing in leaves Show's light on. Signal: on hover (and keyboard focus) while the list shows with no thread open, the Inbox row's icon (or ↻ after the current project's name) becomes ↻ (`data-refreshes`) with the component tooltip "Refresh"; the title shows ↻ beside it on hover; a refresh turns the icon once (`data-refreshing`, `@keyframes ib-refresh-turn` 500 ms, no turn under reduced motion) and the live region says "Inbox refreshed" (or "<project> refreshed"), also when nothing changed. Focus stays on what was pressed. No keyboard shortcut. Tests: `packages/inbox/clickToRefresh.test.tsx` (DOM), `tests/e2e/inbox-refresh.spec.ts` (compiled binary; screenshots in `PLANNOTATOR_REFRESH_SHOTS`). **Pull down to refresh, on a phone** (the owner: "it's a natural behavior we should support, same way clicking title works"; `packages/inbox/pullRefresh.ts`, wired by `InboxList`'s `onPullRefresh`, which App passes as the same `refreshInbox` the title calls): the page sets `overscroll-none` and the list scrolls in its own box, so the browser's gesture never fires; this is the Inbox's own. Only in the phone layout (`PHONE_QUERY`), on `.ib-lbody` while it is the screen on top (no thread over it) and scrolled to its very top. One finger dragged down past a 4 px slop moves the list down at a damped rate (`dampPull`, a rubber band approaching 240 px) and uncovers a small ↻ in a soft circle under the header (`[data-pull-indicator]`, `aria-hidden`, drawn behind the list and clipped at the header), which turns with the pull and is ready past 64 px of damped travel (`PULL_READY_PX`: foreground colour, full size). Released ready, the list holds at 52 px and the ↻ spins until the read lands, then both spring back (320 ms); released short, they spring back and nothing is read. A sideways move, a second finger, an upward move, or a start while the list is scrolled is no pull. The `touchmove` listener is non-passive and cancels only an active pull's moves (it lets go when the browser says a move cannot be cancelled). It is attached only while the list is within 200 px of its top (`PULL_ARM_PX`, a passive scroll listener adds and removes it) or a gesture is tracked: both engines read the listeners when the finger lands, so it must be there before then, and a fling that ends at the top needs it in place; further down no scroll waits for the script. "At the top" is `scrollTop < 1` (a browser can report 0.5 at rest). While a gesture is tracked its end is also heard on the document (capture `touchend` / `touchcancel`, and a touch `pointerup` / `pointercancel` once the element the finger landed on has left the document, since its touch events then reach nothing), only from the finger that started the gesture (its primary `pointerId`, so another finger's lift never ends it); any new touch anywhere (document capture `touchstart`: the header, the sidebar) lets go of a pull whose lift never came, and one that hears nothing for `PULL_LOST_MS` (4 s, iOS may send the lift's pointerup to the detached row too) is let go by itself, so a row that moves section mid-pull never leaves the list stuck down; a thread opening mid-pull detaches the gesture and puts the list back. Taps and long presses on rows are unchanged (no move, no pull). Everything moves by writing styles (the list's transform, `--ib-pull` and `--ib-pull-turn` on the indicator, `data-state` pull / ready / refreshing / settling, `data-pull` on the list), never a React render per frame. Reduced motion: no turn, spin or spring; the ↻ shows, then hides. The title stays the accessible way: no new focusable element and no shortcut; the live region says "Inbox refreshed" as for the title (the title's ↻ turns too). Desktop is unchanged. Tests: `packages/inbox/pullToRefresh.test.tsx` (DOM: once past the threshold, not short, sideways, two fingers, scrolled (and no blocking listener there), desktop, reduced motion, touchcancel, the row under the finger leaving mid-pull, a second finger's lift, a lost lift let go by the next touch in the list or on the header and by itself after `PULL_LOST_MS`, a thread opening mid-pull, a refresh already in flight, a 0.5 px top), `tests/e2e/inbox-pull-refresh.spec.ts` (compiled binary, iPhone 15 WebKit and Pixel 7 Chromium, light and dark: an agent message held behind "1 new" comes in after the pull, scrolling from the top and a drag down from a scrolled list are no pull, in Chromium the row under the finger leaving mid-pull still ends the pull, a row tap opens its thread; screenshots in `PLANNOTATOR_PULL_REFRESH_SHOTS`, default `/tmp/pull-refresh-shots`).
  - The list header of one project shows its name alone (owner, 2026-10-29: a long folder path beside it was noise and cut off its meaningful end). The folder (`~/…`) is the second line of the title's tooltip (`ProjectTipText`, under "Refresh <name>"); Copy path is in the project's ⋯ menu. Test: `packages/inbox/listHeaderPath.test.tsx`.
  - Motion (wide window): on close the list widens at once, then after `SETTLE_BEAT_MS` (160 ms) the rows slide to their places (FLIP over WAAPI: `captureSettle` before the change, `playSettle` after, reads then writes; `translate` 220 ms ease-in-out-cubic, raised with `z-index` while they move; a row that left the rows on screen is a fading copy where it was, 160 ms ease-out, under the moving rows; a row or band that joined fades in after 120 ms, 160 ms ease-out). A new settle cancels the last one's animations and starts from where the rows are on screen; opening a thread inside the beat cancels the settle, and opening one during the slide stops it (`stopSettle`), so nothing slides under the narrowed list. Show, and a row the person's own snooze moves, slide the same way without the beat. When the close also gives back the docked sidebar that #1871 held closed beside a file (a window of about 920 to 1050 px), the beat is the sidebar's spring instead (`SETTLE_AFTER_SIDEBAR_MS`, its 500 ms plus 40), so the rows slide from the column's final width rather than changing shape under the slide. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` nothing animates: the close settles in the same frame. Animations carry the id `ib-settle`; the fading copies are `.ib-settle-ghost` (`aria-hidden`, `inert`, no `data-row`/`data-thread-id`).
  - Focus: the window moves focus for the person only after a key (`lastInput()` in `packages/inbox/lastInput.ts`, which also tells a Back or Forward from a key, `traversedJustNow`: the last `keydown` or `pointerdown`; a `click` that counts no clicks, `detail` 0, is a screen reader or switch press and counts as a key). After a keyboard close, focus that the pane took with it goes to the thread's row; when a focused row leaves the rows on screen (for folded Quiet), focus goes to the row now in its slot (`rowInSlot`: the next on screen, else the one before, else the Quiet band); a keyboard Show (whose line goes away) puts it on the first row it brought in. After a mouse or a tap nothing is moved, so no row the person did not choose holds focus, except a stacked window's Back (#1866), which returns focus to the thread's row as before; that focus is not `:focus-visible`, so the row's Mark read action stays hidden and `E` ignores it. The row's Mark read action shows on hover or keyboard focus (`.ib-rw:has(:focus-visible)`), and `E` acts on a row only when it has keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`), else on the open thread. Opening a thread moves no focus into it: the foot's Reply takes focus only when that thread's own reply box closes (Escape, Cancel, a Send that did not go), never when the thread opens or another thread closes the last one's box (the owner's report: the first thread after a load showed Reply ringed; `boxSeen` in `Thread.tsx`, proved by `packages/inbox/replyFocusOnOpen.test.tsx`). A Back or Forward that lands on the address already shown moves no focus either (`onTraverse` in `App.tsx`): it replaced nothing, and focusing the thread's row there opened the row's card over a list the thread then covered (`inbox-mobile.spec.ts`, "sideways at desktop widths").
  - Where the thread covers the list (a phone, a stacked window), `COVERED_LIST_SETTLES` in `settle.ts` decides; owner's answer (2026-10-12): `'while-covered'`: Back shows the list exactly as left (the row read in place, no longer bold; focus on it after a keyboard Back) and it settles while the next thread covers it (before that thread's look), or on Show or a folder change. The thread being opened settles as it now is too (`settleOwn` with it as the open thread): an agent message held behind "N new" there is the person's to read now, so its row takes the place its state gives it (Quiet to Holding up work, say) instead of keeping the held row's section. `'on-back'` (settle with the slide when the person goes Back) is the one-value alternative.
  - Mark read from inside an open thread: the toast says "Marked read · Moves to Quiet" (phone "Read · Moves to Quiet"), since the row moves only when the list settles; from the row's hover action or the row menu, a thread open or none, it says "Marked read · “<subject>”" (`markReadToast`'s `held`), since the row stays where it is (R7). Its Undo says "Moved back." (the row never left).
  - A seen update held in New (the owner, 2026-10-27): opening a plain update (no question) records the look, so the server puts it in Quiet, while the held list still draws it in New. Mark read stays on it there: the row menu and `E`, and on a phone, which has neither, the thread ⋯ sheet (`seenHeldInNew` in `markRead.ts`: drawn in New, its state Quiet, not marked read, not archived; `canToggleRead` and `offersRowMarkRead` take the group the row is drawn in, `drawnSection` in `held.ts`). The row's hover action and the header's button are Mark unread there instead (the owner, 2026-11-02: "I opened it. I still want the ability to mark it unread", and for the header "If I open a thread, and it transitions to [read] because there's no questions or anything, then I need the ability to mark unread"; `RowWithAction` in `components/InboxList.tsx` and `markUnreadOffered` in `Thread.tsx` take `canMarkUnread`, which no longer looks at the group drawn), on the open thread (from the moment its look goes out: App.tsx `looking`, held.ts `lookedRow`) and on one just left. Mark read there writes nothing (the thread is already Quiet) and moves that one row to Quiet now (`settleThread`, with the settle's slide), with the toast "Moved to Quiet · “<subject>”" and its Undo (`Z`), which puts the row back between the same neighbours (`putRowBack`) and says "Moved back.". Pressed before the look this tab sent has landed (the row still New and unseen on the server), Mark read first waits for that look (`looksInFlight` in `App.tsx`: the open look and seen-on-sight looks, each with the list read after it), then decides, so it takes the same local way however soon it is pressed; it waits at most `MARK_READ_LOOK_WAIT_MS` (1.5 s, `markRead.ts`), and past that a look that stalls leaves the thread New on the server, so Mark read writes as usual. On the open thread this is the one exception to "the selection stays on it": its row goes to folded Quiet while the thread stays open; stepping (J/K, #2257) goes on from the row's last slot. A row drawn in Quiet, Sent or Waiting keeps the rules above; "Mark all as read" and the server are unchanged. Proofs: `openStaysPut.test.tsx` (header, menu, E, Undo and Z, the hover's Mark unread, a row already in Quiet, a question that still writes), `held.test.ts`, `rowMenu.test.tsx`.
  - Proofs: `held.test.ts`, `settle.test.ts`, the DOM test `openStaysPut.test.tsx` (over `tests/helpers/inbox-dom-server.ts ... news`, which also takes an agent's message on stdin) and `tests/e2e/inbox-open-stays-put.spec.ts` (every frame of the settle sampled; reduced motion; a keyboard Show; a mouse close that hands no focus and lets `E` mark nothing; a phone in WebKit and a stacked window; on a phone and a stacked window, questions held behind "1 new", read and left with Back, land in Holding up work; in a stacked window, questions that arrive while the thread is open and are seen on sight, and a thread opened wide whose window narrows to stacked, land there too, never in Quiet), and `held.test.ts`'s `settlesOnBack` case.
- First run (no project yet), "No agent has written yet": "One next step" (design record `.product/approved/inbox-connect-next-step/`, variant C, approved 2026-10-16; not in this repo; `components/EmptyState.tsx`, pure rules in `packages/inbox/connectSteps.ts`). Plannotator's own connections first (Claude Code, Pi, OpenCode 2: your reply arrives in the session as a new turn on its own), agents through MCP after (they see the reply when they next check the Inbox); MCP stays reachable as "Use MCP instead" and the picker at the end.
  - Until the onboarding tour was started, finished or skipped (config.json `inboxTour`), a first run opens on the tour's entry screen, "Two paths", and "Connect my agents" there leads here; once decided, this screen ends with a quiet "Take the 2-minute tour" link (see "The onboarding tour").
  - Read from `GET /api/inbox/agents`. Each agent's state (`connectRowOf`): a plugin host is `ready` (its plugin installed, `reach.kind === 'tool'`, tool on), `tool-off` (`locked` while `PLANNOTATOR_INBOX_TOOL` decides), `mcp-only` (no plugin, connected through MCP: done, but replies don't wake it), `missing` (found, no plugin, not connected) or `absent`; another agent is `mcp` (connected), `connect` (not connected, one click) or `manual` (only a person can add it). One the person turned off, removed or holds its own entry is left out.
  - One large card names the next step (`connectPlan`: turn a tool on, then install a plugin, then give an MCP-only host its plugin, each in Claude Code, Pi, OpenCode order, then MCP agents): "NEXT STEP · 1 of N" / "last one", a title ("Turn on Pi's Inbox tool", "Connect Claude Code with Plannotator's plugin", "Give Pi Plannotator's extension", "Connect Codex", "Connect Cline by hand"), one sentence (Pi and OpenCode's Turn on names the measured cost, `inboxToolCostText`), and one primary: **Turn on** / **Connect** (one click, `POST /api/inbox/agents { agent, reach: true }`; a refusal shows on the card with Try again; the live region says when it applies), or the install command to **Copy** (Claude Code's `claude plugin marketplace add … && claude plugin install …`, Pi's `pi install npm:@plannotator/pi-extension`, OpenCode's `opencode.json` line with an OpenCode 2 / 1 switch; the window never installs) with "Use MCP instead" folded for a host that is not connected (`hostMcpSnippet`, `mcpFoldHint`: "Works, but <host> sees your replies only when it checks the Inbox; they don't arrive as a new turn."). **Not now** puts a step off for this page load (it stays in Left to do as "later"). A tool locked off, or one config.json turns off by name (`tool.chosen_off`), is never a step (Left to do: "locked by …", "off"). The buttons use `aria-disabled` while saving and focus moves to the next card's heading (to Try it once nothing is left), never to the page. A host with a live session (`GET /api/inbox/sessions`) counts as connected whatever the files say (a project's own settings, `claude --plugin-dir`).
  - Under it, "Already connected" (plugin / extension, "· running now" while a session of that host is live, "next session" after Turn on, "MCP · sees replies when it checks" for any agent reached through MCP, "MCP · replies arrive as a new turn" for a Codex the Inbox can wake, from the row's `wakes`) and "Left to do" (the step "now", the rest, "locked by PLANNOTATOR_INBOX_TOOL=0", plugin hosts "not on this computer"). Nothing found: "FIRST STEP · Connect your first agent" with a Claude Code / Pi / OpenCode switch and that host's install command, no lists. With nothing left: "ALL SET · Your agents reach the Inbox".
  - Which agents a reply wakes, said once under the lists (`WAKE_LINE` in `connectSteps.ts`, `data-wake-note`): "With Plannotator's connection, your replies arrive as a new turn on their own in Claude Code, Pi and OpenCode 2. Other agents see your reply the next time they check the Inbox." (OpenCode 2, because OpenCode 1 has the tool but no wake; `wakeLine`, built from `INBOX_WAKING_HOSTS` / `INBOX_MCP_WAKING_HOSTS`, less DeepSeek Harness: the owner keeps the line without it, `WAKE_LINE_UNNAMED`). When a Codex row is on this computer (`mcpWakingHostsHere`: detected) it adds ", and in Codex 0.149 or later through MCP"; without one the words are exactly as before. The same for the line over the "Another agent or editor" picker (`mcpWakeLine`: with Codex here, "Codex 0.149 or later gets your reply as a new turn on its own; the others … can't be woken"). Settings → Agents gives each agent that reaches the Inbox a "Your replies" row (`repliesOf` in `AgentsSettings.tsx`): "arrive as a new turn" through Plannotator's own tool, "seen when it checks the Inbox; it can't be woken" through MCP (OpenCode 1: "OpenCode 1 can't be woken"), except a connected Codex the Inbox can wake (`GET /api/inbox/agents` row `wakes: true`: a Codex CLI 0.149+ it can run), "arrive as a new turn"; a Codex below 0.149 still reads "can't be woken". send_message and submit_guide tell a sender nothing wakes "The person's reply won't arrive in this session by itself: go on with your work and call read_thread with this thread_id later." (`INBOX_REPLY_READ_LATER_TEXT`; see `senderWakes`).
  - Try it: **Message <host>** (opens compose to the live session of a ready host, `GET /api/inbox/sessions`; the primary when nothing is left) and "In a new Claude Code or Codex session, ask:" with the test prompt and Copy. Then "Another agent or editor" folded: the harness picker without the three hosts (`OTHER_HARNESSES`), with one verified artefact, one note and a folded "Another way" per harness.
  - The screen moves on by itself: while it shows, `App.tsx` reads the agents again when the window gets focus or becomes visible and every 8 s (at most once per 2 s; the read runs no agent CLI; never for a tailnet guest), and the live sessions every 5 s while the tab is visible. A numbered read never replaces a newer read or save (`agentsSeq` in `App.tsx`). A tailnet guest (`tailnetGuest`) is refused the agents route: the first step with the commands and "Open the Inbox on the computer to connect agents with one click." A read that failed on this computer says so, with Try again.
  - `InboxAgentRow.plugin` (additive, the three hosts only): whether Plannotator's own plugin or extension is installed (`inboxPluginInstalled`). Claude Code is now found by `claude` on PATH too; its plugin counts as enabled when user `settings.json`, `settings.local.json` or managed settings enable it, or `plugins/installed_plugins.json` records it and no scope says false (`claudeCodePluginEnabled`). Without it its reach is `manual` ("install Plannotator's Claude Code plugin") and Turn on is refused. `tool.chosen_off` (additive): config.json turns the host's tool off by name.
  - DeepSeek Harness is a tool host outside the three plugin cards. It is found by Plannotator's plugin in a profile (`$DSH_HOME/profiles/*/package.json` names `@plannotator/dsh`; `DSH_HOME` defaults to `~/.dsh`; `dsh` itself is never run) or by that `profiles` folder alone, never by a `dsh` on PATH (Debian and Ubuntu ship an unrelated `dsh`, the distributed shell). With the plugin, its reach is `tool`, so its row is `ready` when the tool is on or a session is live, else `tool-off` (a step, "Turn on DeepSeek Harness's Inbox tool", after the three hosts' own steps). It has no first-run host card. Without the plugin, its reach is `manual` ("add Plannotator's DeepSeek Harness plugin"). The picker's DeepSeek Harness panel gives `dsh plugin --profile <profile> add @plannotator/dsh` and no MCP command: DSH strips `DSH_*` from MCP servers, so over MCP the Inbox cannot tell its sessions apart.
  - Cline is a tool host the same way: found by Plannotator's plugin in a Cline plugins folder (global or the Inbox folder's workspace one; the paths are in `inbox-notifications-and-agents.md`) or by `cline` on PATH. With the plugin its reach is `tool` ("Turn on Cline's Inbox tool", with the cost line; after Turn on, "Cline's Inbox tool is on. It applies to Cline sessions started from now on." and "next session", `CLINE_APPLIES`); without it, `manual`. The picker's Cline panel gives `cline plugin install npm:@plannotator/cline`, and the MCP snippet for the extension's Legacy bundle under "Another way".
  - OpenClaw is a tool host the same way: found by Plannotator's plugin under OpenClaw's state folder or by the folder or `openclaw` on PATH (paths in `inbox-notifications-and-agents.md`). With the plugin its reach is `tool` ("Turn on OpenClaw's Inbox tool", with the cost line; it applies from OpenClaw's next message); without it, `manual`. The picker's OpenClaw panel gives `openclaw plugins install @plannotator/openclaw`. Its mark is the OpenClaw lobster as an outline, traced from OpenClaw's MIT-licensed icon.
  - Hermes Agent likewise: found by `$HERMES_HOME` (default `~/.hermes`), with the plugin by `plugins/plannotator/plugin.yaml`. With it its reach is `tool` (after Turn on, "Hermes Agent's Inbox tool is on. It applies to new Hermes Agent sessions.", `HERMES_APPLIES`); without it, `manual` with the install line. The picker's Hermes Agent panel gives the install line and the `hermes config set …allow_gateway_injection true` line together. Its mark is a plain letter H with two small wings, drawn here: Hermes's own icon is a detailed illustration with no published brand terms, so no brand asset is used.
  - Every command names this CLI's absolute path (`mcp_command` from `GET /api/inbox/settings`).
  - Proofs: `packages/inbox/connectSteps.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/connectNextStep.test.tsx` (each of the mock's computers, `connectSteps.fixtures.ts`), and `tests/e2e/inbox-first-run.spec.ts` (compiled binary, scratch HOME and stub PATH per computer; Turn on, the auto-advance after an install, shots desktop and phone, light and dark, into `PLANNOTATOR_FIRST_RUN_SHOTS`).
- Settings, in sections (design of record `.product/approved/inbox-settings-tabs/`, approved 2026-10-12; `components/Settings.tsx`): Workspaces' SettingsFrame in the Inbox's scale. A focused page: "← Inbox" on top (`data-settings-back`, back to the last hash that was not a Settings page, so moving between sections never changes where it goes), then a 184 px section nav (eyebrow "Settings"; a `nav` of links with `aria-current="page"` on the section shown; a count on Archived, a dot on About while an update is available, ready or failed, and a warning dot on Devices while a way a phone reaches this computer, or Over your tailnet, is switched on and not working) beside one section's page: an `h1` that takes focus on each section change (not on the first load), a one-line lede, and grouped cards of rows. The Inbox sidebar slides away while Settings is open and comes back on leaving, without writing its cookie (`settingsSidebar` in `App.tsx`; ⌘B there opens it for that visit only). A section change is a new history entry, so Back and Forward walk the sections. On a phone `#settings` is a list of the sections with a value on each (the theme, the agents with the tool on, phones paired, archived count, store size, version; Capabilities' value, the last row of the first group, names what is on: "Board", "Decision mode", "2 on" or "Off") and a section opens with "‹ Settings", the browser's Back when it was opened from the list (`history.ts`); back on the list, focus is on the row of the section just left. A failed settings read shows "Settings did not load" with Try again. The sections:
  - General: first the version row (`GeneralUpdateRow` in `components/Updates.tsx`): the version this Inbox runs, the sidebar's own update action (Update to X, Updating…, restart, Update failed…) and Check now. With update checks off it still offers Update to X for a newer release this machine knows of, and says why checks are off and how to turn them back on (started with `PLANNOTATOR_UPDATE_CHECK=0`: restart without it; or the config switch in About), as its status when there is nothing to offer, else on a second line (`data-general-checks-off`). Then Appearance (Theme, Text size, and Colors), and "When an agent asks you" (Desktop notifications with the browser's permission, the blocked warning, and the question sound's row).
  - **Text size** (owner, 2026-10-18: "it's default 14, but I could also go to 13 or 15 or even 12 ... I have to be able to test it"; `packages/inbox/textSize.ts`): a segmented 12 / 13 / 14 / 15 control in Appearance, the Theme control's style, applied at once with no reload. It sets `--inbox-text-size` on the document element (removed at 14), which `inbox.css` reads as the window's one type knob, `--ib-type: var(--inbox-text-size, 14)`: every size `inbox.css` sets is `font-size: var(--ib-fs-N, Npx)` with `--ib-fs-N = max(min(N, 11) px, N px × --ib-type / 14)`, so the whole ramp (rows, subjects, the sidebar, message bodies, question cards, Settings, timestamps, chips, badges) keeps its relative steps, nothing goes under 11 px (a size already under 11 never under its own), and at 14 each size is exactly today's (a screenshot at 14 is byte-identical to the window before the setting). Phone controls' 16 px take `--ib-fs-16-in = max(16px, …)`, so iOS never zooms. ui's drawing inside the window follows through Tailwind's `--text-xs` (never under 11 px) … `--text-5xl` scaled on `.pn-inbox`, ui's fixed `text-[12.5px]` … `text-[15px]` in the `ib-type` layer after Tailwind's utilities, and the code blocks (`pre code.pn-code`); ui's other fixed sizes under 12 px are left as they are. Row and control heights keep today's pitch, so phones keep 44 px targets. Kept in config.json `inboxTextSize` through `POST /api/inbox/settings` `{ text_size }`, and adopted from the first settings read like the theme (never over a pick made on the page first); the `plannotator-inbox-text-size` cookie keeps the last size this browser showed, so a reload paints at it before the read answers. Not on the phone's door. `packages/inbox/softType.test.ts` fails on a bare `font-size` or a token without its floor; `packages/inbox/textSize.test.tsx` (DOM) proves a pick moves a row's subject and a message body and 14 is today's.
  - **Favicon** (owner, 2026-11-09: "We could even add a settings inside the Plannotator inbox to allow you to choose the favicon for the inbox as well"; `packages/inbox/faviconChoice.ts`): a segmented Glass / Yellow / Face control in Appearance, after Colors, each choice with its icon, the Theme control's style. One shared choice: config.json `favicon` through POST /api/inbox/settings `{ favicon }`, the same value plan review's Theme tab writes and every server's `/favicon.png` answers with (stored ids `glass`, the default; `yellow`; `totman`, the Face icon; a `classic` left by 0.28.57, the removed P icon, reads as unset and shows Glass, and nothing rewrites it). A pick shows on the tab at once: the tab icon becomes the chosen icon's data URL and the count's badge (`tabCount.ts`) is drawn over it again. A choice made elsewhere (plan review, another Inbox window) shows at this window's next load. The row is absent until the settings read answers. Test: the DOM test `packages/inbox/tabCountWindow.test.tsx`.
  - Agents: the `inboxTool` knob per host ("applies to the next session"; a lock line and disabled switches while `PLANNOTATOR_INBOX_TOOL` decides) and Connect an agent (the compact picker: Other MCP client, the command and `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/mcp`), as they were; the Agents design of its own ships separately.
  - Prompts (approval `.product/approved/inbox-prompts-page/`, 2026-10-26, #2194; `components/PromptsSettings.tsx`): what Plannotator tells agents. "Inbox reminder": the text in effect, always in full, marked Default or Custom (`ib-chip`, `pm-custom`); **Edit** turns it into a field with **Save** (refused while unchanged, blank, or past 9,000 characters; the count `n / 9,000` shows from 8,000, red past the cap, the field's `aria-describedby`) and **Cancel**; once custom, **Reset to default** and a "Show default" disclosure (`aria-expanded`). Saving Plannotator's own text as the shared text deletes the key. In the same card, Claude Code, Pi and OpenCode: "Uses the shared text" with a quiet Edit (starting from the shared text), or "Own text" (shown) with **Use shared** (deletes the key) and Edit; "· Reminder off in Agents" (a link to `#settings/agents`) only where `inboxReminder` (or the tool, or Claude Code's mod) leaves that agent without one. OpenCode 1 (`reminder.wakes.opencode` false, from its config's `plugin` list or `opencode` alone on PATH) with nothing custom reads "Uses the default for OpenCode 1" and shows the no-wake line in its row, the text it really gets, and Edit starts from it. Text config.json holds over the cap (hand-written) is ignored; the page says so calmly under the text in effect ("The text in config.json is N characters, over the 9,000 limit, so it is ignored.", `reminder.over`), with Reset to default (shared) or Use shared (an agent's) deleting it. Then "Guided reviews" (one hint line, "Added to every guided review, from the Inbox, the review page or VS Code."): the Guided Review standing instructions (`guide-instructions.md`, the one file the review UI's guide launch and VS Code edit too, and `get_guide_brief` appends), marked Default (none: the chip and Edit alone) or Custom (the text shown, with **Reset to default** deleting the file); Edit opens an empty field with an example placeholder, or the saved text, with the same Save and Cancel (the count `n / 12,000` from 11,000, the review page's cap). Save reads "Saving…" while a save is in flight, for both the reminder and the guide (a slow link otherwise showed only a faded button). Then "Plan, review and annotate": one folded row, "12 prompts · all default" / "· N custom", opened read-only per group with each prompt's key, mark, text in effect and notes ("OpenCode has a shorter default.", "Added after your feedback.", and the agents with their own text). Focus: Edit puts it in the field, Save and Cancel back on that Edit. Tooltips are `Tip`. No keyboard shortcut. On a phone the field is 180 px tall, Save fills the row, targets 44 px. For a tailnet guest the page is read-only ("Change it on this computer."). The phone's list of sections names its value Default or Custom (Custom with any reminder text or guide instructions of the person's own), and the list's first group is General, Agents, Prompts, Devices.
  - Devices: Phones and "How your phone reaches this computer" (This Wi-Fi, Tailscale; see "Phones"), only while the Inbox serves phones, then "Over your tailnet" with the difference written out ("Your phone's app does not need this"). With phones off, Devices is Over your tailnet alone.
  - Archived: the archived threads, with Unarchive (`components/Archived.tsx`; see "Archived" under "The list").
  - Activity (the owner, 2026-11-07, renamed from Usage 2026-11-08; `#settings/activity`, `clock` icon, after Capabilities, first under "Your data and Plannotator" on a phone; `components/ActivityChart.tsx`, pure half `packages/inbox/activityChart.ts`, ink `packages/inbox/activityInk.ts`, styles `.ib-activity-*`): this Inbox's threads hour by hour. One row per thread in the order threads began, day labels on top, a mark for each hour with messages, a "1 … 10+ messages an hour" key. Since 2026-11-08 (approval `.product/approved/settings-storage-activity/`) Decision data sits under the chart in this section, with no nav row of its own; the section shows with or without the chart. Not the accounts' Usage (Claude account limits, `components/Usage.tsx`, `.ib-usage-*`).
    - Read: `GET /api/inbox/activity` (Bun Inbox only; see server-and-storage.md) from the window's local midnight `days - 1` days ago to the coming midnight, so hours and days line up with its clock (a clock change is a 23- or 25-hour day). Project: the list's filter when Settings opened (`settingsReturn`), unless it is a linked machine's (this store cannot read it: every project instead), changeable in the full screen with the Board's `ProjectFilter` chip (this Inbox's projects only, never a linked machine's; its menu is drawn inside an enclosing `<dialog>` so the modal's top layer does not cover it). Read again 1.5 s after the store's cursor moves.
    - Two sizes (the owner, 2026-11-08: "a slimmer preview and click to expand fullscreen … like a light box"). Settings shows a slim preview: the range packed into the section's width (never scrolls sideways; a week is about 6 px an hour at 1440), rows 2 to 5 px apart sharing up to 480 px (taller only past about 240 threads), a short one-line day band, no tooltip. A click on it, or Expand, opens the full screen in the window's `Modal`. Expand works once a read has answered, empty or failed too, since the range and project live in the full screen: a quiet week is never a dead end (Updates.tsx, `ownKeys`; Escape, × or a click on the backdrop closes it; the whole window less 24 px a side, edge to edge on a phone): the range (Today / 3 days / Week / 2 weeks / 30 days; a phone stops at Week), the project, the summary and the full chart, every hour an equal share of the width (narrower than 1.5 px it scrolls sideways, opened at the latest hour), rows 5 to 9 px apart fitted to the height (more threads scroll, the day labels stay on top). Default range: a week, 3 days on a phone. The range and project are shared by both sizes.
    - Look "blend" (the owner's pick from a reference chart): the mark's area follows the hour's count (1 to 10+), neighbouring hours join into one soft shape (`activityJoin`, a metaball neck), a run takes the opacity tier of its busiest hour (1–2, 3–9, 10+), and a faint strand runs along the thread from its first to its last hour on screen (rows 4 px or more apart only, so not in a dense preview). Rows never touch: the biggest mark leaves a fifth of the row (at least 0.75 px) to the next. Look "dots" (the v4 prototype: same-size dots that never touch, the count in five colour steps) only with `?activity-look=dots` in the page's address, for comparison shots; never a setting.
    - Colours: theme tokens only (`.ib-activity` in `inbox.css`): `--primary` toward `--card` (the full screen's dialog is on `--card` too), mixed in oklab (oklch's hue arc turned a violet pink on warm cards). `activityInk.ts` reads what the faintest mark of the look on screen draws (probe marks with the chart's own classes, a 1 px canvas for RGB) and, when it is under 2:1 on the surface, mixes in just enough `--foreground` (`--ib-act-ink`); the strand likewise to 1.3:1. Read again when the palette or mode changes.
    - Mouse and touch only (the owner, 2026-11-08: "No keys"): the chart takes no keys and has no Tab stop of its own; only Expand, the range, the project chip and × are focusable. One SVG of a handful of paths (every mark of a tier in one path) plus a nearest-mark hit test, so 425 threads draw in a few ms. In the full screen, hover shows the thread's name, that hour's and that day's counts, when it started, its last message and its total; a click opens the thread and closes the full screen (on touch: the first tap shows the tooltip, a second tap on the same mark opens).
    - Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/activity.test.ts` (hours from `from`, the span, start order, the project and its combined ones), `packages/inbox/activityChart.test.ts` (the preview never scrolls, the full screen fills its space, rows never touching at either size, days across a clock change, joins, hit test), `packages/inbox/activityChart.test.tsx` (DOM: the read's span and project, opening and closing the full screen, the tooltip and click, two taps on touch, no keys, the way out of an empty or failed read, a linked machine's project, Escape in the project menu closing only the menu).
  - Storage: the store on disk, then per project and thread with Delete from Inbox on each thread and each project (approval `.product/approved/settings-storage-activity/`, 2026-11-08: "Delete project" read as deleting the project itself; the button's name says what it deletes, `Delete from Inbox: <name>`; "Delete? Click again" before it deletes). The sizes are their own read (`GET /api/inbox/settings/storage`, a walk of every file), asked for only while Storage is shown, each time it opens; the window keeps the last answer for the visit (the phone list's Storage value shows only after that), and a Delete's answer replaces it. While the first count runs (the owner's choice "A · The calm wait", 2026-11-02): the heading and the folder line stay, the total is a soft grey bar (`.ib-store-bar`), the card holds its place at 260 px (`[data-store-wait]`), "Counting what is stored…" shows only after `STORE_SLOW_MS` (0.4 s), and the numbers fade in (160 ms; no fade under reduced motion, nor for numbers kept from an earlier visit). One read at a time, given up after `STORAGE_READ_MS` (20 s, so a held connection on a phone never locks Storage). When it fails with no numbers kept, the card says "The sizes could not be read. <why> Try again" (`role="alert"`, `[data-store-error]`, and no `aria-busy` then); Try again or a reopen clears it and shows the wait again. A failed refresh of numbers already kept leaves them on screen. The window's own settings read asks with `?store=0`; a page older than the storage read asks without it and still gets `settings.store`, so it keeps working until it reloads.
  - Decision data (approval `.product/approved/decision-data/`, 2026-11-05; in Settings → Activity under the chart since 2026-11-08, where `#settings/decision-data` lands; mounted only while Activity is shown, so nothing is counted for another section): where the person asks why or when something was decided.
    - **Ask** (`components/DecisionAsk.tsx`): a box and a button that sends the same New message Compose sends (`POST /api/inbox/messages`, the suggested thread name, the most recent live session; with several live sessions, a native "Which agent" select, whose choice holds by session as the list changes), with `decision_ask: true`. The window then opens the new thread. The answer comes back as an ordinary thread, with no filing and no separate list. The stored message is the person's words as typed. What the AGENT receives for it (the bridge command's `body`, so every host's wake, Codex's queue, and `read_thread`'s full message) has `INBOX_DECISION_ASK_BLOCK` (`packages/core/inbox-types.ts`, the owner's exact words) after them, once (`inboxBodyForAgent`), and Codex's queue cuts only the words before it. `read_thread`'s line form of the root says nothing about it and its JSON has `decision_ask: true`. No tool text, skill or `--help` carries it.
    - With no agent running: "No agent is running" and Copy (the typed question only; the block's "reply to this thread" has no thread outside the Inbox). A session that went between the read and the press is a 409 `session_not_live`: nothing is sent, the sessions are read again and the words stay. A retry after a failed press uses the same idempotency key, until the words change.
    - The live sessions come from `useComposeSessions`, read while the section is open, as Compose's are.
    - Tests: `packages/server/inbox-decision-ask.test.ts` (the block once in the delivered turn, a second poll, Codex's queue and `read_thread`; none in what the window reads, a plain New message, a later reply or the tool list; a retried Send; the long question Codex cuts), `packages/inbox/decisionAsk.test.tsx` (DOM: what a press sends, no agent, the choice of agent, a session that went, a failed press).
    - **Insights** (`components/DecisionData.tsx`, `packages/shared/inbox/decision-data.ts`): up to three trends (the last 30 days against the 30 before, whole UTC days with today in the recent period, so on unchanged data a line changes at most once a day; a small arrow, no good or bad colour), the range, a few lines each shown only with enough behind them, Decisions by day, Who asks (a table; a list under 640 px of section width) and the CSV (cut by range as the page is: a decision by when it was made, what waits whatever its age, anything else by when it was asked). No advice, and no uppercase labels.
    - Trends use one rule (`trendsOf`): 60 days of history, 20 or more in each period, a change of 5 points (a quarter for waits), and too big for chance (z 2.5). A stopped agent still waiting counts as the longest wait. Otherwise the line, or the whole block, is absent.
    - States: an empty store shows "No decisions yet." alone; under 20 decisions and under a week, Ask and "So far:", then "Insights show after about a week, or 20 decisions."; a count that fails leaves Ask only. Ask draws at once when the count is back, and after at most `COUNT_PATIENCE_MS` otherwise, with a quiet placeholder for the numbers; that wait is there so an empty store does not flash Ask.
    - **It must never bottleneck Settings or the system** (the owner). Only this section asks for the count, once when shown and once per range change, and nothing runs in the background. `runInSlices` steps a generator calculation and gives the event loop back every `SLICE_MS` (4 ms); when the reader leaves or changes range the fetch aborts, `req.signal` stops the calculation at the next slice and the route answers 499. No Worker, cache, server timer, stored totals or caps.
    - **Numbers kept on delete**: `deleteThread` and `deleteProject` first append one line per question to `inbox/decision-lines.jsonl` (`keepDecisionLines`; times, ids, fate, agent, project name; never a prompt, choice, answer or note). The count reads the file async, skips a torn line or one with the wrong types, and counts a question once if it is also still stored; a stored record it can't read is skipped too, so it costs its own number, never the count, the CSV or a delete. An unanswered question stops waiting once its thread is gone. It is the only stored data here; it counts in Storage's total, and `uninstall --purge` removes it with the `inbox` folder. The words are gone, so Ask cannot read a deleted thread, and the marks taken from messages (a plain reply, "own words") are not kept.
    - "Waiting on you" follows the Inbox's own rules: a message the archive closed does not wait, one an agent sent after the archive does, and an open question a still-waiting Re-ask note lists waits on the agent (`reaskListedIds`, as decision mode reads it; the CSV still says `open`, its fate).
    - Counts are this computer's store only: linked machines are not merged, and the route is not on the device door (as Storage is not).
    - Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/decision-data.test.ts` (what counts as what, the range, the reader's day, the trend rule, the slices, kept lines, the CSV's formula guard), `packages/server/inbox-decision-data.test.ts` (the route's guards, another request answered mid-count, a reader who leaves the count or the CSV, not on the phone door, a delete through the window's route), `packages/inbox/decisionData.test.tsx` (DOM: Ask while the count runs, each state, range changes, leaving), `packages/inbox/settingsSections.test.tsx` (no count until the section shows).
  - About: the version with its status, Check now, Update now while an update shows, the two update switches, and "Where things are" (the data folder, the MCP address and command, shell-quoted with `shellLine` so a path with a space copies whole).
  - Capabilities (the owner, 2026-10-27; `CapabilitiesSection`): the switches that turn a whole Inbox feature on or off, moved out of General. The nav row right after Devices, before Archived, Storage and About (the owner: "above the archive storage about stuff"; `puzzle` icon), on a phone the last row of the first group. First the main capabilities in one card (`data-settings-capabilities`), today the Board (its row `data-settings-board`, config.json `inboxBoard`; see "The Board switch" in `inbox-notifications-and-agents.md`) and the Checklist under it (its row `data-settings-steps`, config.json `inboxSteps`), same words and behaviour as in General before. Then Code review, with Chapter diagrams right under it while Code review is on (its row `data-settings-chapter-diagrams`, config.json `inboxChapterDiagrams`, on by default; see `environment.md`), and Shape (its row `data-settings-shape`, config.json `inboxShape`, owner only; DRAFT description; see "Shape" in `guides-and-shared-ui.md`). Under them the Experimental group (the owner, 2026-10-27: "Experimental just needs to be within Capabilities, under the main list of capabilities"; `data-settings-experimental`, an `h2` "Experimental" with the line "Features still being tried. Each stays off until you turn it on."): each experimental feature's switch, off until turned on, locked with a line while `PLANNOTATOR_EXPERIMENTAL` decides; today Decision mode (POST `/api/inbox/settings` `{ experimental: { decision_mode } }`, config.json `experimental.decisionMode`, unchanged) and, under it, Bro mode (the owner, 2026-11-08: moved here from the main card, with no chip; its row `data-settings-bro`, config.json `inboxBro`; see "Bro mode" in `inbox-notifications-and-agents.md`), then "Float reply drafts" (the owner, 2026-11-08: off by default; its row `data-settings-float-drafts`, config.json `inboxFloatDrafts`; see "Floating reply drafts" in `inbox-messages.md`). The group shows while any of them is reported. The row shows while the Inbox reports `settings.board` or `settings.experimental`, each group only while its own is reported (an older Inbox with neither: no row; `#settings/capabilities` says it is older and to restart). `#settings/experimental` (Experimental's own page for a while) is rewritten to `#settings/capabilities` with `replaceState` (`settleOldExperimental` in `App.tsx`; `parseSettingsHash` reads it as Capabilities with `experimental: true`), and the page scrolls to the group and focuses its heading once the settings are read. There is no Experimental row or nav divider any more. Code review keeps its own Experimental tab (`guides-and-shared-ui.md`): it has no Capabilities section. `#board`'s off page keeps Turn it on and adds "The same switch is in Settings → Capabilities" with a link. Preferences (Theme, Text size, Colors) and the attention switches (Desktop notifications, Play a sound) stay in General; the update switches stay in About, the agents' reach in Agents, phones and the tailnet in Devices.
- Paste follows the field (`packages/inbox/pasteTarget.ts`, installed once by `App.tsx`): dictation apps (Hex and most others on macOS) insert text as the clipboard plus a synthetic ⌘V, which lands wherever focus is. In the Inbox focus often leaves the field being written in without the person noticing (a click on a question card's choice moves it to that radio, a click on the message text or a reply-box chip to the page, while the reply box stays open with its border), so the dictation used to land nowhere. The window remembers the last 8 text fields that had focus (reply box, a card's note or Other, compose, a comment box, any input) and, on a ⌘V (Ctrl+V off macOS) while focus is on something that takes no text, puts focus back in the newest one still on screen before the browser pastes, its caret where it was (read before `focus()`; a range the person had selected collapses to its end, so the dictation replaces nothing). Chrome runs the paste after the page handled the keydown, and the keydown is never cancelled. Paste is the platform modifier with V by the key's name when that is a Latin letter (so Dvorak's ⌘K, Colemak-DH's ⌘D and Workman's ⌘C on V's physical key are not paste), else by its place (`code` `KeyV`, for Cyrillic or Greek layouts). Left alone: focus already in a text field or a frame; text selected inside an attached file's viewer (`.ib-view`) or a guided review (`.ib-viewer`; code selected in its diff sits in Pierre's shadow root, which Chromium and WebKit report on the document as a collapsed selection at the host's parent whose `toString()` still holds the words, so the words decide, not `isCollapsed`), or any selection toolbar showing (every `[data-pn-annotation-toolbar-row]` is checked: ui's viewer draws the words as a pending highlight and clears the browser's selection; except words selected in a thread message, whose toolbar is an offer: `messageSelectionPending`, see "Comments on the text of an agent's message"), because the person is about to comment on it (a word selected anywhere else, a double-click in a thread message (its comment toolbar showing) or on a choice's label, does not turn this off); and a field that is gone, hidden, disabled, read-only or `inert`, outside an open modal dialog, scrolled out of view (out of the window, or outside the box of an ancestor that clips it, overflow not visible, at its first line), with something drawn over it (the hit test at its first line, `elementFromPoint`, finds neither the field nor something inside it; an element that merely holds the field, its pane or the page, does not count as the field), or outside a full-screen file `.ib-view.ib-full` or a guided review `.ib-viewer` unless the hit test shows it on top (a comment box, the search palette or compose, portaled over the cover; with no hit test the browser cannot tell and it is refused). Proofs: `packages/inbox/pasteTarget.test.ts` (DOM, in tests/dom-test-list.txt: field kinds, the layouts, a selected range, the covers and a box portaled over one, several fields remembered, a file-view selection, code selected in a guided review's shadow-root diff with a guide comment left open, and a word selected elsewhere, the toolbar and a hidden toolbar before a showing one, out of view and clipped out of its pane, a sheet drawn over), `tests/e2e/inbox-dictation-paste.spec.ts` (Chromium: the reply box after a pick, a click on the message and a chip; after a word double-clicked in the message; a selected range kept; a card's note after a pick; a paste aimed at another field; compose; a page with no field used; a file full screen over the thread; its own comment box; search opened and closed; words selected in a file; with image paste, an image pasted after a pick attaching once to the reply box; WebKit: the refocus and caret). It only moves focus on the keydown, so the browser's one paste event then reaches the field and the image-paste handlers around the reply box and in compose (`packages/inbox/compose/paste.ts`): an image pasted with focus off the field now attaches too, and text is inserted once, by the browser.
- A press anywhere in the reply box puts the caret in its text (`focusReplyFromBox` in `Thread.tsx`). The box is drawn as one field whose focus ring is always on, but its textarea is only the upper part: a press on the chips' row under it, or beside the chips, moved focus to the page while the box still looked focused, so typing and a dictation app's ⌘V went nowhere (the owner's "focused on the input" report against 0.28.22, which has no paste-follows-the-field rescue). The press is `preventDefault`ed (no blur, no selection) unless it is on a control (`REPLY_BOX_CONTROLS`: buttons, links, inputs, the annotations list, the skill picker, the human-only notice, a file tile); a textarea that already has focus keeps its caret, otherwise the caret goes to the end. Proof: the chips-row case in `tests/e2e/inbox-dictation-paste.spec.ts` (with Option held and released first, as Hex starts).
- **Plain-text paste, inserted by the page** (`packages/ui/utils/pasteInsert.ts`, `installPlainTextPaste`, installed once by the Inbox, plan review / annotate and code review Apps): a workaround for Chrome 155-156 on macOS, to remove once Chrome 157 is stable. It runs ONLY there (`chromiumPasteBugAffected`: the "Chromium" brand of `navigator.userAgentData` at major 155 or 156, else `Chrome/<major>` in the UA string, and a macOS platform), so it turns itself off at 157 and Safari, Firefox and older Chromium (VS Code's webview) keep the browser's own paste: Safari's `execCommand('insertText')` merges with the typing before it, so one ⌘Z after a paste would also erase what was typed. Tests turn it on with `window.__plannotatorForcePlainTextPaste = true` (`FORCE_PLAIN_TEXT_PASTE_FLAG`) or `installPlainTextPaste(win, { enabled, allowUntrusted })`. Chrome 155's post-paste check (CL 8217390) skips its own insertion when the clipboard's sequence token changed during the `paste` event, and an older ClipboardMac race gives the token a second value when an app writes the pasteboard and posts a synthetic ⌘V right away (dictation apps such as Hex, text expanders): the `paste` event carries the text, `beforeinput` arrives empty, nothing is inserted (fixed in Chromium main by CL 8503629, crbug 568453825). So a window-level `paste` listener, bubble phase (every page handler runs first), takes a paste that nothing cancelled, that carries no files and has `text/plain`, aimed at a focused, writable textarea or text-like input (`text`, `search`, `url`, `tel`, `password`), trusted (a script's `paste` event inserts nothing), and not mid IME composition (focus moving or the window losing it also ends a composition, so a lost `compositionend` cannot leave it off): it cancels the browser's insertion and inserts with `execCommand('insertText')` (an undo step; fires `input`, so React's onChange runs), else `setRangeText` plus an `input` event. It does what Chrome would: the selection is replaced, `maxlength` truncates (never splitting a surrogate pair; no room leaves it native), line breaks stay `\n` in a textarea and become spaces in an input. Left native: contenteditable, code editors (`.cm-editor`, `.xterm`, `.monaco-editor`), email and number inputs, and anything under `[data-native-paste]`. It composes with paste-follows-the-field: that moves focus on the keydown, this inserts on the paste. Tests: `packages/ui/utils/pasteInsert.test.ts` (DOM: the rules and the gate) and three cases in `tests/e2e/inbox-dictation-paste.spec.ts`: forced on with the browser's `insertFromPaste` cancelled to simulate the bug, forced on without it (inserted once), and off in an engine without the bug (the browser pastes; skipped wherever Playwright's own Chromium is 155-156 on macOS, as Playwright 1.64's Chromium 156 is, because the workaround is on there by design; Linux CI runs it).
- Theme: it follows the OS until the person chooses, and the choice is Plannotator's one theme preference (`themePair.mode`: the `plannotator-theme` cookie, and config.json `theme`, which plan review, code review and annotate also read and write), so a choice in either place holds in the other (`packages/inbox/theme.ts`). `configurePlannotatorUI`'s `serverSync` (`inboxServerSync`) forwards to `POST /api/inbox/settings` (whose `theme` merges per field into config.json, `saveConfig`) only the parts of the pair the person changed in this page: `useInboxTheme().choose` marks the mode, Colors' `useInboxPalettes().choose` marks one half, so a pick sends `{ theme: { dark } }` (or `{ light }`, or `{ mode }`) and never the whole pair; with nothing marked (a store write the Inbox did not make) only the mode goes. The store hands over the whole pair, and a tab's copy of the parts the person did not touch can be days old or the defaults, so sending them would undo choices made in plan review since. Every other server-synced setting stays in cookies. config.json's `theme` beats the cookie, as in plan review, but is adopted once, from the first settings read, and per field (`adoptSavedTheme`): a part the person chose on that page first (the mode, or one half's palette; `chosenFields`, kept apart from the POST marks above) keeps the page's value and the other parts come from config.json, so a mode flipped before the read still takes the palettes plan review saved (and the pick still waiting to be sent is queued again after the read). WebMCP is off.
  - The toggle (`ThemeToggle` in `components/Sidebar.tsx`): an icon button at the far right of the Settings row, a sibling of the row's button (`.ib-setrow`), never inside it. It writes an explicit light or dark, the opposite of what is shown, named "Switch to light mode" / "Switch to dark mode" with the destination's sun or moon. It never opens Settings and never closes the phone sheet or the peek; the docked sidebar, the peek and the sheet all draw it. No keyboard shortcut.
  - Settings → Appearance: Light / Dark / Match system (native radios as one segmented control), the way back to following the OS.
  - Settings → Appearance → Colors (`components/Palettes.tsx`, the owner's request 2026-10-24): the palette for the light half and the dark half, plan review's Theme tab choices. ThemeTab's state is a hook of its own now, `useThemePaletteHalves` (`packages/ui/hooks/useThemePaletteHalves.ts`, re-exported by ThemeTab; `paletteSwatchColors` stays in `packages/ui/components/ThemeTab.tsx`), which ThemeTab draws and the Inbox draws in its own rows (`useInboxPalettes` in `theme.ts` wraps it with the half-only server write).
