A tab list in the segmented control's look, named by the row's "Colors" label, picks which half (tabs "Light half" / "Dark half", starting on the half on screen; ← → / Home / End select, one tab stop; each tab `aria-controls` the tile list), then that half's palettes are tiles in a native radio group named "Palettes for the light half" / "…dark half" (`.ib-pals`, five swatch dots, the name, a check; arrows move the pick, as native radios do), listed from the registry (`themesForHalf`: a dark-only palette is offered on the dark half only, and a new palette shows by itself). A pick applies at once (the `theme-<id>` class on `<html>`), is kept in the cookie and config.json, and plan review opens on it. Desktop: tiles at least 150 px wide in a box of 340 px that scrolls; a phone: two tiles a row, 44 px tall, the whole list in the page. In the Soft look (`--ib-soft-groups`) the tiles lose their outline and take the raised card's colour (`--ib-gr-tile-s`), and hover is `--ib-hover-s`; with every switch at `initial` they are as before. The row is last in Appearance because the list is long. A phone's list of sections names the General value as the mode, plus the palette on screen once either half is not the default (`Dark · Tokyo Night`). No keyboard shortcut. Proof: `packages/inbox/palettePicker.test.tsx` (DOM, the whole window over a real Inbox: a dark pick applied, only `{ dark }` posted, config.json keeping the light palette plan review set since, a reload keeping it; plan review's cookie shown, the light half, a mode change posting only the mode; config.json adopted; a mode flipped before the first settings read still adopting config.json's palettes, and its POST still going; two picks within the 300 ms debounce as one POST of that half, a light pick plus a mode change as `{ light, mode }`; arrows between tiles and between the tabs; the tile box scrolled to the chosen tile; the phone list value).
  - The one-time "themes" dialog (above, under the one-time dialogs) picks a theme for both halves and a mode through these same writers (`useInboxThemePick`), for someone who never picked a palette.
  - The switch lands in one frame, with no colour fade, as on every surface: see "Theme switch (instant, one place)" in [guides-and-shared-ui.md](guides-and-shared-ui.md#syntax-highlighting). Diagrams and code re-theme as in plan review.
  - Phones: the toggle is drawn at 40 px with a 44 px hit area (`::after`).
  - Proof: `packages/inbox/themeToggle.test.tsx` (DOM, the whole window over a real Inbox; each test sets its own cookie, store, config.json and OS start, including a stale tab whose palettes changed on disk and a click before the first settings read).
- On a phone (`PHONE_QUERY` in `packages/inbox/phone.ts`: under 768 px wide, or a touch screen under 500 px tall and under 1024 px wide, so a phone on its side too but not a short iPad window; every phone `@media` block in `inbox.css` spells the same query, and the landscape-only rules use its second half) the window shows one screen at a time, like a mail app:
  - The list's rows are `PhoneRow`, in the three-line row's order (approval "list-rows-mail-icons", 2026-10-30): line 1 (`.ib-m1`, approval "inbox-list-row-v2") is the agents' tiles and the project (the tiles alone in one project's list), then the ✓ and the time, on one line; line 2 (`.ib-m2`) is the title (14 px, 400; 16 px, 600 when unread), never cut: it wraps; line 3 (`.ib-m3`) is the grey preview, the guided review book (its icon alone), then the pin, the badge and the count (`?N`) at the right end; a pinned row with nothing else for line 3 keeps its pin on line 1 before the time, so it grows no line for the pin alone (`tests/e2e/inbox-row-pin.spec.ts` proves the pin's place on every row shape). The preview is cut first; a long project ends in "…" on its one line (the 320 px case in `tests/e2e/inbox-row-badges.spec.ts`). A phone never draws the wide row or the three-line row.
  - **The sidebar sheet, once open** (approval "inbox-phone-sidebar-sheet", "Sheet 1 · Same sections, fixed", 2026-11-10; `.product/approved/inbox-phone-sidebar-sheet/`, not in this repo; `components/Sidebar.tsx`, `components/PinnedRow.tsx`, the last block of `inbox.css`). The ☰, how the sheet opens and closes, and the list are unchanged, and so is the desktop sidebar: every CSS rule is under `.ib-sb-sheet-in` (the sheet's own box in `shell/sidebar.tsx`, which exists only on a phone) and every DOM change is behind `usePhone()`.
    - **Width**: `min(100vw - 40px, 360px)`, so 350 px at 390 with a 40 px strip of the list to tap closed; a window of 360 px or less gets the whole width. The head always has a 44 px **Close** button (`[data-sheet-close]`) after the real mark, the only way out at 320.
    - **One scroll** (`.ib-sidescroll`): the top stack's rows (Inbox, Board, Artifacts, Drafts…) are drawn inside it on a phone, above Starred, Agents and Projects; `.ib-side` itself no longer scrolls. The head, the "Showing only" line and the foot (the notices, Usage, then the foot row) stay put.
    - **Headings**: each section is one block (`.ib-sheet-sec`, or Agents' own `.ib-agentsec`) and its heading row (`.ib-secrow`; Starred's gets one on a phone) is `position: sticky`, with a hairline while stuck (`data-stuck`, set on scroll). The heading shows the section's count beside its name (`[data-section-total]`: the stars, the projects shown; Agents has none, by its own approval), then the chevron; a folded Starred adds "N waiting". The fold is `sidebarSections.ts`'s cookie, as everywhere. Add agent, Add machine and the projects' order are 44 px buttons in the row after the heading, never over it. Every heading sits 8 px under what is above it, folded or not.
    - **Rows**: names wrap to two lines, then end in "…". A starred row is two lines: the name, then its project and its state in words (the Stopped and Holds pills, "2 questions" or "1 question", "New", "Archived"; nothing when nothing waits). A starred thread that is also an agent's thread stays in both sections: the mock's "only under Starred" rule was not approved.
    - **"Showing only"** (`[data-sheet-showing]`): while the list is narrowed to a project or a machine, a filled line above the scroll names it in full, with **Clear** (the Inbox row's action: everything, and the sheet closes). The filtered row keeps its fill.
    - **The foot row**: Settings, Feedback and light/dark in one row of 44 px targets (`.ib-setrow[data-sheet-foot]`) while Feedback and Settings are both in the bottom stack; it wraps under a large text size. **Search and New message are not in the sheet** (`drawnSidebarItems` leaves both out on a phone, so Customize does not offer them there): the list's header has them.
    - Tests: `packages/inbox/phoneSidebarSheet.test.tsx` (DOM), the starred-and-agent case in `packages/inbox/agents/agentsSection.test.tsx`; e2e `inbox-sidebar-spacing.spec.ts`, `inbox-projects-sort.spec.ts`, `inbox-pins.spec.ts`, `inbox-search.spec.ts`.
  - A thread covers the list. The list stays mounted under it, hidden, so it keeps its scroll. The header (approval `inbox-thread-header`, 2026-11-02) is `‹ Inbox` (or the project), the title, Artifacts, Expand and ⋯ More at the right edge, where the close was. The read actions and Snooze are not in the bar (nor on a desktop since 2026-11-11): the ⋯ sheet leads with every read action and Snooze (see "The window" in `inbox-messages.md`).
  - **A phone's thread header** stays on one line with nothing cut (owner, 2026-10-29: "Mobile thread header needs to be better responsive"; his iPhone read "‹ I…" and a sliver of the subject), and gives its title the room the read button left (approval `inbox-thread-header`, 2026-11-02; the title is the thread's name by the list's rule, shown at once, else the subject once the body's heading has scrolled away, as on a desktop, see "The window." in `inbox-messages.md`; it used to vanish when short of room). Below, "the subject" is that title. Its icons keep 44 px. A way fits when nothing is past the edge, the way back's label shows whole (or 56 px of a long project name) and the subject has 88 px or more. Short of that it tries, in order, and keeps the first that fits (`packages/inbox/headFit.ts`, measured before paint and on each resize, font load and text size change; `data-fit` on `.ib-phead`): the way back's end padding tightens (`tight`), the Artifacts count leaves the button (`count`), and last the way back shows its chevron alone, named "Back to Inbox" (`back`). "Inbox" shows whole or not at all; a long project name may end in "…" once 56 px of it shows. When no way leaves the subject 88 px, the subject is not drawn (`data-subject-off`, never a sliver) and the first way that fits without it is kept. Keyboard hints are never drawn on a phone. With 11 artifacts and a waiting question at text size 14 every width from 320 to 430 px keeps "Inbox", the count and the subject. A desktop or stacked header is untouched. Proof: `headFit.test.ts`, `threadHeader.test.tsx` (the subject at 320 and 390 px), `tests/e2e/inbox-phone-thread-header.spec.ts` (320 to 430 px, text 14 and 15, from the list and from a project). A file covers the thread in the one full-screen mode (see "Attachments and annotation"): its toolbar sits bottom right with 44 px targets (‹ Back to the thread, the pen, the comment count, ⋯, Hide tools), the count opens the annotation panel (ui `presentation="embedded"`) as a bottom sheet, and Hide tucks the toolbar into a 44 × 52 px handle you tap.
  - New message's sessions and the list of comments are sheets from the bottom edge. Primary targets are 44 px. Inputs are 16 px, so iOS does not zoom. Edges clear `env(safe-area-inset-*)`. `trackKeyboard()` keeps `--ib-vvh` at the height the keyboard leaves, so the reply foot sits on the keyboard. It measures the keyboard against `document.documentElement.clientHeight`, never `window.innerHeight`: on iOS 26, once Safari has panned to the field, `innerHeight` reads the visual viewport's height, so the keyboard read as none, the window went back to its full height and the whole page panned (the owner, 0.28.42: "the top and bottom of the entire app are like scrollable"). On iOS or iPadOS only (`isIOSWebKit`: an iPhone, iPad or iPod user agent, or iPadOS naming itself a Mac with touch points), where Safari pans the whole page under the keyboard whatever the page says, the page is also pinned while the keyboard is up (`data-ib-keyboard-pin`): `html` is `--ib-vvh` tall with `overflow: hidden` and `overscroll-behavior: none`, a pan of the page is reset on the next frame, and a one-finger drag that starts on something with nothing to scroll (a header, a toolbar, a short draft) is refused, decided once per gesture; the thread, the list, a wide code block and an overflowing draft scroll as before. Since Safari no longer pans to what the keyboard covers, the reply foot shrinks and scrolls in its own box with the row that holds Send held at its bottom (the open box's own row, `.ib-rbox > .ib-rrow`, else the closed foot's buttons; attached files, picks, notices and the error line scroll above it; the skill picker is portalled out of it, see inbox-compose.md), and drops its home-bar gap. Under 400 px left (`tight`: an iPhone's ~257 px in portrait, ~140 on its side), the thread's padding also closes and the reply's words scroll past a third of the visible height. A field in the thread itself (an action item's note) takes the room: the foot steps aside while it has focus. Android is unchanged: Chrome resizes only the visual viewport, and nothing there is pinned or guarded. Known limit: a drag inside an iframe (an HTML attachment) never reaches the guard. Proof: `packages/inbox/phone.test.ts` (the iOS 26 readings against a fake window: the keyboard read, a pan undone, no pin on Android, unpinned on a return to the tab and on a rotation) and `tests/e2e/inbox-ios-keyboard-shell.spec.ts` (WebKit as an iPhone 15 in portrait and on its side, with files and a pick and an action note; Chromium as a Pixel 7 and at 1440 px unchanged). The page canvas (`html` and `body`) is `--card` on a phone, the colour of the reply foot and the phone sheets: iOS 26 Safari shows about 100 px past the visual viewport above the keyboard and paints it with the canvas, so a `--background` canvas was a dark band there (proof: `tests/e2e/inbox-phone-canvas.spec.ts`). The desktop keeps `body`'s `--background`. A fixed or top-layer box keeps the layout viewport's height when the keyboard comes up (iOS does not resize it), so every phone surface that holds a field ends where `--ib-vvh` ends, with `bottom: calc(100% - var(--ib-vvh, 100%))` or `height: var(--ib-vvh, …)`: a file in full screen (its composer's Comment and Reply, the image's comment row and the toolbar ride on the keyboard; the image or page shrinks above), the comments sheet in landscape, the sheets from the bottom edge (the annotations list's edit field), Decision mode, the Board's New card and the Feedback sheet. With the keyboard up (`data-ib-keyboard` on the root) the full-screen composer drops its home-bar gap. Proof: `tests/e2e/inbox-fullscreen-keyboard.spec.ts` (WebKit and Chromium at 390 by 844, the keyboard simulated by shrinking the visual viewport).
  - `.pn-inbox[data-view]` names the screen on top (`list`, `thread` once loaded, `file`, or the page).
  - History is a stack on a phone (`packages/inbox/history.ts`). Each screen's entry records the screen it was opened from. On a phone, Back, Escape and close are the browser's Back when they lead to that screen, so the iOS swipe and Android's back agree with them. A second press before that Back lands does nothing, so a double tap never leaves the Inbox. Otherwise, and always on a desktop, they push the screen as a new entry, so the browser's Back reopens what was closed, as before.
  - On its side, the Comments sheet takes the file's height. A file has no stepping bar or reply strip on a phone: ⋯ and the sheet's foot hold the position, and the sheet's foot says what rides the next Send with Write the reply.
  - The toast spans the window above what sits at its bottom, whatever its height: the open thread's foot (an open reply box too), a full-screen file's bar, corner control, name chip and captions (`usePhoneFloor` in `components/Toast.tsx` measures them while the toast is up and again whenever one appears, goes or changes size, so a thread opened under a toast raised elsewhere moves it; the image and video composers place it in CSS). One 44 px line when it fits, else a lead line and a detail line (see "How long words fit" in `inbox-messages.md`); "Read" in place of "Marked read" (short phone leads only where the lead would not fit 160 px), Undo with a 44 px target, no Z hint, and no Reply on a touch phone (the foot has it; a narrow desktop window keeps Reply); an error wraps to at most four lines.
  - Small controls keep their look and take a 44 px tall hit area (an `::after` box): the reply box's Keep open / Skip them, and the file's toolstrip, Global comment and Copy file (ui). ui's toolstrip buttons clip their sliding label with `overflow`, so on a phone they clip with a `clip-path` that reaches 8 px above and below and 4 px at the sides instead (drawn the same, the keyboard focus ring whole). Side by side controls cannot all be 44 px wide; they keep their widths.
  - Proved in WebKit (iPhone 15) and Chromium (Pixel 7) by `tests/e2e/inbox-mobile.spec.ts`. The surface the native apps host (`packages/inbox/surface`) is a different thing and is not affected.
- In a window too narrow for two panes that is not a phone (an iPad upright, a narrow desktop window, a Stage Manager tile; `packages/inbox/stack.ts`), the pane on top covers the one under it:
  - A thread covers the list when the list's 380 px column and a 440 px thread (the column a thread keeps beside an open file) do not both fit in `.ib-main` (the window less the docked sidebar's target width). With the default sidebar that is under about 1052 px, so an iPad on its side (1080) keeps the list beside a 468 px thread, as iPad Mail does, and an iPad upright (810) stacks.
  - An open file covers its thread, full screen, when the thread's 440 px column and a 480 px file do not fit the window (under 920 px; 944 with the Soft panes' 8 px gutters).
  - `.pn-inbox[data-stack]` holds `thread` and/or `file`, measured before paint when the route or the sidebar changes, and by a ResizeObserver otherwise.
  - The desktop chrome stays: the docked sidebar, the wide list rows, the desktop headers (a stacked thread's leads with `‹ Inbox`, or the project, in place of its close; an open file is full screen, its toolbar led by Back to the thread in place of Side by side). The list stays mounted under a stacked thread, at its own width, so it keeps its scroll.
  - Focus follows the screen: a thread that covers the list takes focus to `‹ Inbox`, and closing it puts focus back on the row of the thread just closed.
  - A resize across the stacking width never drops focus to the page. Shrinking with a thread open while a row of the list has focus hides that row under the pane, so focus goes to `‹ Inbox` and Back returns it to that row (a hidden row cannot hold focus). With only the list on screen the row keeps it. Growing back out, with the pane's Back focused, puts focus on the row beside it (the one focus came from, else the open thread's).
  - History: closing a stacked thread back to the list is the browser's Back when it was opened from there (`closeTo`, as on a phone), so an iPad's swipe back and `‹ Inbox` agree. Every other close, and every close in the two-pane layout, pushes a new entry as before.
  - A window at 1280 px and over with the default sidebar never stacks, so it is drawn exactly as before (a pixel comparison against the previous build shows no change). Proved on an iPad upright (WebKit, 810 by 1080, touch), a 900 px and a 1000 px Chromium window and an iPad on its side (1080) by `tests/e2e/inbox-mobile.spec.ts`, and the widths by `packages/inbox/stack.test.ts`.

**Questions and Send.**
- A row opens the thread beside a narrowed list and records a look.
- The thread reads as email: the subject, the sender row, and each message through `Viewer` with the `answerOnly` seam. There are no comments on the text and no document actions, and the question cards keep their handlers.
- A click on a card is a pick, saved at once (`POST /api/inbox/messages/:id/picks`, serialized per thread, a stale revision `409 question_revision_conflict`). The card's footer says "Picked 10:51 AM · in your draft" (its tag "Draft"), "Sent 10:52 AM", "Skipped · 10:52 AM" (the person pressed Skip them; see "Mark read and the Send that skips") or "Closed unanswered when you marked it read · 10:52 AM". "Waiting on this answer · Holds up N" sits under the asking message.
- Only an open or picked question can be answered: a card whose question is sent, closed (a Mark read, the archive) or superseded draws read-only (no ticks, no Accept recommended, no Skip), even while another question in the same message is open (`answerLocked` in `Thread.tsx`, through `Viewer`'s `questionReadOnly`; proof `packages/inbox/sentQuestionLocked.test.tsx`).
- The foot:
  - **Its line shows only while thread text scrolls under it** (approval "inbox-reaction-tray", addendum 2026-11-07, "bottom bar B1"). The foot's top rule and a faint 18 px shadow above it fade in over 160 ms while some of the thread is below the view (`data-under` on `.ib-pfoot`, from `useTextUnderFoot` in `packages/inbox/footLine.ts`: read on scroll, when the body resizes and when one of its children does). The thread all fits, or it is scrolled to its end: no line, the foot sits on the page. A colour fade with no movement, so reduced motion keeps it. The actions come first (Send, Reply or Edit draft, the reaction, an ended session's two buttons), then what is open (the open questions, then the checklist) right after them at the buttons' usual 8 px gap, in that DOM and tab order. Nothing pushes the open things to the bar's far end: no auto margin, and the buttons group does not stretch on a computer (the owner, 2026-11-08, undoing #2860's right-end group: "I want them next to the button. It was always more ergonomic for me to have that right next to it. I'm not looking out that far right."). Same on a phone, where an ended session's two buttons take a line of their own under the row and so come last in the markup (no CSS `order`: Tab and a screen reader follow the screen). The onboarding tour's foot never carries `data-under`, so it draws no line. Proof: `packages/inbox/footLine.test.tsx` (the line, and no auto margin or stretch), `packages/inbox/endedSession.test.tsx` (order, computer and phone).
  - "Send · Edit draft · N open questions … Draft · 2 picks" when picks, comments or kept words wait (see "Reply drafts and the Draft label"; approvals "thread-foot-status-right", option A, and "reply-bar-pairs", option A, 2026-11-05): the buttons stay on the left in today's order and the draft status sits at the row's right end, after the buttons in the DOM (Send and Edit draft name it in `aria-describedby`). Each thing is counted once: the open questions button counts only the questions still unanswered (open, nothing picked) and the open action items; a drafted answer counts under Draft ("N picks"). The skip choice is no longer in the foot: it is the first row of the open questions list (see "Skipping" in `inbox-messages.md`). Before the row gives up its one line it shortens, one step at a time (the owner, 2026-10-25: "We can make the text shorter before we start multi-lining buttons"; `fitWholeFootRow` in `packages/inbox/footFit.ts`, tried on the page in a layout effect in `Thread.tsx`, again on a width change and when fonts load): the keycaps, then "Edit", then the step words, then the open questions button's icon and count, then the pick words after the Draft pill go while the comment count stays, as a comment glyph and the number inside the pill ("Draft 💬3", aria-hidden; with no comments "Draft" alone; `DraftFootNote` keeps the pick words and the comment count in spans of their own, and Send's description stays "Draft, 1 pick, 3 comments"), then tighter spacing (the status's gap too); each step is a token of the row's `data-fit` (`keys edit steps oq draft tight`, `FOOT_ROW_STEPS`), and the words a step drops are `.ib-fl` spans that leave only the eye (inbox.css), so the accessible names and Send's and Edit draft's descriptions keep the full words ("Edit draft", "Draft, 1 pick, 1 comment"); `textContent` is not exact once step 4 can show: it also holds the pill's aria-hidden comment count ("Draft, 1 1 pick, 1 comment"); the open questions button then draws the row's form (`rowForm`) instead of fitting itself. Measured, every layout from a 390 px phone to a 1440 window keeps one row, the 440 px thread beside a file included (`tests/e2e/inbox-thread-foot.spec.ts`). Only where even the shortest form does not fit does the status take its own line ABOVE the buttons, never between them, in full words: two flex items, `flex-wrap: wrap-reverse` with `space-between` (`[data-foot-row]` > `[data-foot-buttons]` + `[data-foot-status]`). The error after a failed Send renders above the button row (`data-foot-error="above"`), in the closed foot and the open reply box.
  - On a phone the foot is three pairs, "Send · ✎ Draft N · ? N open ⌄" (approval "reply-bar-pairs", option A): no status at the right. Edit draft and the Draft pill are one button: the pencil, "Draft" and the count of picks plus comments plus action replies (`data-draft-count`, aria-hidden), named "Draft 2: 1 pick, 1 comment. Edit draft" (its name starts with what it shows, WCAG 2.5.3; `unsentWords`); with only kept words it reads "Draft" with no count. Send's and Edit draft's `aria-describedby` still read "Draft, 1 pick, 1 comment" and, when questions stay open, "3 questions stay open when you send" (hidden spans). The open questions button counts the unanswered ones ("1 open question", "3 open", "3"; the word "open" goes under 360 px) and, after Skip them in its list, reads "N to skip" with the skip glyph, N being the questions Skip them counted (a question that lands afterwards stays open; the name: "3 questions skipped when you send, 1 open action item"). Every button is 44 px tall; Send stays first and filled. With every listed question answered in the draft (and no action item) the questions button is its icon alone, "? ⌄" (no count, never "0"), named "Questions, all answered in your draft"; its list (and select mode's Unskip) stays in reach and Q opens it. The keys are unchanged (Mod+Enter, R, Q). Proof: `packages/inbox/openQuestions.test.tsx`, `packages/inbox/threadFoot.test.tsx` and `tests/e2e/inbox-thread-foot.spec.ts` (390 and 320, one line, 44 px, names, order).
  - The reply box with its "N picks" and "N comments" chips. With no comments there is no comments chip (the owner, 2026-11-02: "not sure if worth it to even show 0 comments, it takes up space"); a list open as its last comment goes stays until it closes itself. Proof: `packages/inbox/replyBoxTyping.test.tsx`. Mod+Enter sends.
  - **Typing stays in the box** (the owner, 2026-11-02, iPhone: "Poor responsiveness in reply box"). The box's words live in a small store (`packages/inbox/replyText.ts`), not in `ThreadPane`'s state: only the field (`ReplyTextField`) reads them on every key, the pane reads whether there are words (`useReplyHasWords`), full screen's composer reads them only while shown, and Send, the draft and the stale guard read `replyText.get()` when they act. A key re-renders the field, not the thread (1,175 components a key before on a 13-message thread, with every message body). Keep it so: a new reader of the words in `ThreadPane` uses `get()` or a selector hook, never `useReplyText`. CSS: no `X:has(…) > *` or `X:has(…) > :not(…)` in `inbox.css`. Chromium merges what follows every `:has()` into one invalidation set, so one such rule made each key (the textarea's new text node) restyle the whole page (3,500 elements, 35 ms a key at 4x CPU). `packages/inbox/hasInvalidation.test.ts` fails on that shape. Proof: `replyBoxTyping.test.tsx` (a key re-renders no message body; one draft save after the keys stop). The answers picked on the cards are listed live inside the box under the words (`ReplyAnswers`, `packages/inbox/components/ReplyAnswers.tsx`: "Your 3 answers go with this reply", one row per answer, `Q2 <prompt> → Yes`, a saved pick or one being saved, which the "N picks" chip counts too; a cut prompt or answer shows whole in a `Tip`; answers to several messages are grouped under each message, "go with your replies"; a row jumps to its card; on a phone about two and a half 44 px rows, then it scrolls), so a pick made while the person writes shows at once. They are never written into the words: Reply / Edit draft opens the box with the kept draft or nothing (until 0.28.25 it seeded a one-time "Prompt: answer." summary of the picks, which went stale as the person picked on and reached the agent twice, beside the answers section; the owner, 2026-10-21, "When i edit a reply, and then add new answers... do they get added to the reply? Because I dont see them"). Proof: `packages/inbox/picksWhileComposing.test.tsx`. Its chips sit in the box's own row (`ReplyRow`, approval "inbox-reply-box", decision D4) and never wrap: a chip never shrinks or breaks its label, and where the chips do not fit beside the recipient pill they fold into the one summary line (a phone always shows the line, on its own line over the row). The fit is held while the person is in the cell (a chip, the line, or a list or sheet open from one), so nothing is drawn anew under them. Before this, in the 440 px thread beside a file, "3 annotations" broke over lines and spilled out of the box. `tests/e2e/inbox-attachments.spec.ts` checks the chips or the line stay inside the box at 440 px, 1440, 810, 900, 390 and 320. The no-wrap applies to the chips only, never to the list of comments (`.ib-annpop`), whose quotes and comments wrap; where that list would run past the window's right edge it lines up with the reply box's right edge (`--ib-annpop-shift`, set by `AnnotationsChip`); the same e2e checks both at every width.
  - **The list of comments** (`AnnotationsList` in `AnnotationsChip.tsx`; approval `inbox-notes-count-list`, 2026-11-01; feedback: "I can't seem to find a button to display the notes or edit them"): every comment going with the next Send, with Edit and Remove, reached without opening the box. Four ways in, one state (`commentsList` in `Thread.tsx`): the reply box's chip; the closed foot's "N comments" off a phone (`DraftFootNote`: a quiet link, and the pill's count at the foot's step 4, where the words are clipped and leave the Tab order; a phone's foot has no status since "reply-bar-pairs", so there it is the box's foot line, a highlight or the card's chip); a press on a highlight in a message, which opens it at that comment and shows the highlight selected (a click on `.ib-body`, because on a touch screen the highlighter does not listen); and the full-screen card's chip. On a desktop it opens over the messages above the foot, or above the card; on a phone it is the bottom sheet, ending at the keyboard's top (`--ib-vvh`). It is never a child of the card: `.ib-fcomp`'s backdrop filter would hold the fixed sheet inside the card (`.ib-annat`). It moves nothing behind it and takes focus without scrolling; its header stays as the list scrolls; Escape or its × closes it with focus back on what opened it (a highlight: the foot's count; on a phone, whose foot has none, the highlight itself, focusable by script only: `tabindex="-1"`, `closeFootList`), and a press outside closes it where the press lands. With the last comment gone, focus goes to the reply field, on a phone's card to the card (the field would raise the keyboard), and stays put if the list did not have it (a Send that took them). A press that ends in a selection (a drag, a double or triple click) is the comment toolbar's and closes the list. Words half written in the list are held with the list (`commentEdit` in `Thread.tsx`), so however it closes they come back when it opens; only Escape, on the edit itself, drops them. Nothing opens it while the foot shows something else (the stale guard, compose, the route choice: `listHome`), and those take it away. From inside an HTML page a click closes it through the window's blur and Escape through the ladder's `popover` rung. A row's name in the card's list on a comment on a message's text goes back to the thread first. The word is "comments" in this list, these counts, their errors and the stale guard; the file drawer's strip and the file panel keep "annotations". A highlight or an HTML page's pin also leaves the file when its comment leaves the records another way than the pane's own Remove (a Send, this list, another window: `listedIds` in `AttachmentPane.tsx`). Known limit: while a Send is on its way the list still opens and its Remove still works, which only shows on a slow link. Proof: `tests/e2e/inbox-comments-list.spec.ts` (Chromium) and `tests/e2e/inbox-comments-list-webkit.spec.ts` (iPhone in WebKit).
- A Send is `POST /api/inbox/messages/:id/reply`, ONE per agent session it answers (owner approval "inbox-explicit-skip", 2026-10-21: "answering questions across messages should just become one reply"), with an idempotency key per (Send, session), kept for a retry. Before it, a Send posted one reply per message, so an agent that asked across 8 messages woke on the first reply and acted before it saw the rest. `sendTargetsOf` (`packages/inbox/sendTargets.ts`) groups the picks waiting, and the questions chosen with "Skip them", by the session of their message (`author.host` + `author.session`; a message with no session is a reply of its own); each group posts to the latest of that session's messages it touches, and every pick or skip on another of its messages names it: `questions: [{ message_id?, key, revision, answer? }]`, `skip: [key | { message_id?, key }]` (`message_id` absent = the route's message). The words, annotations and pasted files ride the reply of the session that wrote the latest agent message (that group goes last), or a last reply to the latest agent message when that session has nothing else to send. The store (`sendReply`) checks each `message_id` (`sendMessageOf`: the same thread and the same agent session as the reply's message, else `422 validation_error`; a message the archive closed, `409 thread_resolved`), plans each message's answers with its own revisions (a stale one refuses the whole reply, nothing written), and writes ONE person reply `reply_to` the route's message, marking every question it sends with that reply's id. Re-ask's picks-only reply (`reaskTargets`' `picks`) is one reply the same way. The reply's body, in this order: its first line (`Answered N questions.`, `Skipped N questions.` or `Answered N questions, skipped M.`, counted across every message by the answers section's own rule, so it never disagrees with "x of y answered"), the person's words, the still-open line, the answers section, then the annotations' feedback. The answers section is `formatQuestionAnswersSection` for the one message the reply answers; when the answers are on several messages, or on another message than the one replied to, it is one "Answers to your questions" section with a `### Your message <message id>` heading per message, in thread order, each with its own "x of y answered" line and its questions numbered as its cards are (`composeInboxReplyBody`'s `messages` and `replyTo`, `packages/core/inbox-questions.ts`), so the agent can tell which "Q1" is which. The still-open line (approval "inbox-words-only-reply", 2026-10-13, extended by "inbox-explicit-skip"): counts every question the replied-to message's session asked in this thread that is still open, or picked and not sent, leaving out the ones this reply sends (another session's questions are never counted, and a message with no session counts only its own); none when nothing is left open. A reply that answers or skips something says "You have N other questions open in this thread. They stay open; the person will answer them in the Inbox. Don't re-ask them." (`inboxOtherStillOpenLine`; for one: "You have 1 other question open in this thread. It stays open; …"). A reply that sends no answer and no skip (words, annotations, or both) keeps its own wording, with no first line and no answers section: "No questions answered yet; all N are still open. Don't re-ask them; the person will answer in the Inbox." (`inboxStillOpenLine`; for one: "No question answered yet; it is still open. Don't re-ask it; …"; when an earlier reply already answered some: "No new answers in this reply; N questions are still open. …"). `INBOX_STILL_OPEN_LINE` matches every form, and the folded line's `replyWords` cuts the reply there, so a snippet shows only the person's words; `withoutAnswersSection` (read_thread's earlier messages) cuts the grouped section too, and search's `inboxSearchReplyText` cuts at the still-open line as `replyWords` does. A retried Send keeps each reply's key unless its words, files or comments changed or it carries a pick or skip the last try did not (`replyKeysToRenew` in `sendTargets.ts`), so a new answer is never dropped by a replay. When the chosen session ends mid-Send, the route's error counts agents: "N of M agents got their reply". The wake text is unchanged and carries this body. Plan review's export (`formatQuestionAnswersSection`, `packages/core/question-block.ts`) is untouched.
- The sent questions are marked before the reply line lands. When the reply line never lands, they read as picked again, so a retry never reads "already sent" with nothing sent.
- **The person's own sent reply, in their words** (owner approval 2026-10-24, the onboarding audit's finding 7; display only). The thread draws a person's reply as their words, then a short answers list ("You answered 2 questions, skipped 1", one row per answer, `Q1 <prompt> → <answer>`, a skip marked, grouped under the asking message when the reply answered several; a row shows its card), then their comments' feedback and files as before (`SentAnswers` in `components/ReplyAnswers.tsx`, `sentReply.ts`). The answers come from the questions themselves (`sent_reply_id` names the reply that carried each); the agent-facing parts of the body (the first line "Answered N questions.", the still-open line, the "## Answers to your questions" section) come out through core's `splitInboxReplyBody`, which cuts the section only when it follows its grammar exactly (`inboxAnswersSectionEnd`, also behind the server's `withoutAnswersSection`) and whose words match what the folded line (`replyWords`) and search read for a body the store wrote (those two keep their own, looser rule). A body the store did not shape, an Inbox note, a Re-ask note or an action report is drawn as before, and so is a reply whose section is there but whose answers cannot be listed. The stored body and what agents receive are unchanged. Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/reply-display.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/sentReply.test.tsx`.
- After a Send, the line under the person's last reply or message says where it is (`deliveryLine`, `@plannotator/core/inbox-delivery-line`, re-exported at `packages/inbox/deliveryLine.ts`; the owner's request 2026-10-16, "it should say this message is queued up … and then maybe an option just to send it immediately"). For a session whose connection wakes it (a reply to a message with `wakes`, or a message with `to`), the window reads that session's connection every 3 s (`GET /api/inbox/threads/:id/sessions?asker=<session>`, the additive `asker.connection` = `{ busy, idle_since, send_now, waits }` while it polls, null when it does not): "Undelivered 3 min · Claude Code is working · goes in by itself in 2 min" with **Send now** (`data-send-now`, only while `send_now`; see the next bullet but one; "Queued · Claude Code is working; it gets this when its turn ends." in the few seconds before the server starts the reply's clock), "Sending to Claude Code…" while idle, "Queued for Claude Code; it gets this as its next turn." when the connection does not say (or an older Inbox), "… when its session is back." when it does not poll (also after Send now was pressed or the Inbox marked it: a session gone after the mark is never "Sending … now"), "Sending to Claude Code now…" once Send now was pressed (`deliver_now_at`) while the session polls. A reply to an agent that only reads the thread (MCP, OpenCode 1) reads "Saved for <agent>. It sees it when it next reads this thread." Then "Delivered to <agent>, 10:52 AM", unchanged. The ended-session and Not delivered lines are unchanged.
- **A reply a stopped Claude Code session holds** (owner approval "inbox-stopped-reply", 2026-11-06; `.product/approved/inbox-stopped-reply/`). After the person stops a Claude Code session, Claude Code refuses the mod's reply until they type there (#2780: the mod keeps it pending, see the Inbox wake in `claude-code-mod.md`). The mod says so with the poll and `state` field `held` (`INBOX_HELD_POLL_FIELD`; a poll without it, or `held: false` on a `state` event, clears it); the server keeps it per live session (`createInboxLiveSessions`, a change moves the rows' delivery signature) and carries it as the additive `connection.held: true` (the sessions route) and in `deliveryConnectionOf` (the rows). `inboxDeliveryState` reads it after `connection === null` and before Send now and busy: `state: "queued"`, `reason: "stopped"`, no Send now (nothing goes in before the person types). The thread's line, the row's tooltip and accessible name (`rowDeliverySpoken`) and the decisions strip (`AfterSend` `stopped`) all say "Stopped: goes in when you type in that session" (`INBOX_STOPPED_REPLY_TEXT`), where they would say Queued or Sending. Once the person types, the hold lifts and the reply waits and goes in as any reply does, then reads Delivered. No button, no setting. The poll is the authority and the `state` event only makes the change prompt, so a poll built just before a change can show the old line for about a second. Tests: `packages/inbox/deliveryLine.test.ts`, `packages/inbox/rowDelivery.test.ts`, `packages/inbox/decisionsModel.test.ts`, `apps/hook/hooks/mod/inbox-held-state.test.ts` (the mod's poll field and `state` events over a memory host), `packages/server/inbox-row-delivery.test.ts` (the poll and `state` field through the real routes), `apps/hook/hooks/mod/inbox-stopped-session.test.ts` (the mod against a real Inbox: Stopped while held, working after the person types, then Delivered).
- **Undelivered replies go in by themselves** (owner approval "inbox-busy-agent-reply", 2026-10-21, behavior and look; `.product/approved/inbox-busy-agent-reply/`). A reply, a message or a Re-ask note that waits on a BUSY session (never the note carrying a 👍, below) goes into the running turn by itself after `INBOX_AUTO_SEND_AFTER_MS` (5 min, `packages/core/inbox-delivery.ts`), exactly as if the person pressed Send now: `deliver_now_at` plus `deliver_now_by: "auto"` (a person's press writes `"person"`; on the message, or on a tag for a tagged session's own copy) and `wait_started_at` (when it began to wait on the busy session), and the bridge command's `now: true`, delivered into the turn per host as below. Only where Send now is possible (the session polls `busy: true` and `send_now: true`); elsewhere it keeps waiting and the line says why. **The clock is the server's, never the item's `created_at`** (`createInboxLiveSessions`' `trackWaits` in `packages/server/inbox-sessions.ts`, in memory): each item a session is handed (`pendingReplies`) gets `since` the first time the server sees that session busy while the item waits for it (the "Undelivered N min" number, row and band alike), and `autoSince` the first time it also sees the session able to take it mid-turn (`send_now`; the countdown "goes in by itself in N min" runs from it, so a session that becomes able after a `/reload-plugins` still gives the person the full 5 minutes). Clocks are looked at on every poll and `state` event, when a person's item is written, and every `INBOX_AUTO_SEND_SWEEP_MS` (5 s). A delivered item, or one moved to another session ("Send to another session", which also clears `deliver_now_at`, `deliver_now_by` and `wait_started_at` in `readdressMessage`), leaves the old session's clocks; a session that stops being live loses all its clocks; an Inbox restart starts every clock again. So a reply left for a session that ended, or a message moved to a busy session, waits its whole 5 minutes from when that session is busy, and the wait is never shorter than 5 minutes (a restart can make it longer). The sessions route carries the clocks as `connection.waits` (`{ [item id]: { since, auto_since } }`); the store reads them through `InboxDeliveryPresence.waitOf` for the rows. `InboxStore.autoSendDue(session, { afterMs, tags, clockOf })` marks every pending item whose `autoSince` is at least the wait ago and that has no `deliver_now_by` yet; the server calls it for every live busy `send_now` session on the sweep and on each such session's poll (`packages/server/inbox.ts`). Never twice: `deliver_now_by` stays when a failed attempt (`send_now_failed`) clears `deliver_now_at`, and the Inbox never tries again by itself (the person's Send now still can). Never for the Inbox's set-aside and reopened notes. **A 👍 waits for the turn to end** (owner's decision 2026-10-21, approval "inbox-busy-agent-reply": "a thumbs-up isn't worth interrupting a turn"): the note carrying a 👍 never goes in by itself. One switch, `INBOX_AUTO_SEND_ACK_NOTES` (`false`, `packages/core/inbox-delivery.ts`), decides it in the server's marking (`autoSendDue` skips `ack_of` notes), the row (`rowDelivery`: no `auto_send_at`) and the 👍 line (`ackPillLine`: no countdown) alike. **How it went in:** when the connection reports `delivered` for an item the Inbox marked, the server writes `delivery.into_turn` (additive): false when the session was seen idle after the mark (its own Ask this session question held the turn, so the item went in when the turn ended), true otherwise (`live.deliveredIntoTurn`). `startInboxServer` takes `autoSendAfterMs` / `autoSendSweepMs` (tests), `plannotator inbox` the testing knob `PLANNOTATOR_INBOX_AUTO_SEND_MS` (see the env table). The look (one rule for row and thread: `inboxDeliveryState`'s `wait` / `autoAfterMin` / `turnEnded`, inputs `waitingSince` (the server's `since`, after the mark `wait_started_at`), `autoSince`, `nowBy`, `nowAt`, `intoTurn`, `autoSendAfterMs`; the window gathers them with `waitInputs` in `deliveryLine.ts`): the row's additive `delivery.waiting_since`, `steerable`, `auto_send_at`, `auto_after_min`, `turn_ended`, and the sessions route's `connection.auto_send_after_ms` and `waits` (absent from an older Inbox: no countdown). A session that stops polling wins over a mark: the line reads "… when its session is back.", never "Sending … now" (`connection === null` is decided before `sendingNow`). The list row draws "Undelivered N min" in place of the count on a wide row in a list of 1150 px or more, and the short form "Undelivered Nm" on narrower wide rows, three-line and phone rows (approval "inbox-row-byline", option A; the full words in its tooltip and the row's name; `UndeliveredBadge`, `components/Undelivered.tsx`; blue `--ib-wait-*` while it can go in now, grey `data-stuck` when not; whole minutes, then whole hours from 60 minutes ("2 hours", "2h") and whole days from 24 hours ("1 day", "1d"), the board cards' age units, so the pill never outgrows its slot; "Undelivered" under a minute; words past the slot end in "…" inside the pill; the row's count cell keeps a 5 s clock, so a memoized row is not rendered again); the wide and three-line rows offer **Send now** on hover and keyboard focus only (`data-row-send-now`, a sibling of the row like Mark read; never on a phone row). Under the reply the band (`UndeliveredBand`): "Undelivered 3 min · Claude Code is working · goes in by itself in 2 min" ("in under a minute"; "it gets this when its turn ends" when it will not) with a filled Send now; grey with no button and no countdown when it cannot go in sooner, then why and the fix (`StuckReason` in `RestartNote.tsx`: the older-plugin sentence with `/reload-plugins` and Copy, the update steps, else "It can't go in sooner."). Tagged sessions' lines say the same; a 👍's line says how long it waited and "it gets this when its turn ends", with no countdown and no Send now. After: "Delivered to Claude Code, 1:53 PM · went into its turn by itself after 5 min" (`data-auto`, the delivered tick's colour) only when it went into the running turn; "Delivered to Claude Code, 1:53 PM · went in when its turn ended" (`TURN_ENDED_TEXT`) when `into_turn` is false. "Not delivered" for a gone session is unchanged. No toast, no shortcut. Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/store-auto-send.test.ts` (injected clock: never without a clock, not before, at 5 min, once, the countdown from `autoSince`, a failed attempt, a move to another session, notes, the 👍 switch, tags, the row, `turn_ended`, a session gone after the mark), `packages/server/inbox-auto-send.test.ts` (the server's timer with a short wait: the poll's `now`, never without `send_now` or for an idle session, a restart starting the clock again, a reply left for an ended session, a message moved to a busy session, `into_turn`), `apps/hook/server/inbox-auto-send-knob.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/undelivered.test.tsx`, `tests/e2e/inbox-undelivered.spec.ts` (compiled binary: the badges and hover, the bands with the page's clock moved forward, going in by itself with a one-minute knob across a restart, the live timer; 1440 light and dark, a phone).
- **Send now** (`POST /api/inbox/messages/:id/send-now`, window guards; door 7.58): the store writes `deliver_now_at` on the message (`requestDeliverNow`; a second press writes nothing; `already_delivered`, `not_addressed`), after the server checked the recipient's connection polls now and said it can deliver into a running turn (`send_now_unavailable` otherwise; `recipientOf` names the session). The Inbox writes the same mark by itself (`deliver_now_by: "auto"`) for a reply, message or Re-ask note that waited 5 minutes on a busy session that can take it (see "Undelivered replies go in by themselves"); never for the note carrying a 👍, which waits for the turn to end (owner's decision 2026-10-21). The bridge command carries `now: true` only on a poll that says `send_now` (so a connection that cannot steer never skips its idle wait), and a held poll answers at once. Once a reply carries `now`, the connection delivers the waiting replies at once, OLDEST FIRST (an urgent reply never overtakes an older one to the same session): the Claude Code mod as a user row in the running turn (`$.session.append`, the one way a plugin puts words into a running turn: `$.prompt.submit` never folds into one); Pi as `sendUserMessage(..., { deliverAs: "steer" })`, delivered when Pi starts it (`message_start`); OpenCode 2 as `session.prompt({ delivery: "steer" })`, delivered only on `session.inbox.delivered` for that row id (a `session.inbox.delivery.changed` to `queue` for it counts too: OpenCode will deliver it); when the run ends first and the row is not delivered within `STEER_IDLE_GRACE_MS` (3 s after the end event), it is sent again as a queued turn ending with `inboxRepeatLine`. There is no cap while the run goes on (a tool call may take minutes, and OpenCode promotes the row at the run's next step): a steer still waiting is looked at every `STEER_WATCH_MS` (2 s), a run with no event for `RUNNING_CROSS_CHECK_MS` (20 s) is checked with `session.wait` (idle: the grace starts), and after the event stream ends the row waits until `session.wait` says idle, then counts as delivered when `session.context` holds it (by its id, or a user message with its text), else the queued turn goes. OpenCode cannot withdraw the steer row and promotes it with that turn (#1734, live on 2.0.22), so the plugin's `context` and `generate` hooks drop the later of the two copies from every model request (`dropInboxDuplicates`, the way they drop the session-URL notice; pairs kept for the plugin's life, at most 200); a later copy that is its message's whole content is never removed (the request would end on an assistant message: a wasted turn or a provider error) but replaced by `inboxRepeatPointer`, the same text in every request so the prompt prefix stays stable; idle, as a turn at once. Pi: Esc during the run puts a steer that has not started into the editor (Pi's `restoreQueuedMessagesToEditor`); when the steer's watchdog then finds the session idle without it, the extension takes the reply's exact text back out of the editor (`editorWithout`, the person's own words and other queued messages kept; an edited copy is left alone) and the reply waits for idle and arrives as a follow-up, so it is never sent twice. Pi and OpenCode never steer into this session's own Ask question either: their wake target's `canSteer` is false while it runs (Pi: `isAskActive`; OpenCode 2: `isAskTurnActive`, a per-session mark the session bridge holds while its Ask turn is in progress), so the poll and the wake's `state` events (`InboxWake` now carries `send_now` on them, as the mod does) stop offering Send now and a pressed reply waits for idle. The mod: the decision is taken right before the append, after every await of the delivery (fresh check, lease, claim), from `steerableTurn` = the running MAIN turn that is not this session's own Ask question (so an Inbox reply never streams into the Ask AI panel; while the Ask question runs or waits queued, Send now waits for idle) and not a turn another plugin's prompt started (`TurnTracker.isOtherPluginTurn`, see "Ask this session" in the mod section: that plugin may read the answer as its own; Send now and auto-send wait for idle then; engine-started turns, a task notification or a scheduled trigger, still take it, as before); a turn that ended by then means a turn instead; after the append, a turn that ended or changed means one short pointer turn at once (`inboxSentNowFollowUpText`), else the row is tied to that turn id and a turn that ends with no model request after it gets the pointer (several per turn). A refused append (or a failed steer on Pi or OpenCode) posts `send_now_failed`: the server clears `deliver_now_at` (`clearDeliverNow`), the line reads queued again, and the reply waits for idle. A connection says it can with the poll field `send_now: true` (`INBOX_SEND_NOW_POLL_FIELD`): the mod on an engine at least `SESSION_APPEND_MIN_ENGINE` (2.1.301, the first build whose published API documents `$.session.append`; decided from `$.session.version()`, because `claude plugin validate` refuses reading `$.session.append` as a value) until an engine refuses a row; Pi where the wake target has `steer`; OpenCode 2 while its event stream runs (`canSteer`, how a steer's delivery is seen). OpenCode 1 and older connections never, so Send now is never offered there. Nothing is ever aborted. Claim-once holds: the same claim folder and lease as a normal delivery; the mod refreshes `alive` for the claim it is submitting under, so another process never reports a reply this one is still delivering; a claim it won but cannot use at once (its own Ask question took the turn, a turn started right after the claim, or the append was refused) it gives back (`INBOX_RELEASE_SCRIPT`: the claim folder removed while still its own and neither delivered nor reported), and a process that lost that claim takes the reply back as pending when the folder is gone, so the process the person works in delivers it even when the lease moves while the turn runs. The mod's `send_now` (poll and `state` events) is true only while a reply could go in now, so while this session's own Ask question holds it Send now is not offered and a pressed one reads "Queued", not "Sending … now". The line under the reply also reads "Queued" for a message sent without the wake mark (the stdio shim run inside Claude Code) while its session's connection polls. Tests: `packages/server/inbox-send-now.test.ts` (routes, guards, the poll, `send_now_failed`, agent-link's steer and order), `apps/hook/hooks/mod/inbox.test.ts` 12 to 17 (the mod against a real Inbox: into the turn, the pointer turn, the turn ending before or during the append, the Ask question running or queued, oldest first), `apps/hook/hooks/mod/send-now-engine.test.ts` (the version gate), `apps/hook/tests/register.test.ts` (the engine harness: the row through `$.session.append`; an older engine never offers it), `apps/opencode-plugin/inbox-v2-wake.test.ts` (the steer confirmed by its delivery, and the fallback; a tool call longer than the old 60 s cap with no fallback; the event stream ending mid-run, then the row found in the context or the fallback once idle; the pointer for a whole-message copy), `apps/opencode-plugin/opencode-session-bridge.test.ts` (the Ask turn mark), `apps/pi-extension/inbox-send-now.test.ts` (the steer, delivered on `message_start`; no Send now during an Ask; `editorWithout`), `apps/hook/hooks/mod/inbox.test.ts` 16a (another plugin's turn: no Send now, a press refused, the reply once as a turn at idle), 16b (a claim it cannot use given back) and 16c (two processes: the lease moves while the claimant's turn runs, and the other process delivers once), `packages/inbox/deliveryLine.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/sendNow.test.tsx`, `tests/e2e/inbox-send-now.spec.ts` (compiled binary; light, dark and a phone).
- **A session that needs a restart after an update** (owner approval "restart-warning", 2026-10-19; design record not in this repo; `packages/server/inbox-restart.ts`). The Claude Code mod reports what it loaded on every bridge poll, additive: `plugin: { version, root, loaded_at }` (plugin.json's version read once from `${$.plugin.root}/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, the folder, and the module's load time; `readLoadedPlugin` in `register.ts`, `Host.loadedPlugin`), ALWAYS, with `version` or `root` null when it could not read them, so a poll without `plugin` only ever means a mod older than `INBOX_PLUGIN_REPORTS_LOADED_SINCE` (0.28.24, in core; this must merge before the version bump to it); `engine` (`$.session.version()`'s `base`, `Host.engineVersion`); and `session_folder` (`$.session.root()` read once when the link starts, so a later `/cd` does not move it: the folder `claude --resume` runs in; `project_path` is the session's project folder, `InboxLink.projectFolder()`: its start folder, see "A session's project" in `inbox-foundation.md`). Pi, Oh My Pi and OpenCode 2 send `plugin: { version, on_disk, host? }` (see the next item). The Inbox reads Claude Code's plugin registry (`<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/plugins/installed_plugins.json`, only `plannotator@plannotator`, the repo marketplace's name, so a fork is never judged against; at most every 2 s through the Inbox's agent environment) and judges each session against the record a restart would load (`chooseClaudePluginInstall`: the record whose `installPath` is the folder the session loaded, then a project- or local-scope record whose `projectPath` holds the session's folder, the deepest, then the user record). A session is outdated (`claudeSessionOutdated`) when what it loaded is not what a restart loads: the versions differ, its folder is not `installPath`, or it loaded before BOTH the record's `lastUpdated` and the install folder's last change (`pluginFolderChangedAt`: `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`'s mtime, else the folder's), an update that reused the folder and the version (`lastUpdated` alone also moves for a project-scope install of the same folder with no new code; whether Claude Code ever reinstalls into the same folder was not verified live). Every path compares by its real path (`realish`: the deepest existing part realpath'd), so a symlinked `~/.claude` or CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR neither hides nor invents a difference. A session that sends no `plugin` (a mod older than the field) is outdated with its version unknown once the installed plugin is at least `PLUGIN_REPORTS_LOADED_SINCE` (0.28.24). A plugin loaded from outside Claude Code's plugin folder (`claude --plugin-dir`, and a local or directory marketplace, which Claude Code loads in place) reloads that folder on restart and is never outdated. When the installed plugin is itself behind this Inbox's release, a restart alone does not help: `fix: "update_plugin"` with `update_command` (the install script's two commands, `--scope user`); otherwise `fix: "restart"`. Pi and OpenCode 2: see the next item. Where it shows: (1) `asker.connection` gains additive `outdated: { loaded, installed, fix, update_command? } | null`, `engine` and `restart_command` (`cd <folder> && claude --resume <session>`, the folder being `session_folder` (else `project_path`) with `~`, quoted when unusual; `claudeRestartCommand`); the line under a reply (`restartNote` in `deliveryLine.ts`, drawn by `RestartLine` in `components/RestartNote.tsx`, the approved "Steps") shows ONLY while that session works, has no Send now, and is outdated with a loaded version that is unknown or older than `INBOX_SEND_NOW_SINCE` (0.28.24, the first release with Send now: a session on that or newer that lacks Send now lacks it for another reason, its own Ask question or a refused row, so the line never blames the plugin), and never for an engine older than Send now's floor (`inboxEngineTooOldForSendNow`, a copy of `SESSION_APPEND_MIN_ENGINE` pinned by `send-now-engine.test.ts`; the owner chose to say nothing about the engine itself, 2026-10-19). Its shape (simplified 2026-10-21, owner: "simple buttons win"; `ReloadLead` and `Fold` in `components/RestartNote.tsx`, shared with Settings): one lead line, "Type /reload-plugins in that Claude Code to get Send now." with a Copy that copies `/reload-plugins` (the owner checked live that `/reload-plugins` loads the new plugin in place and keeps the session). With `update_plugin` the lead is two steps: the update command with Copy, then `/reload-plugins` with Copy. The manual path is folded behind "Still no Send now after reloading?" (`aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`): `/exit` in that Claude Code, then the resume command with Copy, the warning that `/exit` stops the running turn, and "Your reply waits here." `/restart` is never mentioned. Each Copy says so in a polite live region ("/reload-plugins copied.", "Restart command copied."). (2) The list model and the event stream's `status` carry additive `restart_claude_code` (live Claude Code sessions whose fix is `restart` or, since the owner asked to bring the update notice back on 2026-11-10, `update_plugin`: once the binary is current there is no Update button, so without it a plugin that never updated showed only in Settings → Agents; pushed when it changes, checked every 5 s): the sidebar's "Reload Claude Code N" (`data-restart-sidebar`, a button under the Update button / "What's new in X", above Settings; in the navigation sheet on a phone) opens Settings → Agents and goes away by itself once they reload or restart; not on a tailnet guest's page, whom Agents refuses. (3) `GET /api/inbox/agents` carries additive `sessions: { "claude-code"?, pi?, opencode? }` (`InboxHostSessions`: `running`, `outdated`, `installed`, `update_command`, `sessions: [{ session, project, folder, started_at, busy, plugin, outdated, restart_command }]`, newest first, live polling sessions only); Settings → Agents shows an "Open sessions" row per host ("3 running · 2 on plugin 0.28.21"). For Claude Code sessions that need it, a box under the row leads with the same `ReloadLead` ("Type /reload-plugins in each of these N Claude Code sessions." with Copy; with `update_plugin`, the update command first), then the sessions as a short list with no buttons (project, "since <time>", idle or working; two in one folder are told apart by start time), then "Still listed after reloading?" folded, which opens `/exit` and a Copy restart per session (named "Copy restart: the command that restarts the <project> session started <time>"). Pi and OpenCode: see the next item. The head reads "Connected · 2 need a reload", the Agents nav gets the warning dot, and Settings → Agents re-reads every 5 s while open. Only sessions that poll are seen (the Inbox tool on; one process of a two-process session). No keyboard shortcut. Tests: `packages/server/inbox-restart.test.ts` (every rule, the registry's shapes, the command, and over a real Inbox with a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`), `apps/hook/hooks/mod/inbox-loaded.test.ts` (the poll fields over the in-memory Host), `packages/inbox/deliveryLine.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/restartWarning.test.tsx` (the whole window over `tests/helpers/inbox-dom-server.ts` `restart`).
- **Pi, Oh My Pi and OpenCode 2 sessions that need a reload** (owner approval "reload-hosts", 2026-10-26; `packages/server/inbox-restart.ts` `piSessionOutdated` / `openCodeSessionOutdated`). The connections report, additive on every bridge poll, `plugin: { version, on_disk, host? }` (`InboxWakeTarget.plugin()` in `packages/shared/inbox/agent-link.ts`, `InboxLoadedPluginReport`): Pi and Oh My Pi send the version the extension loaded (`loadedPackageVersion`), the version of the package.json on disk now (`readOnDiskVersion`, cached 2 s; null when unreadable), and `host` (`pi` or `oh-my-pi`, so Oh My Pi now reports too); OpenCode 2 sends the version loaded and the package.json beside the loaded file (`openCodePluginReporter` in `apps/opencode-plugin/inbox.ts`; `on_disk: null` once the generation folder is deleted, as the install script does). OpenCode 1 sends nothing. Judgment: Pi/Oh My Pi with `on_disk` different from `version` is `fix: "reload"` (`reload_command` `/reload`, Oh My Pi `/restart`, `reload_where: "session"`); equal and behind this Inbox is `fix: "update_extension"` with `update_command` `pi update --extensions` (`pi install npm:@plannotator/pi-extension` when Pi's settings pin an exact version, `piExtensionPinned` / `createPiPinReader` reading `<agentDir>/settings.json`; Oh My Pi `omp plugin upgrade @plannotator/pi-extension`), then the reload; `on_disk: null` says nothing (a reload would unload it); a poll without `on_disk` keeps the old rule. OpenCode 2 with `on_disk` null or different is `fix: "reload"` with `opencode2 reload` (`reload_where: "shell"`: it reloads every location of the background server); equal and behind this Inbox is `fix: "update_extension"` (not counted as a reload) with `update_command` `opencode2 models && opencode2 plugin update <spec>`, one step since it installs the newer generation and reloads. The `models` command goes first (`OPENCODE2_OPEN_FOLDER_COMMAND`): it waits for the folder's plugins to activate without starting a model turn. In a folder the service has not opened yet, `plugin update` can fail ("Plugin is not configured"; with no target, "No plugin updates available"). `plugin list` did not wait reliably on OpenCode 2.1.4; the real-host reload proof covers `models` before the update. `<spec>` is Plannotator's entry in the OpenCode config exactly as written (`openCodePluginSpec` / `createOpenCodeSpecReader`: `OPENCODE_CONFIG` when set, then `opencode.json`, `opencode.jsonc` or `config.json` under `openCodeConfigDir`, cached 2 s; under OpenCode 2's `plugins`, a string or `{ package }`, or OpenCode 1's `plugin`, a string or a `[spec, options]` pair, which OpenCode 2 also reads and our installer writes): OpenCode refuses the bare name when the config says `@latest`. The spec goes into the copied command only when it is shell-safe (`openCodeSpecShellSafe`: after the `@`, only letters, digits, `_`, `.`, `-`; a range like `@>=0.28.0` would redirect). Otherwise, or with no spec found, the command has no target and `update_scope: "all"` (the window says it updates every OpenCode plugin). A pinned exact version (`openCodeSpecPinned`) makes `plugin update` a no-op, so it carries `pinned_spec` and `pin_to` (`@plannotator/opencode@latest`) and the window says to change the pin, then `opencode2 reload` (`plugin add` would append a duplicate entry); its one Copy sits right after `@plannotator/opencode@latest` and copies that. `fallback_command` `opencode2 service restart` (stops running turns). `opencode2 reload` carries one quiet line: "Cancels any permission prompt OpenCode is showing." `InboxSessionOutdated` carries `runtime`, `reload_command`, `reload_where`, `fallback_command`; `InboxOpenSession.runtime`. Where it shows: (1) under a reply, the same rule as Claude Code (`restartNote`: busy, no Send now, loaded before 0.28.24), so in practice it fires only for a session older than the reporting itself; `StuckReason` / `RestartLine` name the runtime's command with Copy (never `/reload-plugins`); Pi folds `/quit` and `pi --continue`, OpenCode shows one command with Copy (`openCodeCommand`, `OpenCodeWords`), its quiet line (`OpenCodeQuiet`) and, for a reload, folds `opencode2 service restart`; Oh My Pi folds nothing. (2) The list model and the `status` event carry additive `restart_agents: { total, runtimes }` (live sessions whose fix is `restart` or `reload`, per runtime, plus Claude Code's `update_plugin`, counted again in `update_first`, the row's `data-restart-update`, whose screen-reader description then says to update the plugin, then type /reload-plugins; `update_extension` is not counted; `restart_claude_code` stays for older windows); the sidebar row reads "Reload Claude Code", "Reload Pi", "Restart Oh My Pi", "Reload OpenCode", or "Reload agents" for more than one (`packages/inbox/restartAgents.ts`). (3) Settings → Agents' "Open sessions" for Pi and OpenCode leads with the same command per runtime and command (`HostReload` in `components/AgentsSettings.tsx`), the update first when the package on disk is behind, then the sessions (Pi sessions on a connection without the report keep the `pi update --extensions`, then `/reload` line beside them); OpenCode is one short line, "Run `opencode2 reload`." with Copy inline (or its update, or the pin), and its server restart is folded behind "Still listed after reloading?". (4) `tag_needs_update` names the command per runtime in plain text (`tagUpdateFix`; Claude Code's update is "Update Plannotator's plugin, then type /reload-plugins…", the long commands stay in Settings). (5) Inside Pi and Oh My Pi, the extension says once per new on-disk version (`ctx.ui.notify`), and the in-session refusal after an update (#2085, `packageUpdatedMessage`) names `/reload` or `/restart`. Auto-update was investigated and not built (Pi's `ctx.reload()` exists only in command contexts; an OpenCode 2 reload restarts every session's plugin in the daemon): see the PR. Tests: `packages/server/inbox-reload-hosts.test.ts` (the table, the pin, over a real Inbox), `apps/opencode-plugin/inbox-reload.test.ts` (the reporter, the deleted generation, cleanup stopping the polls), `packages/inbox/restartAgents.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/reloadHosts.test.tsx` (`tests/helpers/inbox-dom-server.ts` `reload-hosts`), the browser proof `tests/e2e/inbox-reload-hosts.spec.ts` (PNGs in `PLANNOTATOR_RELOAD_SHOTS`), and the real-host proofs, opt-in: `apps/opencode-plugin/inbox-reload-v2.test.ts` (`OPENCODE2_BIN`, `OPENCODE_PLUGIN_TARBALL`: the real `opencode2 service` under scratch XDG dirs, two repacked versions through a throwaway registry, `opencode2 plugin update` and the deleted generation then `opencode2 reload`) and `apps/pi-extension/inbox-reload.test.ts` (`PLANNOTATOR_RELOAD_PROOF=1`, `PI_EXTENSION_TARBALL`, and `PLANNOTATOR_OMP_BIN` for Oh My Pi: interactive Pi and OMP in a pseudo-terminal, `pi update --extensions` then `/reload`, `omp plugin upgrade` then `/restart`); both run an in-process Inbox at 0.28.99 with its release check stubbed, and `RELOAD_PROOF_SHOTS` adds screenshots (`tests/helpers/reload-proof-shots.ts`). Measured: the line changes at the session's next poll (about 12 to 17 s after the files change, the long poll's length), and goes 1 to 2 s after `/reload`, `opencode2 plugin update` or `opencode2 reload` (OpenCode 2: once the session runs again, since a reloaded plugin starts its wakes on the next turn, so an idle session reappears in Settings after its next event). A disposed OpenCode generation starts no wake (`watch` and `discover` return once its signal aborted). Oh My Pi's `/restart` starts a new session id: the new one reports at once, the old one's line stays until it stops counting as live (30 s).
- No tool answers, approves or sends for the person.
- **Re-ask** (approval "inbox-reask", 2026-10-20; `.product/approved/inbox-reask/`): the person asks the agent that asked for its questions still open in the thread again, together in one new message with fresh context (the open questions spread over several messages, or went stale).
  - Where: the last row of the thread's "N open questions" list, on its left (Skip… on its right) (see "Open questions in a thread"; owner, 2026-10-23: "maybe we should somehow combine re-ask with this… a button in popover that is like re-ask in new message"), **Re-ask in one new message** ("Re-ask in a new message" when it asks for one thing again, `reaskItemLabel`), a `menuitem` with the refresh icon (`data-reask`; arrows and Enter reach it; a 44 px row of the phone's sheet). There is no Re-ask button in the foot any more. It does what the button did (`sendReask` in `Thread.tsx`: the same targets, the picks-only reply, the ended-session route choice, whose Back puts focus on the list's button). Shown when one or more questions or action items wait (open, or a question picked and not sent) and at least one has no pick (`reaskOffered`, `packages/inbox/reask.ts`; owner, 2026-10-23: "re-ask should show for even 1 question back in a thread"). A session is left out while its note waits (no message of that session after its latest note) and right after it answered one (everything it still has open sits in its one message that answers the note, and the session has written nothing since; any later message of that session offers Re-ask again). Tooltip (also its `aria-describedby`): "Ask Claude Code to ask the 3 open questions again, together in one message with fresh context. Questions that no longer apply are closed; until then they stay open here." With one: "Ask Claude Code to ask the open question again, with fresh context. If it no longer applies, it is closed; until then it stays open here." No keyboard shortcut.
  - One note per session (owner's rule 3): `reaskTargets` groups the thread's live agent messages by session and posts `POST /api/inbox/messages/:id/reask` (`{ serverSession?, idempotency_key, to? }`, window guards; door 7.61) to each session's latest message with a question waiting, one key per target kept for a retry. A pick still being saved is awaited first. A session with picks not sent and nothing open gets no note: the same press sends it a plain reply carrying the picks as answers (`kind: 'picks'`, one reply per message, its own kept key), so a Re-ask never strands a pick. A session is hidden from Re-ask only while a note of its waits by rule 2a (its session has not written since, and something it lists is still open); a note that went to another session (the asker ended) stops hiding it once that session wrote, so whatever it neither replaced nor superseded stays re-askable.
  - The store (`InboxStore.sendReask`) writes one person message, `reply_to` that message, with `reask: [<message id>/<key>]` (the session's open questions in the thread, from every live message of that session) and a body from `composeInboxReaskBody` (`packages/core/inbox-questions.ts`): what to do ("Please ask your 3 open questions in this thread again, together in one new message…"), the list by id and prompt, the rule (still needed: ask again with today's context and a `Replaces: <id>` line in its block; no longer needed: its id in `supersedes`, one line why), `reply_to <note id>`. Picks the person made and has not sent go with it (owner's rule 2): marked sent with the note (`sent_reply_id`), printed under "Answers to your questions" with the reply's answer lines (`formatQuestionAnswerLines`), and not asked again. Refusals: `409 nothing_to_reask` (nothing of that session open), `422 validation_error` (a person's message, no key), `409 thread_resolved`, `409 session_not_live` for a `to` that is not live; a replayed key answers the note (a reply's key: `409 idempotency_key_reused`).
  - The note is the person's reply, never an Inbox note: it is delivered like one (the queued/sending line, Send now, "Delivered to"), and while every open question is in a note that still waits the row is in Sent (rule 2a above) (`reask_pending`; the Sent row reads "Re-ask queued for <agent>…", or "Asked <agent> to re-ask; waiting for its message." once delivered, `reaskRowLine`). The old questions stay open (answerable) until the agent replaces them; their cards' footers read "Asked again <time>" (`reaskedIn`).
  - The wake: the bridge command carries `reask: true`, and `inboxWakeText` takes `INBOX_REASK_INSTRUCTION` (connection contract, the mod's copy in step) in place of the reply's line ("it is their answer to you" is not true of a Re-ask). An older connection words it as a reply; the body still says what to do. A note that lists exactly one question or action item also carries `reask_one: true`, and the wake line (`inboxReaskInstruction(items, one)`) and the redirected line (`inboxReaskRedirectedLine`) are then singular ("for the open question in this thread to be asked again, in one new message"); an older connection ignores it and keeps the plural line, while the note's body (`composeInboxReaskBody`) is singular either way ("Please ask your open question in this thread again…").
  - In the thread (`components/ReaskNote.tsx`): "You asked Claude Code to ask the 3 open questions again, together in one message. They stay open here until it does." (plus "Your N picks went with it."), the body behind "What Claude Code was sent". Folded: "Asked to re-ask 3 open questions in one message".
  - Action items (owner, #2059): a session's open action items are asked again with its open questions (the button counts them, the note lists them as `(action item)` and asks for a `:::action` block with `Replaces:`, or `supersedes` / `done`); see "Action items an agent needs you to go do".
  - The session that asked has ended (one target, that session, another live): Re-ask opens the reply's route choice with its own words ("Who should ask again?", primary "Ask Claude Code to re-ask", Cancel); the note goes with `to`, its body says "the N open questions <agent> asked" (named from the host), and its wake carries `redirected_from` with `inboxReaskRedirectedLine` (the questions are another, ended session's; read the thread, then ask them again) in place of the reply's redirected line.
  - Search indexes only the person's own words: a note's body is the Inbox's instructions and the prompts it lists, so `inboxSearchReplyText` gives nothing for it. Several sessions: each note goes to its own session, an ended one's waits for the next agent.
  - Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/store-reask.test.ts`, the thread-state model's `reask` op (`store.test.ts`, property runs), `packages/server/inbox-reask.test.ts` (routes, guards, the wake, an MCP agent superseding, the door), the DOM test `packages/inbox/reask.test.tsx` (A asks 2, B asks 1 and B is picked: one press, A's note and B's plain reply), `tests/e2e/inbox-reask.spec.ts` (compiled binary; desktop light and dark, 1280, the ended choice, a phone in WebKit).
- **Paste to attach** (the owner, 2026-10-16: "When I'm writing a new message or even a reply, I need to be able to paste in images"). A paste in compose (anywhere in the dialog) or in the reply box (`.ib-rbox`'s `onPaste`) that carries files attaches them through compose's upload path, tiles and progress (`useComposeFiles().paste`, `packages/inbox/compose/ComposeFiles.tsx`; the rules in `packages/inbox/compose/paste.ts`). A clipboard image with a name nobody chose (`image.png`, `Pasted Graphic 2.png`, `blob`, none) is renamed `pasted-image-YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.<ext>` in local time (`pasted-file-…` for anything else), `-2`, `-3` when the name is taken in this message; a real name (a file copied in the file manager) is kept. Text is never taken from the browser, because dictation apps (Hex) write the clipboard, post a synthetic paste and restore the old clipboard about 500 ms later: everything reads `event.clipboardData` synchronously inside the event, never `navigator.clipboard`; a paste of text alone is left to the native path (no `preventDefault`; on Chromium 155-156 on macOS the plain-text insertion below inserts it); a paste of files beside text (an image copied from a page brings HTML, a file from Finder its name) attaches the files and still lets the browser insert the text (`clipboardHasText`); only files with no text is prevented. Text with an unnamed picture is text: a cell copied in Excel, Numbers or Pages carries a picture of itself, which Chromium hands over as `image.png`, so when the clipboard has non-empty `text/plain` only files with a real name attach (`isGenericPasteName`; a screenshot and Chrome's "Copy image" carry no plain text and attach, a Finder copy keeps its name). Each attach by paste is said politely ("Attached <name>"): the thread's live region for the reply box, the dialog's own (`[data-compose-paste-live]`) for compose, since the window's region sits outside the modal dialog. Local previews' object URLs are freed on remove, reset and unmount.
