On a phone only (`PHONE_QUERY`), an image copied from a page attaches although it carries text (`pastedImageOwnLink`): the plain text is one `http(s)://` address AND the `text/html` beside it holds an `<img>` whose `src` is that same address; the link is not inserted (the paste is prevented). A spreadsheet cell whose value is a URL (its text, an HTML table, a picture of the cell) stays text on a phone and a desktop, and a desktop's rule is unchanged.
  - **A long paste is a file** (approval `.product/approved/inbox-paste-attachment/`, option A). A text-only paste of 32 KB or more (UTF-8 bytes, `PASTE_TO_FILE_BYTES` / `isLongPaste` in `packages/inbox/compose/pastedText.ts`) into the reply box, New message or the full-screen composer is prevented and becomes a file `pasted-text-YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.txt` (`-2`, `-3` when taken) through the same upload path, so the agent gets `Attached: pasted-text-….txt`. A selection the paste replaced is deleted, as a paste would. The tile is the file tile's size with a pasted-text variant (`PastedTextTile`, `.ib-pt`, `data-pasted-text`): its first line the paste's first line with words (`pastedPreview`), its second "Pasted text · <size>"; for the first 10 s (`PASTED_UNDO_MS`, `data-pasted-fresh`) "Pasted text · Undo"; after that hover or keyboard focus swaps the line in place for "Put back in box" (one grid cell, no layout shift). On a phone (the `PHONE_QUERY` widths, or no hover) the line keeps the size and "Undo", then "Put back", sits beside the × with a 44 px target (`data-pasted-side`). Undo and Put back in box are one button whose words change at the 10 s swap, so keyboard focus on it stays; its name says which paste ("Undo paste of <name>", "Put back in box: <name>"), so two tiles never give two identical buttons. Undo and Put back are one action (`useComposeFiles().putBack`): the exact text goes back where it was pasted (line ends as the box holds them: `\r\n` and `\r` become `\n`, `boxText`, while the file keeps the pasted bytes), the words around it untouched, the caret after it, the tile and its upload gone; the place follows later edits (`mapAnchor`, a prefix/suffix diff; words deleted over it put it where they began). The platform's undo (a `beforeinput` of `historyUndo`, read on a document capture listener, never a key binding) also puts it back, but only as the very next edit to that field; any other edit stands it down and undo is the browser's again. Chromium sends no `historyUndo` when the field's own undo stack is empty (a paste into a box nothing was typed in), so ⌘Z then does nothing; the tile's Undo is the way back. A paste into a read-only box is left alone. A paste of a named file beside 32 KB of text attaches the file and makes the text a tile too. Where the dialog takes no files (the tour), 32 KB pasted into the thread-name chip goes to the message as words, as before; nothing is uploaded. Drafts keep long pastes (PR 2 of the approval): the reply draft's `files` (`InboxReplyDraft.files`, each the upload with `pasted: { at, lead?, preview }`) and a New message draft's `files[].pasted`, saved with the words (`useComposeFiles().pastedDraft(words)` places each in the words being saved; a tile that finishes uploading or is removed saves the reply draft too, `pastedSignature`), compared with the words as one (`replyContentKey`, `base_files` beside `base_body`). After a reload, in another window (`event: drafts`, a box with no edits) or in a reopened New message draft the tile comes back past its Undo (`restorePasted`, `fromUpload`), and Put back reads the paste's words from its upload (`GET /api/inbox/uploads/:id/text`, forwarded to the linked machine for another machine's thread) and puts them back at the saved place, following edits since; a read that fails says so on the tile and keeps it attached. A closed box's draft of a paste alone goes with the foot's Send (its uploads, and `clear_draft`). Another window's save, Send or Discard never drops a paste still uploading here (in no draft yet: it stays, its place carried into the new words, and is saved once uploaded). A save refused because one paste's upload is gone (`upload_not_found`, `files[i]`) is saved again without that one, the others kept. A Send that carried some of a draft's pastes (a stale Send beside another window's paste) leaves no draft naming them: the server drops its uploads from every reply draft (`dropSentUploadsFromReplyDrafts`, a new `updated_at`, a draft left empty deleted) and tells those threads' windows (`event: drafts`). A window that has not heard yet and still names a sent paste in `base_files` is not told apart by it (the compare skips a `base_files` id that is no longer an upload): its save without that paste lands as its own, no conflict line. The restart guard does not ask for a long paste the server's draft holds (`replyDraft.draft.files`), and asks for any other: still uploading, waiting for its save, or refused. A draft for another machine's thread is still not saved. Each is said politely ("Attached <name>", "The pasted text is back in your reply."). Tests: `packages/inbox/compose/pastedText.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/pasteText.test.tsx` (caret and the words around, the 10 s swap and focus across it, the buttons' names, CRLF, a named file beside long text, undo standing down after typing, Send carrying the bytes, New message, the chip, a refusal that is not "too long" and the restart guard asking until a save lands), the tour's chip case in `packages/inbox/tour.test.tsx`, the full-screen card's case in `packages/inbox/fullScreenComposer.test.tsx`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/pasteDraft.test.tsx` (a reload, another window, a closed box's Send, a reopened New message draft, a paste still uploading when another window saves, one paste gone and the restart guard, a Put back whose read fails), the server's `packages/server/inbox-reply-drafts.test.ts` ("reply drafts keep long pastes": the place, the text route and only pasted text, the compare over files, refusals, a stale Send of a paste alone, a stale Send beside another window's paste, a Send carrying some of a draft's pastes dropping them from every draft, the sweep) and the forward in `inbox-machines.test.ts`, and `tests/e2e/inbox-paste-text.spec.ts` (the compiled binary: a real ⌘Z / Ctrl+Z, CRLF and focus at the swap, hover with no layout shift, the agent reading the bytes, a tile-only New message, a phone at 390 and 320 px, a reload bringing the draft's paste back as the tile with Put back reading the upload, and a long draft edited and its page closed within the 500 ms pause: the edit is on the server, for words with `base_body` over 64 KiB (and back in the box after reopening) and for words alone over it; each test has its own thread, since a saved draft reopens the box and hides the footer's Reply).
  - A reply draft too long to keep (over `INBOX_DRAFT_BODY_MAX`, 1,000,000 characters, `@plannotator/core/inbox-types`) is no longer refused in silence: it is not sent, the draft line says "Not saved: a draft holds at most 1,000,000 characters." (`DraftHead`'s `[data-draft-too-long]`, `useReplyDraft().tooLong`), and the restart guard counts it as unsaved. Shortened, it saves again.
- **The reply box** (approval `.product/approved/inbox-reply-box/`, T6, 2026-11-07; `Thread.tsx`, `components/ReplyRow.tsx`, `components/ReplySummary.tsx`, `sendFailure.ts`). One card with the words first. A chevron at its top right (`[data-reply-close]`, "Close the reply box (your draft is kept)") closes it and keeps the draft, as Esc does. It replaced Cancel. There is no header line. The closed foot is today's bar, byte for byte: the open-questions button keeps `footFit`'s words and nothing reorders its buttons (the bottom-bar question is still the owner's; `replyFootPhone.test.tsx` fails on a style that draws a count of its own or moves them).
  - The row inside the box (`[data-reply-row]`) never wraps, and its controls never move. On a computer it holds Send (`[data-reply-send]`), the paperclip, Skills (`[data-reply-skills]`: inserts `/` and opens the skill list) and the mic at the left, then one middle cell (`.ib-rmid`) for what goes with the reply and who gets it (the recipient pill), then the bin at the far right (`[data-reply-discard]`, "Discard draft"; after a two-window conflict it is "Discard my words", which does what the conflict line's "Discard mine" does and never deletes the other window's draft; an empty box with nothing saved only closes, with no "Draft discarded."). While the live-session choice is open under the box, the box shows neither Send nor the bin: the choice stands in for them, as it did for Send's row. Skills inserts no `/` when no skill can be picked here, and does not focus the field (a phone's keyboard would come up for nothing): "No skills are installed" shows for about 2 s in the row's middle cell (`[data-reply-hint]`; over the chips on a computer, after the paperclip and Skills on a phone), and the live region says "No skills are installed to pick from." On a phone (the owner: "maybe we don't need delete icon. I like attachment on left"; since approval `.product/approved/inbox-phone-composer-picker/`, 2026-11-09, see "The phone composer" in `inbox-compose.md`) the row holds the paperclip, Skills and (with several agents) who gets the reply at the left and the mic and Send at the right edge. Each is 36 px and round with a 44 px hit area, and Send is a round arrow whose name is still "Send". A phone has no bin: Discard is on the Draft line above the box. The summary line sits on its own line over the row (`[data-reply-line]`). With the keyboard pinned on iOS the row is sticky at the foot's bottom, so Send stays above the keyboard. The chevron sits clear of a classic scrollbar on the scroll region (`--ib-rsb`).
  - D1: the words scroll inside the box (`[data-reply-scroll]`). The box stops at about half the window (`max(150px, 52vh - 120px)` on a computer; on a phone 40% of what the keyboard leaves, `--ib-vvh`, or of the window with no keyboard), so Send is always in view.
  - D2: there is no cap on files. From `REPLY_FILES_SUMMARY_FROM` (7) the row says "N files" (`[data-reply-files-count]`), which scrolls the box to its tiles.
  - D3: a failed Send says it in one plain line (`sendFailure`; a full disk, an upload's `upload_no_space` or ENOSPC in the words, says "the disk is full"), with the Inbox's own words behind a native Details (`[data-error-details]`). A short refusal written for people (4xx, no path, no errno) stays as it is. A Send that may have gone keeps its own line ("The reply may not have gone…").
  - D4: on a computer the middle cell shows the chips when they fit and the one summary line (`ReplySummary`) when they don't. `ReplyRow` measures before paint, again on a change of the cell's width, the chips' words, the text size or the fonts, but never while the person is in the cell (focus on a chip or the summary line, or a sheet or the comments list open from one): a Skip them in the line's sheet keeps the sheet and focus, the last comment removed from the chip's list sends focus to the field, and an open list is never measured as chips. The fit is tried on the next render after they leave (focus leaving the cell brings one).
  - D5: while a take runs, the voice status covers the middle cell (the chips and the pill on a computer; the paperclip, Skills and the agent control on a phone), and those step aside (`visibility: hidden`). The mic and Send stay where they are. A voice failure note glides in and out above the words (`VoiceNote`'s `glide`). Reduced motion turns the glide off.
  - Tests: `packages/inbox/replyFootPhone.test.tsx` (DOM: the row order on a phone and a computer, the summary line, D3, the bin after a conflict, the summary's sheet on a narrow computer pane, the closed foot's style), `packages/inbox/sendFailure.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/inbox-reply-foot-phone.spec.ts` (the compiled binary: one row at 390 and 320, 44 px targets, the cap with Send in view, the row still while recording, chips or the summary line).
- **Attach in a reply: paperclip and drop** (approval "inbox-reply-attach", 2026-10-29; supersedes "no paperclip, drop zone or shortcut in the reply box on a desktop"). The reply box's row holds a paperclip (beside Send on a computer, at the left edge on a phone; see "The reply box"). It is `AttachFilesButton` (`packages/inbox/compose/ComposeFiles.tsx`, New message's foot uses it too): a label named "Attach files" with a `Tip` over a hidden `<input type="file" multiple data-reply-attach>`, any file type; a desktop's file keeps its own name (`useComposeFiles().add`), a phone's goes through `attach` (see "Attach on a phone"). Files dragged over the box get a dashed outline and "Drop to attach" (`.ib-rbox[data-drop-over]`, `[data-box-drop]`); files dragged anywhere else over the thread (`section.ib-pane`) get the whole-pane target "Drop to attach to your reply" (`[data-pane-drop]`), and a drop there opens the reply box with the files when it is closed (its draft's words come back as Reply brings them). Both use `useFileDrop` (ComposeFiles.tsx, New message's dialog too): a drag with no `Files` type (text, a link, a Board card) is left alone, and so is a drag that started on the page itself (an image in a message: a document `dragstart` / `dragend` pair marks it, since some browsers give it a `Files` type); a drag an inner target already took (`defaultPrevented`: the box inside the pane) is left to it, so only the innermost target shows. The pane's line names the upload limit from `INBOX_UPLOAD_MAX_BYTES` (`@plannotator/core/inbox-types`, re-exported by shared's `uploads.ts`). No pane target where no reply can be written (archived, Artifacts, the replaced-questions guard, an in-thread New message). The files are the same tiles, upload path (`POST /api/inbox/uploads`) and Send (`uploads` on the reply) as a paste. No shortcut; Escape is unchanged. Tests: `packages/inbox/pasteFiles.test.tsx` (the paperclip and a drop on the box add tiles, a pane drop opens a closed box with the file, a text drag is not taken, Send carries the uploads), `tests/e2e/inbox-paste.spec.ts` (the compiled binary, real drag events), `tests/e2e/inbox-mobile-fixes.spec.ts` (the phone's paperclip leading the row).
- **Attach on a phone.** iOS cannot paste a picture into a plain `<textarea>`: WebKit's `canPerformAction:paste:` (`WKContentViewInteraction.mm`) offers Paste in a plain-text field only when the pasteboard holds text, a URL or WebKit's custom data, so an image-only clipboard (a photo copied in Photos, a screenshot) gets no Paste item and no `paste` event ever reaches the page. On a phone (`PHONE_QUERY`, `usePhone`) the reply box's paperclip (the row's first item: `.ib-rclip`, 44 px) takes `accept="image/*,*/*"`, and so does compose's paperclip. A chosen file goes through `useComposeFiles().attach` (`chosenFiles` / `chosenFileNames` in `paste.ts`): the paste's names (the library's `image.jpg` becomes `pasted-image-<stamp>.jpg`), tiles, upload path and announcement. Desktop compose's paperclip keeps the file's own name. A phone cannot tell a photo from the library from a file in Files called `image.png` (both arrive as a plain `File`), so such a file is renamed too. Proofs: `packages/inbox/pasteFiles.test.tsx` (DOM) and `tests/e2e/inbox-mobile-fixes.spec.ts` (an iPhone 15 in WebKit and a Pixel 7 in Chromium).
  - The reply box's files are tiles inside the box, under the words in its scroll region (`[data-reply-files]`, `.ib-rfiles`; each at least 200 px wide in a row, the whole box when that is narrower: they fit a 320 px phone, and a tile's × takes a 44 px hit area on a phone). They are part of the reply draft (`replyDraft.ts`; the owner, 2026-11-02: "ideally it just syncs"): every finished upload in the box (paperclip, drop, paste, a long paste with its place) is saved beside the words (`useComposeFiles().draftFiles`), so closing the box, Escape, another thread, a reload, a second window and the phone keep them, and the box takes them back as tiles (`restoreDraft`). A tile's × removes it from the draft; Discard drops the files with the words. A file still uploading when the box closes or the thread changes (or the pane unmounts) is not stopped: it joins that thread's draft once in (`useComposeFiles().detach`, `landInReplyDraft`; the restart guard asks meanwhile). Two windows changing the files never refuse each other: a save does not send the box's list but its file CHANGES since the box last held the draft's files (`files_add`, `files_remove`, and long pastes' places, `files_place`; `take` and `held` in `replyDraft.ts`), and the Inbox applies them to the draft's files as they are then (`applyReplyDraftFileOps`, `@plannotator/core/inbox-reply-draft`). So another window's file stays, one another window removed stays out, and only different words refuse a save. Changes of a save that did not land (refused, unanswered, or skipped behind a conflict) are never dropped: they go with the next save of the box (`handOn`; with none queued they wait in `carried`, module-level, and the restart guard asks while a file added waits), and a save refused for words this window had already moved past is not sent again behind a newer save (the newer one carries its changes). After every save of this window lands, the box takes the draft's files with its changes still on their way (`onFiles`), so the box's tiles are the saved draft's files; reopening the box (Edit draft, the thread again) shows the draft's files with this window's changes not landed yet (`withMyChanges`). An added file whose upload is gone (a phone's Send carried it, the sweep took it) is left out by the Inbox and the save lands: the box drops its tile, no retry (a long paste leaving the box this way is said in the thread's live region). A save that changes the words moves another window's long paste it does not place with them (`mapAnchor`, in `@plannotator/core/inbox-reply-draft`, clamped to the words), so Put back puts it where that window pasted it. A save refused because only the other window changed the words (this box still holds the words it last knew) is no conflict when three things hold: the box took the newer words or is not showing that thread (`onWords` answers `took`: open on the same thread, its live words unchanged; or `hidden`: closed, or another thread in the pane, so nothing of the person's is overwritten and Edit draft shows the newer words with the file; `differs` is a conflict), every newer save of the box (queued or waiting for the pause) carries the old words too (each is given the newer words, its long pastes' places moved to them by `rewordReplyDraftFileOps`, so the Inbox never refuses a place past their end), and no Send is claiming the thread. Then its file changes are saved with the newer words. Otherwise (a key typed meanwhile, a Send pressed) the conflict shows, with the box's newest words, and the Send clears nothing it did not carry. A save with no answer counts as landed only when the read-back shows its words and its file changes (`holds`); otherwise its changes are sent again, and the Inbox skips an add of a file removed since the version that save names (`replyDraftRemovals` in the store), so sending a change again never brings back a file another window took out, and an attach is never counted as saved before the Inbox holds it. A Send's `claim`, and the stale page's `settle`, save file changes still waiting first, so the draft the Send clears holds none of them. A tile that finishes uploading after the box's last save stays when a save's answer comes (`heldOf`), and its own save adds it. Discard drops a file still uploading too: in the box, or landing from an earlier Cancel or thread switch (a per-thread Discard count `landInReplyDraft` checks). Only different words bring "Keep mine / Discard mine" (Keep mine saves these words with this window's file changes over the newer draft, its other files staying; Discard mine drops this window's words and file changes and takes the other window's words and files). A box that opens on a conflict's words shows the tiles too: the draft's files with this window's changes waiting on the choice (Edit draft, or the same pane given this thread again), so Keep mine keeps them; a tile removed or attached meanwhile counts as such once the conflict clears. A thread switch in the window remounts the pane, which then no longer knows a file whose save waits: its tile is left out, Keep mine still keeps the file, and the tile comes back once that save lands. Another machine's thread keeps no draft, so its files still live with the box only (Cancel and another thread stop them). Storage is behind a seam (`ReplyDraftStore`, `DraftFileUploader` in `@plannotator/core/inbox-reply-draft`; this Inbox's HTTP adapters in `replyDraftHttp.ts`), so a host with private storage until Send plugs in its own. Send waits while one uploads ("Wait for the files to finish uploading."). The ids ride the reply that carries the words (the last target, with the words and annotations), as `uploads`. A Send clears only the files it carried (`useComposeFiles().forget`): one pasted while it was in flight keeps the box open for the next Send. A retry after a failed Send whose words, files or annotations changed takes a new idempotency key for the reply carrying them (`sendContents` in `Thread.tsx`), so a Send that went through but answered an error is never replayed in place of the changed one. The ended-asker note counts only finished uploads, as Send does.
  - Server: the reply route takes `uploads: [upl_id]` with compose's rules (`inboxUploadAttachments`: each copied, read-only, to `uploads/<att_id>/<name>`, the pending record removed once the reply is written; `404 upload_not_found` for an id that is gone, `422` for a list that is not one; copied files are removed again when the store refuses the reply, an archived thread for example). A replayed idempotency key answers the reply it wrote before anything is copied (`InboxStore.replyByKey`). The reply carries them as `attachments`, so agents get them as compose's files: `Attached: <name> (<path>)` lines under the body on the wake (`inboxBodyForAgent`; a reply with no words is the lines alone), attachment records in `read_thread`. A Send of files alone (no words, no answers) is a reply, read as a words-only one: nothing answered, the still-open line; its folded line names the files (`foldSnippet`). The window shows them on the sent reply in the same read-only tiles and viewers as compose's files. The phone's door forwards `uploads` unchanged (contract 7.57). Tests: `packages/server/inbox-reply-uploads.test.ts`, the door case in `inbox-devices.test.ts`, `packages/inbox/paste.test.ts`, the DOM test `packages/inbox/pasteFiles.test.tsx` (a text-only paste never prevented), and `tests/e2e/inbox-paste.spec.ts` on the compiled binary.

**Debug log** (the owner, 2026-11-01: "an optional thing or like a debug mode that anybody can run"). When something stalls, it says which step hung and whether the server got the request. `PLANNOTATOR_INBOX_DEBUG=1`, or the config-only `inboxDebug: true`, read at start. Off, the default, nothing is written, no answer is marked and the window's route answers 404. No Settings row, nothing on screen.
- **Turning it on.** Agents start the Inbox on demand, so one is usually running already, and the variable does nothing for a running Inbox (`plannotator inbox` says so on stderr). Run `plannotator inbox stop`, then `PLANNOTATOR_INBOX_DEBUG=1 plannotator inbox` from that shell before an agent starts one without the variable. Or set `inboxDebug: true` in `config.json` and press Restart in the window, which also holds across later starts.
- **The file:** `<data dir>/inbox/debug.log`, JSON lines, owner-readable, 5 MB, then rotated once to `debug.log.1`, so at most two files. Lines are kept in memory and appended in order, asynchronously, every 250 ms (sooner past 64 KB), so no request waits on the disk; the Inbox's stop and its exit write everything not yet on disk, synchronously and in order (a write in flight again at its own offset, so no line twice), and past 4 MB not yet on disk (a disk that stopped answering) new lines are dropped. A request's start reaches the disk within about 250 ms; one whose handler blocks the loop keeps its start in memory until the exit, so only a force-killed blocked Inbox loses it. A window's batch stays in one file across a rotation. Tests read the file after `flushInboxDebugLogs()`.
- **What may enter it** (`packages/shared/inbox/debug-log.ts`, private to the Inbox: not a `@plannotator/core` export, which would need a core release): the owner may paste this file publicly, so every value is a number, a word from a fixed list, an id in the exact shape the Inbox makes (`msg_` and the rest with a ULID), or a window id made of digits. Any other path segment becomes `*`, and a query never enters. The server rebuilds each window record field by field and drops a value that fails its check, and it takes `X-Plannotator-Debug-Id` only from the owner. What a shape cannot rule out: a crafted 26-character string in the ULID alphabet passes as an id, though the Inbox makes none from a person's words. A new route's words show as `*` until they join `ROUTE_WORDS`.
- **Server lines** (`side: "server"`, `packages/server/inbox-debug-log.ts`): each `/api/inbox/` request writes `req_start` before any work, then `req_end` (`status`, `ms`) or `req_error`, with `method`, `route` and `via` (`local`, `tailnet`). `req_end` is when the handler returned, not when the bytes reached the phone, and for the event stream that is when the stream opened. Bun does not expose the HTTP version. A request from anyone but the owner gets a server id (`s12`). Device door requests (a paired phone, a linked machine) reach the Inbox through its local `fetch`, so they log as `via: "local"` under the inner route, with a server id: they do not join any window's lines.
- **Window lines** (`side: "window"`, `packages/inbox/debugLog.ts`): an Inbox with debug on marks its answers to the owner (`X-Plannotator-Inbox-Debug`), and the window then wraps `fetch`. `req_start`, `req_head` (the answer began), `req_end` (the whole answer is in), `req_abort`, `req_fail`, each with `page` and `seq`; `send_press` and `send_clear` (from the reply's `sending`, the one call in `Thread.tsx`); `page_load` with `protocol`, the page's HTTP version (a reload the server answered 304 has none on its navigation entry, so a `protocol` line follows with the first same-origin answer that came over the network); `page_show`, `page_hide` with `open` (requests still out), `visibility`, `online`, `offline`. Crash breadcrumbs: `mem` every 30 s (`DEBUG_MEMORY_MS`) with `heap` and `heap_limit` (whole MB, Chrome's `performance.memory`; absent elsewhere), `imgs` and `nodes` (the page's `<img>` and element counts); `slow_ui` carries the same four. While it records, the page keeps an alive mark in the tab's session storage (`plannotator-inbox-debug-alive`: its page id, the thread, file and view the address has open, its last `heap` and `imgs`), refreshed on each hash change and each `mem`, and removed at `pagehide` or when debug goes off. A page that dies without `pagehide` (a renderer crash, "Aw, Snap") leaves it, so the next page in that tab (its Reload) logs `renderer_gone` after its `page_load`: `last_page`, `ms` since the mark was last refreshed, `thread`, `file`, `view`, `heap`, `imgs`, and `discarded` (Chrome's `document.wasDiscarded`: a tab the browser discarded to save memory, not a crash). Chrome's Duplicate Tab copies session storage, so a duplicate of a recording tab logs one false `renderer_gone`. Voice dictation (`voiceSession.ts` through `VoiceDeps.note`, `voiceRecorder.ts`): `voice_press` with `input` (`press`, `release`, `lost`, `toggle`) and the `phase` and `mode` it met, so a tap that did nothing shows, and for a pointer's press and release `dx`, `dy`, its offset in px from the button's centre (none on `lost`: a pointercancel can carry 0,0 or a stale point); `voice_miss` (`voiceMiss.ts`, listened for only while the mic is opening or listening and the debug log is on; a log turned on mid-take is heard from the next take), a pointerdown outside the mic's hit box (its rect, grown by the phone's `::after`) but within 24 px of it, with `px` (the distance), `edge` (`above`, `below`, `left`, `right`), the `phase` and `mode` (`hold` tells a second finger during a hold from a stop tap), the button's `w` and `h`, and `other`, true when the tap landed on a neighbouring control (a button, link, input, textarea or `[role=button]`, such as Send or Save draft), at most one per 300 ms; `voice_phase` on each change of `phase`, `mode`, `prompting`, `slow` or `trouble`; `voice_open` for each step of opening the microphone (`step`: `context`, `stream`, `worklet`, `ready`) with `ok`, `ms` since the press, `audio` (the AudioContext's state) and, when it failed, the error's name. Fixed words, timings and error names only: never audio, the words or a status line. Only `fetch` is wrapped. Uploads (`XMLHttpRequest`, `api.ts`), `sendBeacon` (a closing window's `/window` report) and the event stream (`EventSource`) have no window lines, only server lines once they arrive. The wrapper must not change a request: the caller gets the same response or error, from one call in the same tick, and a failure of the recording falls back to the plain `fetch`.
- **Performance lines** (`packages/server/inbox-perf.ts`, `packages/inbox/perfMarks.ts`): with the log on, the server writes `store_load` (`ms`, `threads`, `messages`) once at start, `slow_req` (`route`, `ms`, `phases`) for an `/api/inbox/` request of 200 ms or more (not the routes that wait or stream by design: the `bridge/poll` long-poll, `events`, `m/` forwards, `uploads`), and `loop_stall` (`ms`) when its event loop runs 100 ms or more late, with what held it since the watch's last tick: `in` and `in_ms`, the named synchronous section that ran longest (a `perfPhase` such as `rows`, or a `syncSection`: `taggable`, `timer:delivery`, `timer:auto-send`, `timer:restart`, `timer:upload-sweep`, `timer:data-check`, `image-capture:gaps`, `image-capture:write`, `tailscale:<command>` for a `tailscale` call that waits), `route`, the request it ran for (null outside one), `handler_ms` and `handler_route`, the longest synchronous part of a request handler (up to its first await), and, when no section ran, `last`, the one entered last. The window writes `slow_ui` (`what`, `ms`, `n`) for an interaction of 100 ms or more: `what` is one of the `pn:` marks below.
- **Timings, with the log on only.** Off, the server does not wrap its handler (no header, `perfPhase` is one number compare, no loop timer) and every window entry point returns after one boolean check (no clock read, no frame callback, no measure, no observer); `inbox-perf.test.ts` and `perfMarks.test.ts` guard both sides. On, every `/api/inbox/` answer, over loopback and the tailnet, carries `Server-Timing` (`app` plus the phases run inside `perfPhase`: `rows`, `thread`, `disk`, `json`), shown in DevTools → Network → Timing; and the window leaves one `performance.measure` per key interaction (DevTools → Performance → Timings): `pn:first-list`, `pn:thread-open` (detail `messages`), `pn:record-refresh`, `pn:theme` and `pn:theme-settled`, `pn:expand-all`, and `pn:keystroke` (only at 50 ms or more), each to the next frame drawn; entries are cleared as they are made. A hidden window times nothing, an interaction the window was hidden at any moment since its start is dropped, a burst (events, keys) waits on one frame timed from its first, another thread's open replaces one whose read never came back, a second read of the thread being opened keeps its start, and a read that lands after the person left that thread ends it. When the window hears debug go off (`whenDebugLogStops` in `debugLog.ts`), the overlay, both observers and the visibility listener go, and nothing in flight is measured. The window learns the switch from the answers' debug mark, as its recorder does (`debugLogOn`). The overlay needs both the log on and its own opt-in, `localStorage['plannotator-inbox-perf'] = '1'`, or an exact `perf=1` token in the address's hash (`#perf=1`; `perf=0` turns it off). Only that token: agents choose parts of the hash (a linked page's path, a question key), so a substring such as `linked=perf-report.html` switches nothing. it shows the last `pn:` timings and long tasks, which exist only with the log on. Budgets: `PLANNOTATOR_PERF_BUDGET=1 bun test packages/shared/inbox/store-perf-budget.test.ts` checks that store work grows no faster than the data.
- **Buffer first.** Every record goes to a local buffer (localStorage, 2,000 records, oldest dropped with a `buffer_dropped` line, shared by the origin's tabs), and is sent to `POST /api/inbox/debug/log` every 5 s while no request is out, on coming back, and right after a refresh. The timed send waits while a request is out, because it could take one of the last connections or keep a dying one alive and hide the stall. So during a stall the file can lag the window until the refresh. That is how the old page's requests reach the file after a stall. A window's `t` is its own clock; `at` is when the server took the record.
- **Reading it.** The window's request id is on both sides (`X-Plannotator-Debug-Id`). For a stall, find the last `send_press` with no `send_clear`, then its open requests:
  - a window `req_start` with no line of that `id` from the server: it never arrived;
  - a server `req_end` with no window `req_end`: it landed and the answer was lost;
  - a server `req_start` with no `req_end`: the server is still working;
  - no `req_start` for `/reply` after the press: Send never got that far, and the open request is what it waited on. With no open request, suspect a hung upload: Send waits on those too, and they have no window lines.
  - a refresh itself ends what is open: WebKit fails the request a few ms before `page_hide`, so a `req_fail`, then `send_clear`, then `page_hide` with `open: 0`, all within milliseconds, is the refresh, not the network. The `ms` on that `req_fail` is how long the request had hung.
  - `jq -c 'select(.ev == "send_press" or .ev == "send_clear" or (.route // "" | endswith("/reply")))' debug.log`
- **A mic that did nothing.** `jq -c 'select(.ev | startswith("voice_"))' debug.log`. A `voice_press` with `input: "press"` and `phase: "opening"` or `"sending"` is a tap the take was too busy for. A `voice_open` that stops at `context` or `stream` (no `worklet` line, and no worklet GET on the server) is a microphone that never opened; its `ms` is how long the press waited. A `stream` or `worklet` line with `error: "AbortError"` is a mic that answered after the take had ended (Cancel, or the 10 s give-up): its tracks were stopped then. A tap with no `voice_press` at all never reached the session: it missed the button, or the mic was dimmed (a dimmed mic has no press handler). A `voice_miss` with `mode: "tap"` and `other: false` is a stop tap that landed just off a live mic; set its `px` and `edge` against the hits' `dx`, `dy` to see how close real taps come.
- Tests: `packages/shared/inbox/debug-log.test.ts` (what may enter), `packages/inbox/voiceDebugLog.test.ts` (DOM: voice lines only while debug is on, with no words; a press's offset on its line), `packages/inbox/voiceMiss.test.ts` (DOM: near misses only while live and on, with mode and `other`; far taps and hits not; the hit box with `::after`; the 300 ms gate; no listener or layout read while off; the press offset), `packages/server/inbox-debug-log.test.ts` (off, on, the fields, a start with no end, the caps, no words, an allow-listed guest), `packages/inbox/debugLog.test.ts` (the buffer, the flush after a refresh, the wrapper changing nothing), `apps/hook/server/inbox-lifecycle-cli.test.ts` (the note for a running Inbox), `tests/e2e/inbox-debug-log.spec.ts` in WebKit and Chromium (a healthy Send; a stalled page's records in the file after a reload, through a relay that goes silent).

**The comment card and visible drafts** (approval `.product/approved/inbox-comment-box/approval.md`, round 3, 2026-11-07; `@plannotator/ui` opt-ins turned on in `packages/inbox/App.tsx`: `configurePlannotatorUI({ commentCard: { pane, foot }, visibleCommentDrafts: true })`, styles in `packages/ui/comment-card.css`, imported by `apps/inbox/index.css`). Every text comment box in the window (a message, a Markdown or code file, an HTML page, a diagram part) is one small opaque card by the selected words (`CommentPopover`'s card, `.pn-cbox`, 352 px): the quoted words, the field, then one row with the tools at the left, the bin, and a solid "Comment" at the right end, in that order in the markup too, so Tab follows the screen. No header line, no blurred page behind, no fill that changes on hover. The chevron at its top right closes it and keeps the words; the expand button grows it in place (Escape shrinks it first); it never becomes a dialog. It sits under the words, else above them; on a phone, or with no room, it docks at the view's foot (`pane` / `foot` selectors) and scrolls the words above it. Closed with words in it, the words stay highlighted pale (`mark.comment-draft`) and a "Draft" badge (`CommentDraftBadges`, `[data-comment-draft-badge]`) floats just under their end, inside the column, with a thin ring in the page's colour; no line moves and the document gains no text (the word is CSS). The badge or a click on the words reopens the card with the words, and Escape from there puts focus back on the badge. Selecting other words while a card holds words keeps it as a draft too; picking a draft's words again by hand opens its card, never a second badge. Drafts come back wherever their document shows again: another thread and back, a file closed, opened or stepped past (`utils/commentDraftShelf`), with every draft of that document, not only the last. Drafts live in the window, as the box's words always did; a reload drops them. Message highlights ignore a draft's marks (`Thread.tsx` `openHighlight`). The quoted words are the card's handle (the owner, 2026-11-09: "I can't drag the popover like I can in Plannotator"): dragged by them, the card stays where it was put, whole inside the window (`useDraggable`'s `keepInside`), with its words, until other words are picked or it is opened to full; a phone's docked card has no handle. Its `/` and `$` skill list hangs off the card, not off the field (the field scrolls and clipped the whole list: "the skills don't work"), on the side away from the words; it lists skills only, never the reply box's commands (`/side`). Tests: `packages/ui/components/CommentPopover.card.test.tsx`, `packages/ui/components/Viewer.commentDrafts.test.tsx`, `packages/ui/components/diagram/DiagramComposer.card.test.tsx`.

**Full screen's composer** (approval "inbox-fullscreen-composer", 2A, round 4, 2026-10-29; record not in this repo; `packages/inbox/components/FullScreenComposer.tsx`, `packages/inbox/fullScreenComposer.ts`). The person can comment on a file or answer the agent without leaving full screen.
- Where: one card floats at the bottom centre of an image, a film or an HTML page in full screen (`[data-fcomp]`; 600 px wide, 20 px above the foot, one opaque card with a light shadow). `AttachmentPane` gives it through `FullScreenComposerContext` only in full screen and only for those three kinds. The image and film views draw it in place of their comment row, and the pane draws it for HTML. A file beside the thread, Markdown, code, an archived thread with nothing after the archive and the onboarding tour's own file pane (`tour/Tour.tsx`, which passes no `composer`) have none.
- Round 3 (approval `.product/approved/inbox-comment-box/approval.md`, 2026-11-07): the card's top is a tinted tray with the two tabs carved out of it (shape c: the picked tab in the card's own colour and 32 px, joined to the body by two curved feet; the other 28 px and quieter, with a dot and ", has words" for a screen reader while it holds words), icons kept, and ⌄ ("Hide tools") at the tray's end. Under the words, one row. The Comment tab's is the file's own tools at the left (Mark up as its icon, still named), the bin (`.ib-fcomp-cend`, dimmed with no words; clears the field; the card hands it to the file's row through `FullScreenCommentBin`, which the row draws just before its button, so Tab meets the bin and then Comment, as the screen shows them) and a solid "Comment" (`.ib-csave`, no icon, 8 px corners) at the right end. The Reply tab's is the Send pill first (the word "Send"), the paperclip and the mic, what rides, Open in thread and the reply's bin. No frosted plate, no hover fills, no transitions on the buttons. The image button is gone from an HTML page's comment box (`HtmlViewer`'s `allowCommentImages={false}`; images were never taken there). On a phone's HTML card, the pin's Parent and Child buttons are arrows (`arrowUp`, `arrowDown`), so ⌄ is the card's one chevron.
- Two tabs on the card's top edge, a radio group (`role="radiogroup"`, arrows move the pick): "Comment on this image" (or "this video") holds the file's own comment row as it was (its field, Mark up, Comment; still `[data-image-composer]`, so the Escape ladder treats it as before and its words are kept as the file's draft when it closes), and "Reply to <agent>" holds the reply. On an HTML page the Comment tab ("Comment on this page") is the page's own row (`HtmlPageCommentRow` in `HtmlPageComment.tsx`; the owner, 2026-11-08: "either comment on the artifact … or send the reply. With commenting being the default"): a comment on the whole page, saved on the attachment as a `GLOBAL_COMMENT` (as `HtmlViewer`'s "Add global comment" makes in annotate) and sent with the reply as "General feedback about the document"; comments on an element or words are still the page's pin and selection. Its words are a row draft like an image's, parked in the pane while the row is off screen with the file open (out of full screen, a linked page over it, which is Reply only). A read-only file is Reply only. The tab is kept per thread for the window's life. Until the person picks, the card opens on Reply when a reply draft waits, else on Comment. The comment row stays drawn, hidden, on the Reply tab and while the card is gathered or folded (`[data-fcomp-comment-slot]`), so words typed in it are kept, and a step or a close keeps them as the file's draft (`useRowDraft`), back in the row when the file opens again.
- One draft. The Reply tab has no words of its own: it shows the thread's reply words (the open box's, else the kept draft's), and every keystroke goes through the reply box's own change (`changeReply` in `Thread.tsx`). The box opens behind full screen without taking focus, and the draft saves as it always does (`replyDraft.ts`, `base_body` and all). Typing in either place is the same draft.
- Send is the thread's Send (`send({ stayFull: true })`), with the same rules and refusals. Its error shows in the card (the thread's own `role="alert"` line announces it). A Send that goes clears the words and the draft (`clear_draft`), keeps full screen, keeps focus in the card, and shows "Sent to <agent>" for 4 s (the thread's live region says it). A replaced pick's guard, and the choice of where an ended session's reply goes, need the thread: the Send then takes the person there. Files: the Reply tab's toolbar starts with the reply box's paperclip (`AttachFilesButton`, the card's 30 px button, 36 px on a phone; none on the Comment tab, as a comment takes no files), a paste of files into the field attaches, and files dropped on the card attach (`useFileDrop`, the box's dashed "Drop to attach"). They are the reply box's own files (`replyFiles`; the box opens behind full screen), shown in the card with the box's tiles, and the Send carries them. Send is dimmed when nothing would go (no words, picks, comments, files or action items); Mod+Enter in the field still sends, and an empty Send says "Write a reply or pick an answer first."
- What was on screen goes with the Send, for the agent only (approval `.product/approved/inbox-reply-viewing-context/approval.md`; the owner, 2026-11-04: "It doesn't mean I'm choosing that one"). A Send from the card posts `viewing` (`replyViewingOf`, `packages/inbox/replyViewing.ts`) on the reply that carries its words; `store.planViewing` reads the file's name and message from the thread and stores them as `viewing` on the reply, never in `body`, so the thread and search show only what the person wrote. `inboxViewingLine` (`packages/shared/inbox/uploads.ts`) renders it at read time as one line: `` (They sent this while viewing `c-labels.png`, from the 8:47 PM message msg_….) ``, with "as first sent" for the sent version of a changed file or the sent copy of a file gone, and "`b.html`, linked from `a.html`" for a page an attached HTML page linked to (only an HTML file: on any other file `linked=` in an address names nothing on screen). It reaches the agent in the wake (`inboxBodyForAgent`, so every connection, tagged sessions and Codex's queue), in read_thread's text (under the words, or in an older reply's one line), and as `viewing` in its JSON (`format: "full"` too, and so `plannotator inbox call`). A file's name is anyone's text: one line, no controls, no format characters (`\p{Cf}`, bidi among them), no backtick, 120 characters. The time sits outside the quotes, so the store keeps only the shape `whenTime` writes (`INBOX_VIEWING_TIME`), and a stored field of another shape reads as no line rather than failing the read. A file not of this thread drops the field without refusing the Send; so does a comment on that file riding the same reply, since "Feedback on the attached files" names it already. The card draws on an image, a film and an HTML page, and on a phone too (same `onSend`); a PDF or a code file has no card, so a reply to one comes from the thread's box. The thread's box never sends it, and the Comment tab is unchanged. Nothing in the tool text or the skill mentions it: the line explains itself. Tests: `packages/server/inbox-reply-viewing.test.ts`, the last test of `tests/e2e/inbox-fullscreen-composer.spec.ts`.
- The bottom row (Reply tab) is the reply's toolbar: the paperclip, then what rides the Send (picks, action items, comments) and the open count in the thread foot's words (`openItemsLabel`: "1 open question", "2 open action items", "2 questions, 1 action item"; owner, 2026-10-30) or "Sent to <agent>" just after a Send, with **Open in thread** at its right. Open in thread takes the device's own route to the thread: beside it where there is room (as Write the reply), else the file closes (a phone's or a stacked window's Back). A comment not saved yet in the file waits as its draft (nothing asks). The box keeps its words and focus lands at their end. When another window changed the draft, the row says so; Keep mine and Discard stay in the thread.
- Where there is no room for a view beside the thread (a phone, a stacked window), leaving full screen would change nothing on screen, so Write the reply and R close the file and put focus at the end of the thread's box. An unsaved comment waits as the file's draft, as with Open in thread.
- On a phone that row is one row from 360 px up (`tests/e2e/inbox-mobile-floating-box.spec.ts`); below 360 px the chips take their own row over the paperclip, the mic and the link, so every press stays 44 px. A chip never wraps or shrinks, and the mic takes only its own room. What yields, in order (`cardFootFitCandidates` in `footFit.ts`, drawn from the foot's `data-fit`): the open count's words ("3 open", then "3"), then the comments' word (the pill becomes its icon and number), then "Open in thread" becomes "Thread". Only when text is too large for that, or under 360 px, do the chips take their own row. The words stay as visually hidden text, so names stay whole. Dictating, the status has the row: the chips, "Sent to" and the link step aside. The tabs, minimize, Send, the comment row's buttons, the paperclip, the chips, the mic, the link, and the voice links (Cancel, Retry, Discard, Voice settings) are 44 px to press through an overlay (`::after`), so nothing moves; a file tile's own controls are not. A long agent's name is cut on one line in the header, in "Sent to" and in the empty field's placeholder. The card and its pill step aside while the comments sheet is open: its foot holds Write the reply, which on a phone closes the file and opens the thread's box. Opening the sheet ends a dictation (the words land; Esc stays with the sheet), and a comment edit put back when the file opens again opens the sheet to show it. A folded pill shares the toolbar's row when both fit and sits above the toolbar when they would touch (measured, since "Draft" and a position like 75/78 change both widths).
- Minimize: the chevron at the tray's end (the toolbar's 30 px `.ib-fvb`, tooltip and name "Hide tools") is the toolbar's »: the card, the toolbar and everything else that floats gather into one corner control (`onGather` from the pane; the full rule is under "Full screen, one mode" in `inbox-files-and-guides.md`). The corner control reads "Draft" while words or files wait in the reply; one press brings everything back with focus in the card's field. No "Draft saved" line: the words carry the state. One dot: while a comment typed in the card is not saved, a dot in the theme's red (`--destructive`, ringed in `--popover`) sits on the control's arrows (`[data-full-view-comment-dot]`, `FullViewBar`'s `commentDraft` from `AttachmentPane`), and its name starts "Show tools, comment not sent" (`packages/inbox/fullViewCommentDot.test.tsx`). (Approval `inbox-fullscreen-gather`, 2026-11-03; it replaces the card's separate desktop pill.)
- Moving the card (the owner, 2026-11-09: "grab this thing and move it around. It'll only stay moved for that artifact"; `packages/inbox/composerDrag.ts`). On a desktop the card is dragged by its tray, the strip the tabs sit in (`[data-fcomp-drag]`): the empty part, never a tab or the chevron, and a press that moves under 4 px is no drag. Pointer events with capture (mouse, pen, touch; `touch-action: none` on the tray only), the cursor is the affordance (grab, grabbing), and a press on the tray leaves focus and the words where they were. The move is a transform from the card's home (`--ib-fcomp-dx`, `--ib-fcomp-dy`), with no transition while dragged. The whole card stays 8 px inside the view it floats over: what the person asked for is kept and drawn clamped, so a smaller window or a card that grows pushes it back in and it returns when there is room. It holds for that file only (`fileKey` on the composer's value, the pane's draft key: the attachment and which version of it shows) through a tab switch, typing, a Send and the tools gathered and brought back (the card flies to the corner control from where it is, and back there); another file, or the same one closed and opened again, shows it at its home. Nothing is saved. A double click on the tray puts it home with a 200 ms ease (none under reduced motion). The comments list above the card goes with it. A phone's card is docked: no move. No keys. Test: the DOM test `packages/inbox/composerDrag.test.tsx`.
- Resizing the card (the owner, 2026-11-09: "after I move it, if some like resized corner panels show up"; same file). Once the card is off its home, its four corners resize it (`[data-fcomp-resize="nw|ne|sw|se"]`, 18 px to press, 28 for a finger; a card at home has none and is as it always was). The corner under the pointer moves and the opposite one stays. As narrow as 320 px (`MIN_WIDTH`; more when the reply's row of buttons and chips needs it, measured when the drag starts) and as low as one line of text with the tray and the buttons; as large as the view less its 8 px margin, from every corner, with the comments panel open too. Under 480 px (`data-fcomp-narrow`) the tabs read "Comment" and "Reply" as a phone's, their whole names kept for a screen reader and as tooltips; a smaller window limits it as it limits the place. The size is `--ib-fcomp-rw` and `--ib-fcomp-rh` with `data-fcomp-sized`: the card becomes a column, the field takes what the height leaves and scrolls inside (the size the person gave wins over the field growing with its words), and the row of buttons stays at the foot. Kept and dropped with the place: a tab switch and the tools gathered keep it; another file, or the double click on the tray, gives the card's own size and home back. Pointer only (mouse, pen, touch), as the app's other resize handles (`ResizeHandle`) take no keys. No mark at rest (the owner's pick, 2026-11-09): the cursor, and a faint arc on the corner under the pointer.
- The pill (`[data-fcomp-pill]`, 30 px, "Reply draft" while words wait, else the tab's label) is left only for a card folded by default (an HTML page on a phone opens clear) and for a host that gives no `onGather`. One click opens the card and focuses its field.
- Gathered (`data-tools="tucked"` on the pane), the card and the pill are `inert`.
- Phone: the card spans the width (8 px in), the toolbar sits above it (the card is the pane's foot, `data-file-foot`, so `--ib-fvfoot` lifts the toolbar), the tabs read Comment and Reply, Send is the word "Send" at the row's right end (named "Send to <agent>"; last in the markup, so Tab follows the screen), and there is no bin (the words go by clearing them). ⌄ gathers everything into the 44 px corner control, 12 px in from the bottom right. Folded (an HTML page opens so), the pill is a 52 px button left of the toolbar, at its height: the icon, plus "Draft" when words wait. On a phone a film's comment row is in the Comment tab, so its "Comment at" button does not show in full screen. What it did is kept: a tap in the comment field pauses the film and pins that moment, and clearing the words lets go of it (that was Cancel), so another moment can be picked.
- An HTML page on a phone (approval "inbox-html-attachments-phone", part A; `packages/inbox/components/HtmlPageComment.tsx`, `packages/inbox/windowPinch.ts`, proof `tests/e2e/inbox-html-phone.spec.ts`): it opens clear with the reply in the pill (a fold that is that view's default, not the thread's: an image on the thread keeps its card) and the pen off. A pin or a selection (selected by finger: `HtmlViewer` `touchSelection`) opens the card on its Comment tab, which holds the pin's or selection's card while the draft lives (the page's own row stays drawn under it, hidden, its words kept; opened from the pill with no pin, the tab is the page's row), through `onHostDraft`, `stepPin` and `revealPin({ clearBottom })`; the toolbar hides under it and saving puts tab and fold back. The tools tuck while `visualViewport.scale` is over 1.05, read in the window so a page cannot forge it; a pinch does not change the person's own choice, and a comment stays out while it is open. No web call sets a window's zoom: `releaseWindowZoom` clamps the viewport meta for two frames when a page opens, which Chromium honors; iOS Safari is expected to keep the zoom on the next file.
- The size of an HTML page on a phone (same approval, part B "Fit the page", addendum 2026-11-07; `packages/inbox/pageSize.ts`, proofs `packages/inbox/pageSize.test.ts`, the DOM tests `packages/inbox/htmlPageSize.test.tsx` and `packages/ui/components/html-viewer/HtmlViewer.frameScale.test.tsx`, and `tests/e2e/inbox-html-fit.spec.ts`). One control, like Safari's "Request Desktop Website": a small icon button in the page's top right corner (`[data-page-size-corner]` > `[data-page-size-toggle]`, 34 px drawn, 44 px to press; the owner's pick, 2026-11-07, "Just the icon no words"). The icon shows the size the page is at (a phone, a laptop); the approved words are only its name and tooltip, saying what a press does ("Desktop size" at Phone size, "Phone size" at Desktop size). It floats like the other tools: `GATHERED` lists it, so » and a pinch tuck it with them. It gets out of the way (addendum 2026-11-07 17:53Z, q-a68aff4a; `packages/inbox/sizeIconFade.ts`): shown when the page opens, faded after 3 s (`SIZE_ICON_SHOW_MS`; `data-faded` on the corner, opacity 0 and `pointer-events: none`, so a tap there reaches the page), back for another 3 s on a scroll up in the page (the bridge's `page-scroll-up`: 16 screen px up in one go, at most every 200 ms) or a tap near the top (the bridge's `page-tap`, at most 44 px below the icon, `HtmlViewer` `onPageScrollUp` / `onPageTap`; the tap's 10 px movement limit is in screen pixels, so it works at the 0.3 fit scale too), and when the tools come back after » or a pinch; a press starts the 3 s over, keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`, also a CSS rule, whatever the timer) holds it shown while a finger's focus and its blur do not bring a faded icon back, it stays in the accessibility tree with its name, a press is announced as just "Desktop size" or "Phone size", and under reduced motion it shows and hides with no fade. "Desktop size" lays the page out at 1280 px and scales it to the phone's width with a CSS transform (`.ib-fitdesk`); a pinch then zooms the window, and the page redraws sharp. "Phone size" goes back. A page the bridge reports clearly wider than its frame (`content-width`, at least 1.5 times, `PAGE_WIDE_RATIO`: desktop layouts are 768 px or wider, while one long hash or URL poking out, about 500 px, does not count) opens on Desktop size by itself; the bridge-failure banner is scaled back up so it stays readable; a press is remembered per file (its path) in localStorage (`plannotator-inbox-page-size`, the newest 200), wins over the page's width, and survives a reload. The pane passes the scale to `HtmlViewer` (`frameScale`), so the composer's anchor and `revealPin`'s `clearBottom` convert; a pin itself is placed by the bridge in the page's own pixels and lands under the finger. Phone only: a desktop draws no icon and no scale. No keys.
- Keys: nothing new. Escape in the reply field is full screen's `field` rung, as in a comment row: focus leaves the field and the words stay ("Left the reply box. Your words are kept."). R from a file in full screen goes back beside the thread first, also when the composer has opened the box; the box keeps its words and caret (before the composer, R with the box open put focus in the box hidden under full screen).
- Tests: the DOM test `packages/inbox/fullScreenComposer.test.tsx` (tabs, one draft both ways, ⌄ gathering the card and the toolbar into the corner control and one press back with focus in the field, Hide, HTML Reply only, refusals, a Send that goes, the paperclip, a paste and a drop, an unsaved comment kept on Reply and gathered, a comment saved from the card) and `tests/e2e/inbox-fullscreen-composer.spec.ts` (an image: Reply, ⌄ and » gathering into the bottom-right corner control with "Draft", one press back, a file, Send; HTML; a 390 px phone's 44 px corner control; the shots); the phone film's tap-to-pin and clear-to-unpin in `tests/e2e/inbox-video.spec.ts`.

**A page older than its Inbox** (the owner, 2026-11-02: an iPhone restored a cached 0.28.44 page against a 0.28.45 Inbox, and a bug 0.28.45 fixed came back; `packages/inbox/stalePage.ts`, `components/StalePageNotice.tsx`). The `private, no-cache` page header does not stop a phone restoring a tab, and a restarted Inbox leaves open pages as they were.
- Detection: the page's release is `__APP_VERSION__` (apps/inbox/vite.config.ts, the root `package.json`). The Inbox's is the first list read's `version`, then `GET /api/inbox/health`'s `version` (read within 3 s, `healthWithin`) on each event stream `hello`, on `pageshow` from the back/forward cache and on `visibilitychange` to visible. No server field was added (the Inbox is Bun-only).
- Only a stable `X.Y.Z` strictly newer than the page's shows it (`newerInbox`). `dev`, `0.0.0-dev`, a page with no build (tests) and an older Inbox (the version matrix serves today's page from a 0.28.10 build) never do. Nothing is read or shown while the person's own Restart runs (it reloads the page by itself).
- The notice is the list's "N new · Show" line: "Inbox updated to X", Reload, and a ×. It sits at the list's top, and at the open thread's top when no list is beside the thread (a phone, a stacked window, full screen, a card's thread, beside a file). The × hides it for that release: only a newer release shows it again, or the next page load (no nagging on each return to the tab). It never reloads by itself, and it has no keyboard shortcut. While Reload saves, it stays focusable (`aria-disabled`) and the × still works; the × moves focus to the thread's subject or the list's first row.
- Reload saves first (`reloadOntoInbox`, then `flushBeforeReload`, at most 8 s): each part that holds words registers a flush (`registerReloadFlush`). The thread saves the reply box as its draft, its words and files (`useReplyDraft().settle` with `draftFiles`), with any file change of the box still waiting for a save (a closed box too; one that cannot land yet stops the reload) and every answer on its cards (`pickQueue.settled`). A compose dialog keeps its words as a draft, decision mode waits for its picks, and the action notes settle. Anything not saved stops the reload, and a toast offers "Reload anyway": a conflict, a file the draft does not keep, an upload, a refusal, no answer, words in another machine's reply box, a "Can't do it" reason not yet given, or reply words left unsaved in any thread (`unsentReply`; a phone's Safari shows no "Leave page?").
- The session switch: when the Inbox restarted under the page, its saves would be refused (409). So, only when the health's `inbox_id` is the page's own Inbox id (`selfMachineId`), the saves carry the running Inbox's `serverSession` (`adoptPageSession`). When they do not all land, the page sets its first session back, so the old page's writes are refused again.
- A page from an older release whose entry script the Inbox no longer has, and the browser no longer holds (a restored tab), has no notice to show: nothing on it runs. A small inline script in the page (`INBOX_ENTRY_GONE_SCRIPT`, `build/pack-inbox.ts`) reloads it once on that script's 404, with no words of its own; a network failure reloads nothing. A lazy part that 404s on an open page shows ui's `LazyLoadNotice` in place, beside the notice.
- Tests: `packages/inbox/stalePage.test.ts` (same release, newer, older, dev, a reconnect after the Inbox's release changed, a dismissed release held until a newer one, paused during Restart, the flush, the session switch: a restart, saves that failed, another Inbox, no answer), `tests/e2e/inbox-stale-page-webkit.spec.ts` (a 390 px phone and a desktop in WebKit; the reply draft saved before the reload's request; × holding on the tab's return; the shots).

**Window controls** (`inbox.css`, "Window controls"; proof `tests/e2e/inbox-window-controls.spec.ts`). A desktop host that draws the window's buttons over the page reads `window_controls` from `GET /api/inbox/health` (1 or higher: a later number only adds), then sets `--pn-window-controls-width` and `--pn-window-controls-height` on `:root`, in a sheet or inline. Unset, every value is 0 and nothing moves.
- **The fixed row.** The buttons stay in one spot whatever the sidebar does, on the line `--pn-window-controls-center-y` names on `:root` (read-only, a length; from health 2): the gutter plus 23. With the variables set, `.ib-lhead`, `.ib-bhead`, `.ib-wn-bar` and `.ib-dh` are 46 px tall, the thread toolbar's height, so the toggles, the titles and the toolbar sit on it; the tour's bar is tall enough for its content to sit there too, and what starts below the bar (the sidebar, a full-screen file) has nothing more to clear; its viewer covers the bar and keeps its room. A Shape board's title row stays lower, under the band. The value is for a window wider than a phone's layout, which has no gutter.
- The sidebar's first row clears the height (docked, peek, a phone's sheet). The header against the window's left edge clears the width: the first column of `.ib-main` while the sidebar is not docked open (`--ib-controls-left`, which the headers' left padding reads), or the next column when a stacked window or full screen hides the first. The peek's hover strip starts under the buttons.
- **A new full-window layer adds the class `ib-wlayer` to its root.** That clears the height with a transparent top border, so its sticky and absolute children start below the buttons too; its band drags, its body does not, and an `.ib-vhead` directly inside reaches up behind the buttons.
- Dragging applies only under `@container style(--pn-window-controls-height)`: with the variable unset, nothing has `-webkit-app-region` and the band does not exist. The band over the whole window (`.pn-inbox::before`) and blank header space drag; every control, everything inside a header but its spacers, and every float do not. Blink reads these boxes in layout order, a later one over an earlier one, modal dialogs last, so a float placed before `.ib-main` in the page does not win over a header below it.
